# Implementation Plan: session-multi-pr-tracking

**Feature**: A session (and a backlog item) tracks several PRs, each with its own state; PR polling, PR actions and the backlog done rule act on the right PR; the Artifacts tab becomes reliable, richer, searchable across sessions and browsable.
**Date**: 2026-11-03
**Status**: Ready for implementation (revised six times after architecture and adversarial review, then a Phase 4 PATCH pass: consistency review, pre-mortem, and two owner requests, issue-close on re-import (Story 5.5) and mobile e2e (T6.3.3); see "Review repair log: Phase 4 patch pass"; iteration 6 was a SCOPED pass: wave-order and dependency-graph repair (table appended), PR maintenance only from service bootstrap, unconditional item-direct link writes, tombstone scoping, awaited reconciler refresh, chunked queries; see "Review repair log: Iteration 6 (scoped)"; iteration 3 was a SIMPLIFICATION pass that deleted the item-level mirror links and made `PullRequestState` single-writer; iteration 4 was TARGETED text/AC/ordering fixes with no new service, flag or table; iteration 5 was the FINAL targeted pass: wave-order fix, boot-loop classification, single-writer counter resets, transition table, error classifier, known-risk list; see "Review repair log: Iteration 5 (final)" and "Known Risks (accepted)")
**ADRs**: [ADR-001](../decisions/ADR-001-session-pr-entity-and-identity.md), [ADR-002](../decisions/ADR-002-active-pr-derivation-and-legacy-mirror.md), [ADR-003](../decisions/ADR-003-backlog-multi-pr-done-rule-and-additive-report.md), [ADR-004](../decisions/ADR-004-artifacts-separate-from-sessionpr-and-search-index.md)

**Amendment (owner, 2026-11-06): no squash default.** `MergePR` with no method keeps the pre-branch default, `merge`. The merge dialog preselects `merge`; `squash` and `rebase` apply only when explicitly chosen. The deprecated `method` field (2) and `merge_method` (4) stay wire-compatible. This supersedes every "default squash" statement below (T4.1.1, T6.1.3, U-134, N7).

Confidence labels: VERIFIED = I opened the code or ran the command in this planning session; INFERRED = reasoned from reads, not run. Nothing here was built or executed.

---

## Open Questions from requirements.md: resolutions

| Question | Resolution | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Rollout flag name | `session_multi_pr`, default off, global plus reader scopes `reader:poller`, `reader:backlog`, `reader:actions`, `reader:ui`. Live-settable via the feature-flag RPC. No env var. The scopes gate readers only; the legacy mirror is NOT gated (always the active-PR projection once any link exists) and scopes depend on each other in the order poller, backlog, actions, ui, applied when the flag is READ (an out-of-order enable is stored but resolves off until its prerequisites are effective; Triad iteration 2, verified against the flag service) | Template `agent_wake_triggers` (`feature_flag_service.go:428`); owner rule on live flags. ADR-002 (revised) |
| Role chips at launch | Yes, in the detail PR list only, shown only when role is a known non-primary role (`followup`, `stacked`); never on the card or row. Role is derived from the existing links and `base_branch` (T2.1.3), never guessed from text. Phase 4 C11: `rework` and `external` were removed from `PRRole` because no task could assign them | `research/ux.md` §5 tier 2; keeps list noise at one chip |
| Next free `Session` proto field | `100` for `repeated SessionPullRequest pull_requests`. Highest used is 99 (VERIFIED above). Re-confirm at `make proto-gen` and `make proto-breaking` | types.proto:379 |
| Many-to-many or one owning session per PR row | Many-to-many through two entities: `PullRequestState` (one row per `PRRef`, holds all state and is polled once) and `SessionPR` (link rows: session and/or backlog item to a `PullRequestState`). The same PR attached to a rework session and its original is two link rows pointing at one state row, so state cannot diverge and there is no fan-out. Link rows use loose `session_uuid` / `backlog_item_id` strings like `ItemSession` | ADR-001 (revised) |
| Do Jules and up-next write through the relation | Jules: yes. `applyCompletedState` attaches every PR in `Outputs[]` as an item-direct link (`attach_source=jules`, `backlog_item_id` only; consults the item tombstone first). Up-next / `github_user_nudge.go`: no. It lists the user's GitHub PRs (`UserPR`) and is not session-keyed; it only reads the relation to show a "session: name" chip. | `jules_session_poller.go:505-516` VERIFIED; `github_user_nudge.go` listing INFERRED from file name and `research/ux.md` §1 |
| Scanner `PRURLs` vs `SessionPR` | Separate (binding decision). `OnScanComplete` never seeds the legacy number or a `SessionPR`, regardless of the flag (Phase 4 B2). Exact PR lookup (E9) unions both stores and labels each hit with its source | ADR-004 |

## Step 0.5: Alternatives considered (CREATIVE pass)

| Approach | Strength | Weakness |
|---|---|---|
| A. Two ent entities: `PullRequestState` (facts, one row per `PRRef`) plus `SessionPR` (link rows from a session and/or backlog item), derived active PR with pin, legacy columns as a projection (CHOSEN) | Queryable across sessions (poller, sweeper, gate, search); per-PR state without rewriting the session blob; a PR has exactly one state row, so no divergent copies and no poll fan-out; matches `ItemSession` precedent for the link | Largest change: two schemas, backfill, dual-write, two ratchets |
| A'. Single `SessionPR` entity with a `""` sentinel in the six-column unique index (the first draft of this plan) | One table | The same PR becomes 2-3 rows with divergent `state`/`terminal`; item verdict double-counts; item-only rows are never polled (architecture B3, adversarial B3). Rejected |
| B. JSON array of PR records on the session row | No new table; trivial backfill | Poller, retention sweeper and backlog gate would load-all-and-filter; every PR state tick rewrites the session blob; two writers race on one blob |
| C. Make the backlog item the PR aggregate (item-level PR table) and let sessions read through the item | One home for the completion rule | Sessions with no backlog item (most manual sessions) get nothing; breaks when the item link is broken mid-review (memory `instinct_backlog_session_link_broken_mid_review`) |

Chosen: A. B and C are recorded in the Pattern Decisions table.

## Domain Glossary

| Term | Definition | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `PRRef` | `{host, owner, repo string; number int}` with UNEXPORTED fields and accessors (`Host()`, `Owner()`, `Repo()`, `Number()`): the identity of a PR | Value object in `session/pr_set.go`. `NewPRRef` is the only constructor, so the compiler rejects `PRRef{...}` literals outside that file (no grep guard test) and JSON/proto decode cannot fill it by accident. `NewPRRef` canonicalizes host, owner AND repo to lowercase (empty host becomes `github.com`), the same rule as `github.RepoRef.repoKey()` (`github/repo_ref.go:57-59`, "all three are case-insensitive on GitHub"); `NewRepoRefWithHost` (`repo_ref.go:27-37`) keeps owner/repo verbatim, so identity never relies on it. Display case lives only in `PullRequestState.url`. An ent validator on the host/owner/repo columns of the state and tombstone tables rejects upper-case values (backstop for a raw column write). A repository rename or transfer yields a separate row (out of scope). Replaces the `owner, repo string, prNumber int` pile in the poller and `pr_tracking.go`. Limits of the unexported-fields trick: `PRRef{}` (no fields) still compiles outside the file, and `encoding/json` silently drops unexported fields. So `PRRef` has `IsZero()` (every repository entry point returns `ErrZeroPRRef` on it; T1.3.6 table test) and `String()` plus `MarshalText` so `debug_snapshot.go` dumps and logged `PRView`s show `host/owner/repo#n` |
| `PullRequestState` | Persisted facts about one PR, unique per `PRRef`: state, draft, checks, approvals, terminal, timestamps, poll bookkeeping (`last_polled_at`, `poll_fail_count`, `poll_error`, `rate_limit_reset_at`) | Ent entity `pull_request_state`; polled exactly once per tick; SINGLE-WRITER (invariant I1, see "State writer"). A backfill-seeded "unverified terminal" row is simply `state=unknown` with `terminal=true`; there is no separate column. Named "State" rather than "PullRequest" to avoid clashing with `github.PRInfo` |
| `SessionPR` | Persisted link row: attaches one `PullRequestState` to exactly ONE owner, either a session (`session_uuid`) or a backlog item (`backlog_item_id`), with role, pin, attach source and attached time | Ent entity `session_pr`; holds no PR state and no per-link flags except `pinned`. Unique on (`pull_request_state_id`, `session_uuid`, `backlog_item_id`) with exactly one owner string non-empty (ent validator), so one row never serves two owners. There is no `abandoned` flag and no `origin_session_uuid`: nothing is copied from a session link to an item link. A link stops counting only by being deleted (detach) or by its owner leaving the item's current round (Glossary: Current round) |
| `PRView` | Immutable per-PR copy (link joined to state) carried in `InstanceSnapshot.GitHub.PRs` | Always copied on write |
| `PRState` | Sum type: `open`, `closed`, `merged`, `unknown` | String-backed type with `Terminal()` method (`unknown` is NOT terminal and NOT open: it blocks done and cleanup, and is surfaced); unrecognized strings fail parse |
| State writer (invariant I1) | `UpdatePullRequestState(ref, observation)` in `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go` is the ONLY code that changes a `PullRequestState` column after the row exists. Its only production callers are the poll fetch core in `session/pr_status_poller.go` (the `reader:poller` loop and the flag-independent refresher are the same core). One documented exception in the same single-writer set (same file): `ResetVerifyCounters()`, called only from the service-startup maintenance step (`Storage.RunPRStartupMaintenance`, invoked inside `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions` ONLY when `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance` is true; that flag is set at the service bootstrap call sites, the `core-deps` phase at `main.go:384` and `BuildDependencies`, and is false for the plain `BuildCoreDeps()` that `buildMCPDeps` (`main.go:1871`, the stdio MCP fallback) uses; it runs right after `Storage` exists and before any poller, sweeper or ticker is built; NOT from `NewEntRepository`/`startupMigrations` or `buildMCPDeps`, which also run in hook, CLI and MCP-fallback processes), restricted by its WHERE clause to `state='unknown' AND terminal=true`. Hook, CLI and MCP-fallback processes never write state columns and never run the step, so inside the service no writer exists at that point; the only possible concurrent writer is an old service process alive during a restart overlap, and losing that race only re-zeroes a counter on a non-terminal row (safe direction) (T3.2.5). Attach, backfill and every full save use `EnsureState(ref, initial)` (insert-if-absent, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) | Snapshot `PRView.State` is a read-through copy refreshed from the row by `ApplyPRUpdate`; persisting a snapshot writes link rows and the session's legacy mirror columns, never state columns. Enforced by a caller allow-list test and the stale-snapshot test (T2.3.4) |
| Tombstone | `session_pr_tombstone` row keyed by ONE owner (`session_uuid` or `backlog_item_id`) and a canonical `PRRef`, written in the same transaction as the detach that deletes that owner's link | Backfill, discovery, terminal scan and Jules never recreate a tombstoned owner link. For an ITEM owner it is also a read-time filter on the derived item PR set; a SESSION tombstone is not (architecture C4): it records that this session's own contribution was removed (its link is already deleted), so a PR still held by an item-direct link or by the current round's live session link stays in the set. "Tombstones excluded" in the item candidate rule (T1.5.1), the `itemPRs` legacy fallback (T5.1.2) and the `pr_number` recompute (T5.2.3) is the wider `PRCoveredByTombstone(item, sessions)` test (a tombstone on the item OR on ANY of the item's sessions, every round and role), so detaching a session-only PR can never make it "unrepresented" again; the set derivation itself subtracts item tombstones only. Collected with its owner (T1.3.5). Attaching by URL (user) clears it |
| `CleanupGate` | One predicate `session.PRCleanupGate.Blocked(sessions []ItemSessionSummary)` returning allowed/blocked(reason naming each holding owner), called at the single worktree-removal choke point `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept`. The retention sweeper uses the pure `PRsAllTerminal` over a session's own links | Reads link rows joined to state; fails closed on `unknown` (non-terminal), on a lookup error, and on "legacy PR number but no link" |
| `PRRole` | Sum type: `primary`, `followup`, `stacked`, `unknown` (Phase 4 C11: `rework` and `external` were removed; no task could assign them, a rework session's PR is `primary` or `followup`) | Display only; derived from the session's existing links and `base_branch` by T2.1.3, never inferred from text; `unknown` only for an unparseable stored value |
| `AttachSource` | Sum type: `created_from_url`, `push_scan`, `branch_discovery`, `pr_creation_service`, `report_pr_created`, `jules`, `backfill`, `manual` | Recorded for provenance; `manual` and `report_pr_created` are verified sources |
| `ActivePR` | Pure function `ActivePR(prs, currentBranch) *PRView` | Pin, else open PR on current branch, else newest `open` PR, else newest `unknown` that is not terminal, else newest effective-terminal (`PRView.Terminal()`, which includes an unverified terminal row only once its verify is exhausted), else nil. `unknown` ranks below every `open` PR and above terminal ones; ranking uses effective terminal, never the `state` string alone, so a seeded `unknown`+terminal row cannot outrank a newer verified merged PR. This refines the owner's "newest non-terminal" only to order `unknown` last among the non-terminal |
| Pin | `SessionPR.pinned` boolean, at most one true per session; set only by a user RPC | Automation never pins. UI label for detach: "Not mine" on a session-owned row, "Stop tracking" on an item-owned row (both are the same detach operation on different owners; Phase 4 N6) |
| Legacy mirror | The `github_pr_*` columns and `Instance.GitHubPR*` fields, always written as a projection of `ActivePR` once the session has any link (independent of every reader scope; the flag gates readers, not the projection) | Keeps old clients and 27 Go files working; the legacy value is always a valid attached PR. Terminology (Phase 4 N14): "legacy projection" means this session projection; "item scalar mirror" means `backlog_item.pr_number/pr_url` (T5.2.3); "mirror links" survive only in the repair-log history |
| `PRTargetSelector` | Optional `PullRequestRef` on a PR action request; absent means `ActivePR` | Unset proto value never means PR number 0 |
| `AttachPR` | The single Instance actor setter that upserts a `SessionPR` view and recomputes the mirror | The only legal writer of the legacy fields once the flag is on |
| Terminal (PR) | `PRState` is `closed` or `merged`; the row stops being polled | Stored per row (`PRView.Terminal()` = state is closed/merged, OR the stored `terminal` bool with `state != unknown`, OR `state = unknown` with `terminal` AND `VerifyExhausted()`). The one exception is a backfill-seeded "unverified terminal" row (`state=unknown`, `terminal=true`): it is polled until resolved and counts as terminal for the gate, sweeper and `ActivePR` ranking (never as merged) ONLY when `VerifyExhausted()` (>= 5 COUNTED failures, classified per T3.2.4, with `last_polled_at - first_fail_at >= 30 minutes`, i.e. first to LAST counted failure, never to now, so time passing alone cannot make a row terminal); before that it is `unknown` non-terminal. The done VERDICT never treats an exhausted row as terminal: it reads as `unknown` (held), so an unfetchable, possibly merged PR is not read as abandoned. `poll_fail_count` and `first_fail_at` of these rows are reset once per SERVICE boot by `ResetVerifyCounters` (service-startup maintenance only; no nightly reset, never from hook or CLI processes), so an outage during verification never strands a row as terminal. A merged or closed row is STICKY (see `UpdatePullRequestState` monotonic rule, T1.3.2). Session-level terminal is derived |
| Session terminal | Derived: at least one link, every linked PR terminal (`unknown` is not terminal), AND discovery is idle | Written to `github_pr_status_terminal` through the clearable `PersistPRMirror` (T2.3.3); a session with a legacy PR number and no link is never terminal. SOURCE OF TRUTH (architecture C1): the Instance field `GitHubPRStatusTerminal` is what `buildSnapshot` copies (it never re-derives, so it stays pure). With every scope off the legacy loop (`instance_terminal.go:449`) is its only derivation and `AttachPR`/`DetachPR` change it only by clearing it when a non-terminal PR is attached; with `reader:poller` on, `ApplyPRUpdate`/`MarkPRTerminal`/`AttachPR`/`DetachPR` derive it from the links. A flag-off two-link characterization test pins that a legacy "merged" observation is not overwritten by stale `PRView.State=open` (T3.2.5) |
| `ItemPRVerdict` | Sum type over an item's PR set: `NoPRs`, `Open` (some PR open or `unknown` non-terminal), `Done` (>=1 merged, none open), `AllClosedUnmerged` | Drives `pr_pending -> done` (ADR-003). Runs over distinct `PullRequestState` rows by construction. An unverified terminal row (exhausted or not) is read as `unknown` (held, blocks `Done`); only the cleanup gate and sweeper treat an exhausted one as terminal |
| Item PR set | READ-TIME derivation, nothing stored: distinct `PullRequestState` ids from (a) links whose `backlog_item_id` is the item's, UNION (b) the links of the item's current round work session (below), MINUS states carrying a tombstone of the ITEM (a session tombstone does not subtract: that session's link is already gone, and a PR another live contributor still holds stays in the set) | The membership rule. Triage, review, diagnose and every earlier work session contribute nothing. If both (a) and (b) reach one state it is one PR (dedup by state id, no precedence needed) |
| Current round | The item's latest `ItemSession` with `session_role=work`, falling back to an earlier work session ONLY while the later one has NEVER linked: walk work sessions newest first, skip a session with no link AND no session tombstone (never linked yet), stop at the first session that has a link (it is the round) or a tombstone but no link (a link was detached: the round exists and contributes nothing, so an earlier round's PR cannot re-enter, adversarial C6); if none qualifies, only item-direct links count. Ordered by `created_at` (non-null, immutable), tie-break `id` (a random UUID: the order is deterministic, not semantic, and only matters for two rows created in the same instant). Only `created_at`, the role and link existence are read; `ended_at` and the nullable `started_at` are NOT consulted | Why the never-linked skip (adversarial C4): `AttachSessionToItem` (`backlog_service_sync.go:111`) and the rework/respawn paths create the new `work` row BEFORE discovery links its PR, so by `created_at` alone the earlier session's live PR would vanish from the set in that window (verdict `NoPRs`, spurious `PRPendingNoPR`). With the rule, a PR is dropped only once the new session has its own link; a manual re-link of a session that already has other links deliberately supersedes (operator intent). An earlier work session is "superseded" simply by no longer being the latest-with-links. A later `review` session never supersedes its work session (same-role rule). A superseded session's PRs stay linked to it, keep being polled and still hold THAT session's worktree, but do not count for the item's verdict. The item-direct links (agent `report_pr_created`, Jules, manual, `pushAndCreatePR`) are never superseded |
| `PRSetReader` | Interface in `session/` returning an item's `[]PRSnapshot`, with legacy fallback | The seam over `backlog_lifecycle_pr.go` |
| Additive report | `report_pr_created` with a PR number differing from the item's current one, flag on: attaches another PR instead of reassigning | `replaces_pr_number` selects the old correction path |
| Scan state | `artifacts.ScanState`: `not_linked`, `scanning`, `none_found`, `has_data` | Fixes the permanent "Extraction pending" state |
| `ArtifactRecord` | Sum type (sealed interface, one variant per kind) in blob v2: `PRRecord`, `BranchRecord`, `CommitRecord`, `IssueRecord`, `PlanDocRecord`, `CILinkRecord`, `CommandRecord` | Each variant carries only its own fields; a tagged-JSON `kind` is decoded into the variant at one parse point (v1 and v2 share it). Replaces bare string lists; v1 lists still readable |
| Authored vs mentioned | A PR record is `authored` when it appears in the `tool_result` of a `gh pr create` command; otherwise `mentioned` | Mentioned PRs are collapsed in the UI and never enter `SessionPR` |
| `PullRequestChip` / `PullRequestList` | Shared web components for one PR badge (+N) and the detail list | One implementation across card, row, detail, worktree list (jscpd gate) |
| `ArtifactIndex` | In-memory index: a dedicated BM25 `Engine` + `DocumentStore` instance for artifacts (not the history-search store) plus exact maps (`PRRef`, commit SHA prefix) to session uuids | Built from blobs at start, updated by `OnScanComplete` |
| Live work | Pure rule `IsLiveWork(facts, now, window)` (`session/pr_set_rules.go`, T1.2.3): an item's current-round work session is live when it is unended and its `Snapshot()` shows a non-idle status or a `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` within `liveWorkIdleWindow` (default 10 minutes, injected clock, owner to confirm) | Used only as a HOLD: the done verdict stays `pr_pending` (reason `merged, session still active`) and the cleanup gate blocks worktree removal; never as evidence that a PR is open |
| Finished item | An item whose status is exactly `done` AND (it has no PRs OR `ItemPRVerdict == Done`: at least one merged, none open); `archived` and `duplicate` items are never finished | Basis of the issue-close offer on re-import (Story 5.5); pure function `ItemFinished` in `session/backlog_item_prs.go` |

Glossary terms: 29, recounted by command after Phase 4 (27 rows before it; the earlier "28" was stale; Phase 4 added "Live work" and "Finished item").

---

## Pattern Decisions

| Component | Pattern Chosen | Source | Alternative Rejected | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PR set storage | Facts entity (`PullRequestState`, unique per `PRRef`) plus link entity with loose FK (`SessionPR`) | PoEAA Association Table Mapping; repo precedent `ItemSession` | JSON blob on session (B); item-only aggregate (C); one entity with a `""` sentinel (A') | Cross-session queries and per-PR writes; one state row per PR (no divergence, no fan-out); survives session deletion |
| Item PR set | Read-time derived query (item-direct links UNION the current round's work-session links, minus item tombstones) | PoEAA Query Object / derived data | Materialized "mirror" links from sessions to items with an `abandoned` flag and an origin column | Two rows for one fact needed a cascade (`AbandonByOrigin`) and an all-links rule, and waive/detach could not reach them (architecture N2, adversarial N3). Derivation has one source of truth |
| Cleanup and deletion safety | One shared predicate `PRCleanupGate`, called at the ONE choke point `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` that every backlog-driven worktree-removal path reaches | Guard clause at the lowest shared call (Fowler) | Two predicate forms at several entry points | VERIFIED that all six backlog paths funnel into that function (see spot-verification), so one call site cannot be bypassed by a caller that skips a pre-check (adversarial C5) |
| Artifact record kinds | Sealed-interface sum type decoded at one JSON boundary | type-driven-design | One struct plus a `kind` discriminator | A `commit` with a `host` or a `pr` with a `sha` is unrepresentable |
| Artifact search store | Second `Engine`/`DocumentStore` instance for artifacts (shares tokenizer and BM25 code, not the store) | Separate corpus | Extra `Kind` documents in the history store | Shared store leaks artifact hits into `SearchClaudeHistory` and skews idf/avgdl |
| PR identity | Value Object `PRRef` | type-driven-design; primitive-obsession checklist | `owner, repo string, n int` parameters | Poller and `pr_tracking.go` already pass the pile around |
| `PRState`, `PRRole`, `AttachSource`, `ItemPRVerdict` | Sum types with exhaustive switches | type-driven-design | Bare strings | Compiler-visible handling of new states |
| Active PR | Pure function (Transaction Script) | PoEAA | Stored "current_pr_id" column | One derivation, nothing to drift; pin is the only stored choice |
| Instance writes | Single idempotent upsert setter in the actor | repo pattern `instance_actor_setters.go` | Read-modify-write of a list in callers | Poller and `report_pr_created` race otherwise (pitfalls B3) |
| State-row writes | Single-writer repository method plus insert-if-absent for everyone else | Single Writer principle | Snapshot full saves upserting state | A stale snapshot overwrote a fresher poll result in the fail-open direction (adversarial N2); a writer allow-list and a stale-snapshot test make it structural |
| Legacy compatibility | Dual-write projection ("legacy mirror") behind a flag | Expand/contract migration | Drop columns | Rollback needs no restore (requirements Risk Control) |
| Backlog over `backlog_lifecycle_pr.go` | Anti-corruption seam `PRSetReader` + `itemPRs()` | PoEAA Gateway | Restructure the 2450-line file | Disposition: isolate via seam |
| PR action target | Parameter object `PullRequestRef` + one `resolveTargetPR` helper | Fowler Introduce Parameter Object | Per-handler resolution | `dupl` gate and one place for the belongs-to-session check |
| Poller | Extract Method (T3.1.1) then widen the unit of work to the `PullRequestState` | Extract Method | Add branches in place | Churn hotspot with auth/backoff/ETag state |
| Per-PR chip/list UI | One shared component pair | repo `GitHubBadge` precedent | Per-surface markup | jscpd 0.14% absolute threshold |
| Artifact records | Typed records in versioned JSON blob | repo precedent `session_artifacts` | New ent tables per artifact type | In-memory index already rebuilds from blobs; ADR-004 |
| Artifact search | Extra documents in the existing BM25 engine + exact maps | `session-search-fts5` ADR-001 | SQLite FTS5; `LIKE` over blob | FTS5 already rejected (no build tag); `LIKE` is unindexed |

## Tech Debt Disposition

| Area | Existing Issue | Disposition | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go` (2450 lines, ~40 `PrNumber/PrURL` reads) | God file keyed on the scalar item PR | **Isolate via seam** (T5.1.1 pin tests, T5.1.2 `PRSetReader` + `itemPRs`) | Restructuring it is out of scope and risky; reconcilers call the seam |
| `session/pr_status_poller.go` (531 lines) | `fetchAndUpdatePRStatus` and the `checkAllSessions` loop interleave filter, discovery, fetch, persist; modified functions face gocognit/funlen | **Refactor-first** (T3.1.1) | Extract `pollableSessions`, `discoverPRsForBranch`, `pollOne` before widening the unit of work |
| `session/pr_tracking.go` (234 lines) | Every method repeats the `GitHub()` / ref / number prelude | **Refactor-first** (T3.1.2) | Extract `resolvePR(ref)` once, then add the selector |
| `session/session_driver.go` `scanAndLinkPRURL` | Direct field write outside the actor and outside `actor-field-guard` | **Extend as-is plus fix one call** (T2.2.1, T2.2.4) | Route through `AttachPR`; add a field-scoped `pr-field-guard` (NOT a file-list extension of the field-agnostic `actor-field-guard`, which fails on unrelated `session_driver.go` lines) |
| `instance_actor_setters.go` (already ~30 `Set*/Attach*` setters), `instance_snapshot.go` | Large setter file; stable pattern | **Extend into a new file** (T2.1.2); snapshot field extend as-is (T2.1.1) | New PR setters live in `session/instance_pr_set.go` (still `Instance` methods, same lock and snapshot protocol); `instance_actor_setters.go` keeps only thin `SetGitHubPR*` wrappers |
| `server/services/session_service_delegates.go` (973 lines) | Routing file the new RPCs would swell | **Extend into new files** (T4.2.3, T7.2.4, T9.2.1, T10.1.1) | PR handlers in `session_service_pr_refs.go`, artifact handlers in `session_service_artifacts.go`; the delegates file only routes |
| Ent schema, `ent_repository_migrations.go` | Stable pattern | **Extend as-is** (T1.3.1, T1.5.1) | New entity and one backfill step |
| `server/` legacy readers (`pr_annotations.go`, `review_queue_manager.go`, `prwatch_sessions.go`, `session_retention_sweeper.go`, `unfinished_work_service.go`, `approval_*`, `instance_adapter.go`, `debug_snapshot.go`) | Read the scalar | **Extend as-is** behind the reader scopes, with an allow-list ratchet test (T2.2.4) | Missing a reader is the stale-PR risk; the ratchet makes the set explicit. Each allow-list entry is tagged snapshot-backed or `InstanceData`-backed (the sweeper, `unfinished_work_service.go`, `approval_*` read `InstanceData`, not snapshots). Backlog-side `PrNumber/PrURL` readers get a second allow-list (T2.2.5) |
| `session/artifacts/` `scan.go`, `store.go` | Empty-scan early return, oldest-first caps, `agent-*` skip | **Refactor-first** inside E7 (T7.2.x) | These are the defects being fixed |

## Migration Plan

- **Schema**: three new hand-written ent entities whose identity columns store the canonical lowercase `PRRef` (see Glossary; an ent validator rejects upper-case values); generated output is gitignored and never committed: `session/ent/schema/pull_request_state.go` (facts, unique `(host, owner, repo, number)`), `session/ent/schema/session_pr.go` (links) and `session/ent/schema/session_pr_tombstone.go`. Generate with `go run -mod=mod entgo.io/ent/cmd/ent generate --feature sql/upsert ./session/ent/schema`. `client.Schema.Create()` (`session/ent_repository.go:237`) adds the tables and indexes additively; no column drops.
- **Data migration**: `session/session_pr_backfill.go`, run by `Storage.RunPRStartupMaintenance(ctx)`, called from `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions` (`server/dependencies.go`, right after `storage := sessionService.GetStorage()`, VERIFIED line 425) only when the new `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance` is true (set at the service `core-deps` phase, `main.go:384`, and in `BuildDependencies`; false for the plain `BuildCoreDeps()` of `buildMCPDeps`, `main.go:1871`), so it runs before `BuildServiceDeps` constructs any poller or sweeper and before `LoadInstances` hydrates and projects. It is deliberately NOT a `startupMigrations` entry (architecture C1): VERIFIED `startupMigrations` run on EVERY `NewEntRepository` call (`session/ent_repository.go:263-268`), which also serves `cmd/ssq-hooks/main.go:633` (gemini/agy/opencode and `--db` paths), `daemon/daemon.go:24`, `main.go:558, 658, 746` and `integration_credential_cmd.go:32`. Those short-lived processes keep running only `Schema.Create` plus the existing non-destructive migrations, never the PR maintenance, so a SQLite busy in a hook cannot exit 1, no hook call re-zeroes verification counters, and no hook pays the per-boot candidate queries. The stdio MCP fallback (`buildMCPDeps`, `main.go:1871`, spawned per Claude Code subagent when the HTTP server is unreachable, `main.go:141-157`) is excluded the same way: it calls `BuildCoreDeps()` with `RunPRMaintenance` false, so it can neither abort MCP init (`main.go:158`) on a busy database, nor re-zero counters under a live refresher, nor repeat the candidate queries per subagent (Phase 4 B1; the call is NOT unconditional inside `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions`). The step is constructive-only, non-resurrecting, and also performs the rollback import (a non-service process that loads and saves a link-less session keeps today's set-only full-save behavior, T2.3.3, so it cannot overwrite a legacy number the maintenance has not imported yet; INFERRED, the hook paths were not traced further):
  - No run-once marker (the repo has no `migration_marker` mechanism, and a marker breaks rollback). Every boot runs a cheap, bounded pass over two candidate sets: (1) sessions with `github_pr_number > 0` whose `PRRef` (session `github_host/owner/repo` plus number) is NOT represented by one of that session's own links. This covers never-linked sessions AND the rollback case (a reverted binary wrote only a legacy number to a session that already had links), and because it runs before any snapshot is built, `buildSnapshot` can never project over an unimported legacy PR; (2) items with `pr_number > 0` whose `PRRef` is NOT represented by ANY link of the item OR of any session in its `ItemSession` rows (every round and role; "unrepresented legacy item number"; a PRRef carrying a tombstone of the item OR of any of its sessions is excluded, architecture C1). This is deliberately wider than "not in the derived item set": the `item.pr_number` mirror (T5.2.3) can hold a PR that is reachable only through the current or a SUPERSEDED round's session link, and re-creating an item-direct link for it would make it permanent and never superseded (architecture N3). It still covers pre-feature items and the rollback case (an old binary wrote only the scalar, so no link anywhere). Cost: the existing chunked `ListItemSessionsForItems` (`session/storage_backlog.go:364`) plus T1.3.2's chunked `ListBySessionUUIDs`, `ListByItem` and bulk tombstone reads (all wave 0; no wave-2 dependency). The reconciler therefore needs no import step (T1.5.2).
  - **Item `PRRef` derivation (architecture C6, adversarial C1)**: the item has only `pr_url` and `pr_number` (VERIFIED `session/ent/schema/backlog_item.go:98-102`), and no existing helper parses host/owner/repo from a PR URL (VERIFIED: `ParsePRNumberFromURL`, `jules_session_poller.go:656`, returns only the number). T1.5.1 adds one small parser in `session_pr_backfill.go`: `net/url` parse of `pr_url`, path `/{owner}/{repo}/pull/{n}`, host from the URL (GHE hosts included), run through `NewPRRef`, and the parsed number MUST equal `pr_number`. No `repo_path` or git-remote fallback (the item path has none today). Anything else (empty URL, other shape, number mismatch) is the skip class `skipped_item_bad_ref`.
  - **Failure handling (adversarial C6)**: the pass is one transaction per candidate and, unlike its template `runGitHubPRURLBackfill` (which swallows query errors), classifies errors. TRANSIENT errors (candidate or links query failure, begin/commit failure, SQLite busy/locked, I/O, context cancellation) NEVER exit the service (Phase 4 pre-mortem #1: with a 5 s busy timeout on the 1.4 GB single-connection database, `RestartSec=5s` and `StartLimitBurst=10` in 600 s would leave the unit `failed`, VERIFIED `scripts/install-service.sh:334-335, 345-346`, `session/ent_repository.go:190, 203`). `RunPRStartupMaintenance` retries the pass in-process 3 times with backoff (1 s, 4 s, 16 s, injected sleeper), then logs, increments `ssq_pr_maintenance_failed_total` and returns nil so the service CONTINUES booting on the fail-closed rules below; a 5-minute ticker, started with the call and stopping after the first success, runs the pass again until it succeeds. Until a pass has succeeded in THIS process, `VerifyExhausted()` is forced false for unverified-terminal rows read through the storage bulk queries (counters left by an earlier process are not trusted and `ResetVerifyCounters` has not run), so the gate and sweeper read them as non-terminal. Only a database that cannot be opened at all aborts (existing behavior, before this step). DETERMINISTIC per-row failures (an ent validator rejection, `ErrZeroPRRef`, the 50-link cap, the one-primary index, a `NewPRRef` or item-URL parse failure) would fail identically on every boot, and the unit uses `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=5s` (VERIFIED `scripts/install-service.sh:345-346`), so aborting on them is a permanent boot loop that also takes backlog automation down. They are therefore a counted skip class: that candidate's transaction is rolled back, the row is counted in `skipped_invalid` (sessions) or `skipped_item_bad_ref` / `skipped_item_invalid` (items) and logged as a count plus the first 10 titles, and the pass continues. Classification is by error type (`ent.IsValidationError`, `ent.IsConstraintError`, the package's sentinel errors), anything unrecognized is treated as transient (retry, then continue booting), never as skip. Other classified skips (unrecoverable host, empty uuid, tombstoned) are non-errors. A partial import (a transient failure that outlasted the retries) cannot be followed by a destructive projection either: `Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented` is computed at load from the stored links (T2.3.2), so every session the pass did not reach keeps its legacy columns untouched. Belt and braces for the skipped classes: `Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented` (an `Instance` field carried into `InstanceSnapshot` and back out through `ToInstanceData`, T2.3.2; NOT a value set on the `InstanceData` returned by load, which every full save rebuilds, adversarial C2) is true when a session has a legacy number that no link represents and no tombstone covers; while true, full saves leave `github_pr_number`/`github_pr_url` untouched and the drift reconciler skips (and counts) the session instead of overwriting. Skipped ITEMS are protected the same way: the cleanup gate and sweeper step 4 treat an item with `pr_number > 0`, no link anywhere and a skip-class ref as NOT safe (T5.4.1), the exit being an operator setting a valid `pr_url` through the manual-link RPC (next boot imports it), see Known Risks. Per-boot read cost is bounded: the pass selects only the needed columns (`uuid`, `title`, `github_pr_number`, host/owner/repo, terminal flag, path) of rows with `github_pr_number > 0`, fetches their links with `session_uuid IN` queries chunked by the existing `itemSessionBatchChunk` (500, `session/storage_backlog.go:357`) and diffs in memory; items likewise with `pr_number > 0`, through the existing chunked `ListItemSessionsForItems`. At most `maxBackfillWritesPerBoot` (2,000; sessions and items counted separately; only SUCCESSFUL imports count, skipped and rolled-back candidates do not, so poisoned rows cannot starve healthy ones, architecture N2) candidates are imported per boot; the rest are logged as `deferred=N`, stay protected by the same fail-closed rules (an unrepresented legacy number is not safe: gate clause (iii), sweeper step 4) and are picked up on later boots (representation is the cursor, no marker). The per-boot skipped log is a count plus the first 10 titles.
  - Host rule: an empty `github_host` means github.com (schema comment, VERIFIED `session/ent/schema/session.go:153-155`), so a session with owner/repo and an empty host is NOT a skip (`hostFromClonedPath` may still override it with a GHE host); only a session with no owner/repo is skipped.
  - At most one `primary` per session is enforced (partial unique index on `role='primary'` per `session_uuid`, or a transactional check). A `(owner, PRRef)` present in `session_pr_tombstone` is never recreated.
  - Skips, never guesses, a `PRRef` it cannot build (no owner/repo; reuses `hostFromClonedPath` and the session `github_host` column, empty host = github.com). A skipped session is counted, logged by title, and remains protected: the sweeper and the gate treat "legacy PR number set, no link" as NOT safe to delete unless the persisted terminal flag is true (shared `LegacyOnlyPRSafe`, defined in T4.3.0, imported by T4.3.1 and T5.4.1). Skipped sessions and items are re-logged every boot as a count plus the first 10 titles (no marker); the only exit is a manual attach by URL (runbook note).
  - Sessions with an empty `uuid` (`Optional().Default("")`, `session/ent/schema/session.go:24-28`) are not skipped silently: the pass first calls the existing uuid-assignment path if one exists (T1.5.1 finds it); otherwise they are counted in `skipped_no_uuid`, logged by title, and covered by the same "legacy number, no link is not safe" rule.
  - Seeding never defaults to `open` or `merged` and never modifies an existing row (invariant I1: seeds go through `EnsureState`, insert-if-absent). VERIFIED fact that drives the rule: the session schema stores only `github_pr_url`, `github_pr_number` and the `github_pr_status_terminal` bool (`session/ent/schema/session.go:136-145`), so no stored merged/closed value exists. Seed table, per candidate:
    - session `github_pr_status_terminal=true`: `state=unknown`, `terminal=true` ("unverified terminal"). The legacy poller probably observed a terminal PR, but the flag can be STALE for this number (VERIFIED: `scanAndLinkPRURL` assigns a new number and URL without touching the flag, `session_driver.go:961-962`; the legacy poller then skips the session forever, so a later open PR B inherits a merged PR A's `true`). So the flag is evidence, not proof: the row counts as terminal for the gate and sweeper only once its verify is EXHAUSTED (`PRView.VerifyExhausted()`: >= 5 counted failures, first to last >= 30 minutes, see T3.2.4), never before; the done verdict reads it as held `unknown` even then; until then it is treated as `unknown` non-terminal and blocks. A successful poll resolves it to its real state first. Historical sessions are therefore blocked for at most the cold drain, and a PR that can never be fetched (deleted repo, no token) is released by exhaustion or by detach. The UI shows "Closed or merged (unverified)", never "Merged" (the adapter derives the label from `state=unknown && terminal`); the poller resolves it (T3.2.1).
    - session terminal flag false or absent: `state=unknown`, `terminal=false` (blocks cleanup until the poller resolves it; never a guessed `open`).
    - item `done`: unverified terminal, as for a terminal session; any other item status (`pr_pending`, `archived`, ...): `unknown`, `terminal=false`. Known gap, accepted: a manually completed `done` item whose PR is actually still open is seeded unverified terminal and, once polled, becomes a normal open PR that holds cleanup; no worse than today, where cleanup ignores PRs.
  - One `PRRef` seeded by several candidates in the same pass (a session and an item): the pass first collects `map[PRRef]seed` and the row is unverified terminal ONLY if every contributing candidate is terminal-seeded; otherwise `unknown`, `terminal=false` (non-terminal wins, fail closed, independent of iteration order). The common historical case, a merged PR whose item is still `pr_pending`, therefore stays non-terminal until the poller resolves it, and the item verdict never sees an unverified terminal row for a `pr_pending` item. Later boots only add links to existing rows.
  - Item-direct links are created for `backlog_item.pr_number > 0`. Logs `[Migration] session_pr backfill: created=N imported_unrepresented=R skipped=M skipped_no_uuid=K`.
- **Reversibility**: additive and reversible by flag. The table can stay populated while the flag is off. No `down` script; dropping the table later is a separate cleanup project.
- **Zero-downtime strategy**: expand/contract. Phase 1 writes both stores always (sessions through `AttachPR`; items through the item PR writers, which upsert the item-direct link unconditionally, T5.3.1) and keeps per-PR state current even while the flag is off: a flag-independent state refresher (T3.2.5; stoppable live by the `refresher_off` scope of the same flag, T1.4.1) polls every non-terminal `PullRequestState` from the first deploy, so the cleanup gate and sweeper (which are not flag-gated) never read stale rows during the soak, and flipping `reader:poller` does not start a cold poll burst. The legacy columns are ALWAYS the `ActivePR` projection once a session has a link; the flag gates readers only, so there is no snapshot-republish problem on a flag flip (the mirror does not depend on config, `buildSnapshot` stays pure). Phase 2 flips reader scopes one at a time, in order poller, backlog, actions, ui (enforced in the flag service, T1.4.1). Legacy columns are never dropped in this project.
- **Write atomicity**: the link upsert, the state `EnsureState` (insert-if-absent) and the mirror columns on the `session` row are one ent transaction (T2.3.1). On a crash the DB is authoritative; the in-memory snapshot is rebuilt from it at load. Persisting happens from the snapshot at persist time (never from a stale captured value), persists are serialized per session, and every mirror correction goes through the actor, so the drift reconciler cannot race the poller (T1.5.2). A persist never updates state columns (invariant I1). Accepted gap: a poll result is one `PullRequestState` update followed by N per-session mirror writes (one per linked session) with no enclosing transaction (T3.2.2); a crash between them leaves the state row ahead of some sessions' mirror columns, repaired by the always-on reconciler.
- **Proto**: additive only (`Session.pull_requests = 100`, new messages, new optional `pr` fields). Legacy `github_pr_*` scalars (fields 25, 26, 33-35) stay populated for the mobile app.
- **Rollback procedure**: set `session_multi_pr` off globally (and clear scopes). Readers return to the legacy columns, which were dual-written the whole time, so nothing is restored. If a bug is in the writer path, revert the PR; the extra tables are harmless. Reverting accepts TODAY's behavior, not the new protections: a reverted binary's sweeper reads only the session mirror (`github_pr_number` + terminal flag), so with a pinned merged PR and a non-pinned open PR it treats the session as safe (adversarial M1), and the cleanup gate is gone. Revert then later re-deploy is a supported path: the old binary writes only legacy columns and creates no links, so on re-deploy the boot backfill imports every legacy number that no link represents (unless tombstoned) BEFORE instances load and project. Role of an imported link: `primary` only when the session has no `primary` yet, otherwise `followup` (never `primary` when one exists), so the one-primary partial unique index can never reject the supported rollback path. Tested through `NewEntRepository` + `RunPRStartupMaintenance` + `LoadInstances` + a full save in T1.5.3.
- **Drift guard**: a startup and slow periodic reconciler (always on, no scope condition) asserts the legacy columns equal the `ActivePR` projection, fixes drift through the actor (including a TOMBSTONED legacy value, which is simply overwritten with the projection; an untombstoned unrepresented value, i.e. a backfill-skipped session, is left alone and counted; the flag read is the `Instance` field, not `InstanceData`), and increments `ssq_session_pr_mirror_drift_total` (T1.5.2). It does no import (the boot backfill does). It cannot see a link that exists only in a snapshot and never reached the DB (failed persist); that is covered because every full save upserts the snapshot's LINKS idempotently (next save retries) and on load the DB wins (T2.3.1).
- **Artifact blob v2 compatibility and kill switch**: an older binary must still load a v2 blob (unknown fields ignored, `pr_urls` still populated; tested in T8.1.1). The scan-version bump that rescans every session's history is behind the live-settable flag `artifact_scan_v2` (default off; T8.1.6), so the deploy does not trigger the rescan until the owner flips it, and flipping it off stops further rescans.
- **Orphan-link GC (one rule, adversarial M3)**: links and tombstones are deleted with their session in `DeleteSession`; a nightly `SessionPRLinkGC` (T1.3.5) deletes ONLY links and tombstones whose owner row (`session_uuid` or `backlog_item_id`) is gone AND that are older than `orphanGCGrace` (24 hours), and `PullRequestState` rows through ONE conditional statement (`DELETE ... WHERE NOT EXISTS (link) AND created_at < now - orphanGCGrace`), so an attach racing the GC (`EnsureState` finds the row, link insert follows) cannot lose its state row; search RPCs also filter links whose session is gone. A state whose owners were deleted by a crashed path is polled for at most until the next nightly GC (an ETag 304 each interval); no separate eligibility bound.

## Observability Plan

- **Logs** (structured, `log.Info`): `session pr attached` (session, PRRef, role, source), `session active pr changed` (session, from, to, reason), `session_pr backfill` counts, `session pr detached` (user), `session pr pinned`, per-PR `terminal` transitions, `artifact scan state` transitions. Error paths log error plus session and PRRef.
- **Metrics**: `ssq_session_pr_count` histogram (PRs per session, observed on attach); `ssq_pr_poll_total{state}` counter by PR state, including `unknown` (confirms merged and closed stop being polled); `ssq_pr_poll_failures_total{reason}`; `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_total{path}` (first block of an item/session per path, deduplicated across the 60s re-evaluation) and gauge `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items{path}` (currently blocked; which cleanup path was held back by an open or unknown PR); `ssq_pr_discovery_list_calls_total`; `ssq_session_pr_mirror_drift_total`; `ssq_pr_maintenance_failed_total` (boot maintenance gave up after retries; the 5-minute ticker keeps trying); `ssq_artifact_scan_total{result}`; `ssq_artifact_search_seconds` histogram (search over 100ms gets a metric per the template); `ssq_artifact_blob_bytes` histogram (pre-mortem #9) and `ssq_artifact_index_docs` gauge (pre-mortem #14). Adoption and usage signal (Triad iteration 1, product gap 4): `ssq_pr_pin_total{action=pin|unpin|undo}` (emitted in T4.2.3), `ssq_artifact_search_queries_total{kind=text|pr|sha|branch,result=hit|miss}` (T9.2.1), `ssq_artifact_browse_total` (T10.1.1) and `ssq_issue_close_offer_total{outcome=offered|blocked|closed|failed}` (offered and blocked in T5.5.1, closed and failed in T5.5.2; "declined" = offered - closed - blocked, because Not now sends no request); `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows` gauge (T3.3.3); `ssq_pr_live_work_hold_total{result=held|released|max_hold}` (T5.2.1).
- **Alerts and dashboard (T3.3.3)**: one dashboard `docs/observability/grafana/dashboards/session-pr-tracking.json` (same layout as `client-network.json`, see `docs/observability/README.md`) with panels for the K10 request budget (`increase(ssq_pr_poll_total[1h])` against the 1,500 line), the `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows` gauge (the K2 drain), `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items{path}`, the drift counter and the adoption counters above; plus two warning-level alert rules (no paging): hourly poll total above 1,500 for 2 consecutive hours, and `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows > 0` for more than 24 hours. The drift counter is also read by the rollout runbook.

## Risk Control

- **Feature flag**: `session_multi_pr`, default off, global plus scopes `reader:poller|backlog|actions|ui`, plus the kill-switch scope `refresher_off` (default off = the refresher runs; outside the enable order; read as an explicit override only; T1.4.1). Registered in `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, proven in `server/services/config_surface_proof_test.go`. Live-settable; no env var.
- **What the flag gates**: reader behavior only (not the mirror, not the writes). Links and `PullRequestState` are written, and kept current, from the first deploy. Two safe-direction changes are NOT behind the flag because they only ever withhold a delete: the retention sweeper and the cleanup gate fail closed on any non-terminal, `unknown`, or "legacy number but no link" PR (T4.3.1, T5.4.2). Because those two read state rows while the flag is off, row refresh is ALSO not flag-gated: the state refresher polls rows from the first deploy (T3.2.5); `reader:poller` only retires the legacy per-session loop. Artifacts epics E7 to E10 are bug fixes and additive features outside `session_multi_pr`, except the cross-session `/artifacts` page, which shows regardless (read-only); the blob-v2 rescan has its own kill switch `artifact_scan_v2` (T8.1.6). Rollback for artifacts is flip `artifact_scan_v2` off, or revert-PR.
- **Always-on exceptions (what flag-off does NOT undo; mirrored in requirements.md Risk Control, Phase 4 pre-mortem #3)**: (1) link and `PullRequestState` writes, and the legacy scalars as the `ActivePR` projection (differs from last-writer-wins only when two PRs are open and the older one matches the current branch); (2) the retention sweeper and cleanup gate reading link rows, fail closed; (3) boot-time PR maintenance and `ResetVerifyCounters` (service path only, never non-fatal-blocking); (4) the artifact scanner never seeds a PR any more (ADR-004; deliberate, so a mentioned-only PR is no longer polled even with the flag off); (5) the drift reconciler. Only the network load can need stopping without a deploy, so the refresher (T3.2.5) alone gets a live kill switch: `UpdateFeatureFlag{name:"session_multi_pr", scope:"refresher_off", mutation:SET_ENABLED}` stops new polling (CLEAR_SCOPE restores it); rows then go stale, which only ever withholds deletes (non-terminal rows block the gate and sweeper). Runbook step 0 for the first deploy: watch `ssq_pr_poll_total` for 10 minutes with every reader scope off and set the `refresher_off` scope (SET_ENABLED) if the hourly rate exceeds the budget in Known Risk K10.
- **Rollback**: flip the flag off; or revert the PR. Legacy fields are dual-written for the whole rollout.
- **Staged rollout**: flip `reader:poller` first and watch `ssq_pr_poll_total` and the drift counter; then `reader:backlog` (after the retention and cleanup tests are green); then `reader:actions`; then `reader:ui`. Soak window between stages is a wall-clock blocker (owner).

## Known Risks (accepted)

Each risk is accepted for this project, has an owner and a detection signal (K1-K14); none is fixed by polling or new machinery. The first four are the owner's "Accepted as written" decisions (requirements.md, 2026-11-03).

| # | Risk | Consequence | Owner | Detection signal | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K1 | A PR reopened on GitHub is noticed only via the `pull_request.reopened` webhook (optional endpoint, `docs/how-to/expose-github-webhook-endpoint.md`); a `closed` row is sticky and never polled | An `AllClosedUnmerged` item can archive and its clean worktree can be removed while the PR is open again; commits survive on the pushed branch | Tyler | Runbook note; webhook delivery failures; a PR the UI shows closed that GitHub shows open | Enable the webhook; user attaches by URL / re-opens the item |
| K2 | Backfill-seeded unverified terminal rows count as non-terminal until a poll verifies them (5 counted failures over >= 30 minutes) | Old done/archived items briefly keep worktrees after boot; frequent SERVICE restarts (every `make install-service`) reset verification (hook, CLI and MCP-fallback processes do not, architecture C1, Phase 4 B1), so unfetchable historical rows may never exhaust and keep their worktrees (safe direction) | Tyler | `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows` gauge; runbook note | Detach the PR; wait for a poll |
| K3 | Item-direct PRs are never superseded | An abandoned open round-1 agent-reported PR holds the item in `pr_pending` and the shared worktree path | Tyler | Stuck report names the PR; `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items` | `DetachItemPullRequest` or the PR going terminal |
| K4 | Force-reset and backward-to-ready stay ungated by open PRs (warning only) | A clean checkout under an open PR is removed; branch and PR survive | Tyler | T5.4.5 log line and response text | None needed |
| K5 | An `unknown`/unverified-terminal row whose repo the token cannot see (private repo, SSO, expired host token) is never counted as failed, so it never exhausts (T3.2.4 classifier) | The session/item/worktree it belongs to is held until detached | Tyler | `ssq_pr_poll_failures_total{reason}` (`repo_inaccessible`, `auth`, `rate_limit`), stuck report | Detach |
| K6 | Item `pr_number > 0` with an empty or unparseable `pr_url` (skip class `skipped_item_bad_ref`) fails closed in the gate and sweeper | A `done`/`archived` item's worktree is retained with no PR ref to detach | Tyler | Boot log count + first 10 titles; stuck report "cleanup blocked" list | Set a valid `pr_url` via the manual-link RPC (next boot imports it) |
| K7 | Deleting the latest work session of a pr_pending item (`DeleteSession` also removes its tombstones) makes it look never-linked, so an earlier round's session link re-enters the set | A merged round-1 PR could complete the item | Tyler | Log line `item current round fell back to earlier session` (T5.1.2); the delete is an explicit operator or retention action | Re-open the item; avoid deleting the live round's session |
| K8 | CAS drops older observations; a wall-clock step back could delay recording a merge | Row lags until the clock catches up; bounded by the future-`last_polled_at` clamp (T1.3.2) | Tyler | `ssq_pr_state_observation_dropped_total{reason}`, warn after 3 consecutive drops of one row | None needed |
| K9 | A retention-swept session's tombstones are deleted with it, so a stale `pr_number` on an `archived` item is re-imported at the next boot as an item-direct `unknown` link | A fresh `unknown` row until polled (benign for `done` items) | Tyler | Backfill log `imported_unrepresented` | Poll resolves it |
| K10 | Poll cost grows with abandoned-open PRs (every non-terminal row of archived/suspended sessions and item-only links is polled each interval), each service restart empties the in-memory ETag cache (one full GET per open PR), and with every scope off the legacy loop and the refresher each fetch the same PR (pre-mortem #8) | Secondary-rate-limit 403s in unrelated features (prwatch, up-next, backlog sync and webhooks share one token) | Tyler | `ssq_pr_poll_total` per hour; runbook budget: total PR requests <= 1,500 per hour | Set the `refresher_off` scope (T1.4.1); detach abandoned PRs. A slower cadence for old rows is deferred until the soak shows the need |
| K11 | `repeated SessionPullRequest` rides every list response and watch event (about 20 fields per PR, cap 50 per session), populated regardless of reader scope (pre-mortem #10) | Slower mobile list loads on Tailscale/cellular right after deploy | Tyler | Size of a captured list response; mobile-app check (Owner confirmations pending #10) | Verify the mobile build against a captured response BEFORE merge, not only before the `reader:ui` flip. Measured, not conditional (T4.1.2 AC): serialized bytes added per `ListSessions` response must stay at or below 1.5 KiB per session at 3 PRs and 40 KiB for the 200-session, 3-PR fixture; above that ceiling the cap (non-terminal PRs plus the 3 newest in list responses, the full list only in `GetSession`) is mandatory before merge |
| K12 | At the maximum hold (1 hour after the FIRST merge) the item goes `done` while its current-round work session is still live, and RULE L (T5.4.6) then skips archival and the pane kill for that session (the 60 s sessions sweep leaves the item unsettled, so it re-reads the item every pass until the session idles or ends), so the pane, its claude process and its MCP subprocesses keep running and the worktree stays until the session goes idle past `liveWorkIdleWindow`, ends or the operator ends it (Triad iteration 2; before the rule the pane was killed at max hold, mid-work) | A session that never idles holds memory (the 2026-07-29 OOM shape) beyond the item's completion; bounded to one live session per such item | Tyler | `ssq_pr_live_work_hold_total{result=max_hold}`, the one `notify()` and the MAX_HOLD line on the item; `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items{path}` | **End session** on the item; or overrule RULE L (Owner confirmations pending #2) |
| K13 | The T1.5.4 rehearsal runs `RunPRStartupMaintenance`, the retry ticker (started and stopped), the bulk load and one sweeper and gate evaluation on a `sqlite3 .backup` copy with recording cleaner and deleter, but it does NOT boot the service: the real `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions` and `BuildDependencies` wiring, the real tickers and the real `WorktreeCleaner` would act on the live worktree paths the copy's rows point to | The boot order (maintenance before poller, sweeper and cleanup tickers) and goroutine start in the real server are verified only by the fake-recorded ordering test (Story 1.5), not against production data | Tyler | First-deploy runbook step below; `ssq_pr_maintenance_failed_total`; the `[Migration]` backfill counts compared with the rehearsal record | Manual first-deploy rehearsal (T11.1.1 runbook step -1): back up the database with `sqlite3 .backup`, deploy with every reader scope off, watch the maintenance log line and `ssq_pr_poll_total` for 10 minutes, compare the counts with the rehearsal record, and only then leave the refresher on |
| K14 | Not fixable by text (Triad iteration 3): (a) whether `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` advances during a silent long tool run is unverified, so after `liveWorkIdleWindow` a quiet but working agent can look idle and its item complete; (b) screen-reader announcement wording, text-only 200% zoom, the real on-screen keyboard and real-device 44px feel are manual-only; (c) the keep or cut thresholds for the adoption counters are defaults until the baseline is recorded | (a) an agent's last minutes of work are not held by the live-work rule (its PR and branch survive; the session is archived once idle, as today); (b) a wording or reflow defect ships behind `reader:ui` | Tyler | (a) the worker's PR note on T5.2.1 and `ssq_pr_live_work_hold_total{result=released}` followed by new commits on the item's branch; (b) the T6.3.2 PR checklist line; (c) the T1.5.4 baseline record | (a) raise `liveWorkIdleWindow` (config constant) or drop the hold (Owner confirmations pending #2); (b) hold the `reader:ui` flip until the checklist is signed; (c) set the thresholds from the baseline at the first soak review |

## Owner confirmations pending

ONE block (Triad iteration 2 consolidated the owner items that used to sit in "Unresolved Questions"). Each row has the default the plan implements if the owner does not answer. References elsewhere in this plan to an owner item in "Unresolved Questions" mean the row of this block named in the reference.

| # | Decision | Recommended default (what the plan implements) | Needed before | If the owner overrules |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ADR-003 (PROPOSED): cleanup-gate scope. The gate blocks worktree removal at the single choke point while any linked PR is non-terminal; explicit removals stay ungated with a visible warning (T5.4.5): user destroy/pause, agent-callable MCP pause/destroy (`tools_lifecycle.go:410, 510`) and operator force-reset / backward-to-ready (`stopLiveWorkAndReviewSessions`, path 14) | Accept as written: gate the automated paths (terminal cleanup, retention sweeper), warn on explicit removals; ADR-003 moves from PROPOSED to ACCEPTED | T5.4.2 | A hard gate on explicit removals: extend T5.4.5 to refuse (one added test per path row 10-14); the done rule itself is already an owner decision and does not change |
| 2 | `liveWorkIdleWindow` N = 10 minutes, hold in `pr_pending` rather than going done and only keeping the worktree, the maximum hold `liveWorkMaxHold` = 1 hour after the FIRST merge, and RULE L (a live current-round work session is never archived or pane-killed by terminal-item cleanup; the sweeps, which stay unsettled for that item, archive it on the first 60 s pass after it is idle or ended) | N = 10 minutes; hold in `pr_pending` with reason `merged, session still active`; after 1 hour the item proceeds to done with a visible warning and the live session keeps its pane and worktree until it goes idle, ends or the operator ends it (Known Risk K12). Open sub-question: whether `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` advances during a silent long tool run (T5.2.1) | T5.2.1 | Change the two constants (config, no code); "keep worktree only" removes the T5.2.1 hold and keeps the gate clause; dropping RULE L restores kill-at-done and reverts the max-hold wording to "the agent is stopped" |
| 3 | Scope for issue-close on re-import: `reader:backlog` (github.com with a token only, never GHE, never a source-linked item) | `reader:backlog` | T5.5.1 | A separate sub-scope `reader:issue_close` (one more flag scope in T1.4.1) |
| 4 | Session archival (archive plus kill pane: `archiveItemWorkSessions`, `reconcileTerminalItemSessions`) stays UNGATED by open PRs because it removes no data and gating it would reintroduce the 2026-07-29 OOM leak; only worktree removal and retention are gated (a live work session is skipped, row 2) | Accept as written | T5.4.2 | Gate archival too: accept the OOM exposure or add a cap |
| 5 | Attaching a PR to an already-`done` item is allowed (item-direct or session link), does not reopen the item, and holds cleanup until that PR is terminal (Story 5.4) | Accept as written | T5.4.2 | Reject the attach, or reopen the item |
| 6 | Backfill treats a historical `done` item or terminal session as terminal-but-unverified (UI: "Closed or merged (unverified)" until one poll); after N=5 consecutive counted poll failures over at least 30 minutes a non-terminal PR becomes `unknown` and blocks done and cleanup until detached (T3.2.4) | Wording as written; N=5; confirm N at the `reader:poller` soak | T3.2.4; T6.1.3 | Change the constants and the strings |
| 7 | CHANGED BEHAVIOR (iteration 3, safe direction): a work session's worktree is retained while ANY PR linked to that session (or directly to its item) is non-terminal, even when its round is superseded; the retention sweeper likewise keeps an archived session whose own links are non-terminal. Release is by the PR going terminal or `DetachSessionPullRequest` | Accept as written | T5.4.2 | Restore marking superseded links `abandoned` (the pre-iteration-3 design) |
| 8 | CHANGED BEHAVIOR (iteration 3, scoped in iteration 4): a PR that reached the item ONLY through a superseded round's session link does not count toward the done verdict even if it later merges ("current" = the latest work session, falling back to an earlier one only while the later one has never linked); item-direct PRs (agent `report_pr_created`, Jules, manual, `pushAndCreatePR`) are never superseded, so a different round-2 PR does not release an open round-1 item-direct PR | Accept both halves | T5.1.2 | Count superseded-round PRs, or release item-direct PRs on a new round |
| 9 | Boot rehearsal (T1.5.4) copies the production database with `sqlite3 .backup` onto a disk path (never tmpfs) and uses a stated `ListSessions` p99 budget of +5 ms; the manual first-deploy rehearsal step in the runbook (Known Risk K13) | Both as written | The first deploy | A different budget; a staging instance |
| 10 | Mobile app: the current build tolerates the additive `Session` fields (proto3 ignores unknown fields, INFERRED; confirm no strict decoder) | Verify against a captured `ListSessions` response before merge (K11) | Merge (K11 ceiling); the `reader:ui` flip | Gate the field behind `reader:ui` server-side |
| 11 | Soak sign-off between flag stages and the discovery cadence for sessions whose PRs are all terminal (`terminalDiscoveryBackoff`, plan default 10x the no-PR backoff) | Decide at each soak review; keep the default until the first soak | Each flag flip | Adjust the constant |
| 12 | Keep or cut rule for the adoption counters, read at each soak review against the baseline in requirements.md over 30 days of use after `reader:ui` is on. The thresholds below are DEFAULTS until the T1.5.4 / T7.1.1 baseline is recorded; then the owner sets each as a fraction of baseline usage (requirements.md). Small multi-PR share rule: keep `reader:ui` if the 90-day baseline finds ANY session with more than one PR; if it finds none, keep, demote or cut is the owner's call. Defaults: `ssq_pr_pin_total` pin plus undo below 5 uses, `ssq_artifact_search_queries_total` below 10 queries or under half of them hits, `ssq_artifact_browse_total` below 10, `ssq_issue_close_offer_total{outcome=offered}` equal to 0. Below a threshold the owner decides keep, demote (move behind an overflow or remove the nav entry) or cut; nothing is removed automatically | The defaults until the baseline is recorded, then the baseline-fraction rule and the small-share rule as written | The first `reader:ui` soak review | Other thresholds or fractions |

Worker schedule (Phase 4 pre-mortem #2; replaces "widest fan-out is four concurrent epics in W2"): waves are dependency tiers; AT MOST 2 workers run at any time, each in its own worktree created from the feature branch, one PR per wave rebased on main before the wave starts (pre-mortem #12).
  W0: slot A = T11.1.4 (table check and briefs, XS, before any worker is spawned), then E1 (S1.1 || S1.2 -> S1.3 -> S1.4, T1.5.1); slot B = E7.
  W1: slot A = E2, then T1.5.2 -> T1.5.3 -> T1.5.4; slot B idle (E2 is one serialized file chain).

Step 2 (after E3 and T4.3.0): A = E5 in one chain ordered by the dependency table (S5.1; T5.2.1; T5.2.4; T5.4.1; T5.2.3; Story 5.3; T5.4.2, T5.4.6, T5.4.3, T5.4.5, T5.4.4; T5.2.2; then T5.5.1 -> T5.5.2; one chain because they share backlog_lifecycle_pr.go and the cleanup funcs); B = E8 (T8.1.6 first), then T4.1.2 (needs T5.1.2, T5.2.1 and T5.4.1 for the `pr_holds` fill) and T4.2.7 (needs T5.4.1) as A reaches them.
  W3: A = E6 (T6.3.3 first), then T8.2.2, then T6.3.4 (after T6.3.2 and T8.2.2), then T5.5.3 and T5.5.4; B = E9.   W4: E10, one worker.   W5: E11.
  Before every spawn the orchestrator runs `free -g` and `uptime` and refuses to spawn below 12 GiB available or above a load of cores/2 (tunable; memory `feedback_backlog_wip_limit`: two OOM incidents, 2026-07-12 and 2026-07-29). Each worker keeps `GOTMPDIR`, `GOCACHE` and scratch on DISK under `~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/scratch/<worker>`, never `/tmp` (a RAM tmpfs on this host; memory `instinct_tmp_is_tmpfs_ram_oom_restarts_service`), runs under `systemd-run --user -p MemoryMax=8G`, runs tests with `-p 2`, and never uses an unscoped `pkill` (memory `feedback_subagent_worktree_base_and_pkill`). Per-task briefs are generated from the dependency table plus the owning story's ACs (pre-mortem #11), and the table check (each task once, no prerequisite in a later wave, no cycle) is re-run before each wave.

Full task -> prerequisite table (with waves) and its cycle/back-edge check: "Task dependency graph (Phase 4)", after the Effort estimate.
