---

## Phase 1: Foundations (Wave 0 and 1)

### Epic 1: Foundations (domain, schema, flag, backfill)
**Goal**: A tested domain model, persisted `PullRequestState` + `SessionPR` entities, a live-settable flag, and an idempotent, non-resurrecting backfill, with no behavior change yet.

**Ordering inside E1 (repaired)**: Story 1.2 (types) and Story 1.1 (characterization only) run first and in parallel; Story 1.3 (schema/repository) follows; Task 1.3.3 (read first) precedes T1.3.1/T1.3.2 although it is listed after them; the headline red tests (Task 1.3.4) stay in this story for effort accounting but are scheduled at the head of wave 2 (they need E2's `Instance` PR snapshot, architecture B2/C2). Stories 1.4 and 1.5 follow 1.3, except that T1.5.2, T1.5.3 and T1.5.4 are scheduled in wave 1 after T2.3.3 (they need E2 code).

#### Story 1.1: Pin the legacy behavior with characterization tests
**As a** maintainer, **I want** characterization tests of today's behavior, **so that** the flag-off path is provably unchanged.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Flag-off behavior is pinned.
  - *Given* a session with `GitHubPRNumber=11` and `GitHubPRStatusTerminal=true` and the flag off, *When* the poller runs, *Then* it makes zero GitHub calls for that session (today's `pr_status_poller.go:318` skip).
**Files**: `session/pr_multi_legacy_test.go`

##### Task 1.1.1: Characterization tests of today's single-PR behavior (S)
- Poller terminal skip; `SetGitHubPR` overwrite leaves `GitHubPRStatusTerminal` stale; `scanAndLinkPRURL` link-once; `CreatePullRequest` existing-PR short-circuit. Use the existing injected-clock seams in `pr_status_poller_test.go`; no `time.Sleep`.
- Files: `session/pr_multi_legacy_test.go`, `server/services/pr_creation_service_test.go`

#### Story 1.2: Domain types and pure rules
**As a** developer, **I want** `PRRef`, the sum types and the pure `ActivePR` / `ItemPRVerdict` functions, **so that** every layer uses one definition.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- `ActivePR` follows the owner's order: pin, open PR on the current branch, newest non-terminal, newest, nil.
  - *Given* rows `#11 merged head=feat-a`, `#12 open head=feat-b`, `#13 open head=feat-c` and current branch `feat-c`, *When* `ActivePR` is called with no pin, *Then* it returns `#13`; with `#11` pinned it returns `#11`; with branch `main` and no pin it returns `#13` (newest non-terminal).
  - *Given* `#12 open` (older) and `#14 unknown` (newer, unreachable) and branch `main`, *When* `ActivePR` is called, *Then* it returns `#12` (an `unknown` PR never outranks an open one); *Given* only `#11 merged` and `#14 unknown`, *Then* `#14`.
  - *Given* a seeded `#14 unknown terminal=true` (older, verify exhausted) and `#13 merged` (newer, verified) and branch `main`, *When* `ActivePR` is called, *Then* it returns `#13` (ranking uses effective terminal then recency, never the `state` string alone); *Given* only `#14 unknown terminal=true` (verify exhausted) and `#9 closed`, *Then* the newer of the two by time; *Given* `#14 unknown terminal=true` NOT exhausted, *Then* it ranks as `unknown` non-terminal (above any terminal PR, below any `open`).
- `ItemPRVerdict` implements the done rule.
  - *Given* `#11 merged` and `#12 closed` (unmerged), *When* the verdict is computed, *Then* it is `Done`; *Given* only `#12 closed`, *Then* `AllClosedUnmerged`; *Given* `#11 merged` and `#13 open`, *Then* `Open`.
- Verdict runs over distinct PRs and `unknown` blocks.
  - *Given* two link views of the same `PRRef` (one from a session link, one from an item link) pointing at one `PullRequestState` `merged`, *When* the verdict is computed, *Then* the PR is counted once (`Done`, "1 of 1 merged"); *Given* `#11 merged` and `#13 unknown` (non-terminal), *Then* `Open` (unknown blocks); *Given* `#11 merged` and `#12 unknown terminal=true` (exhausted OR not), *Then* `Open` (an unverified row is held in the verdict: an unfetchable, possibly merged PR is never read as abandoned; adversarial M4); *Given* only `#12 unknown terminal=true` exhausted, *Then* `Open` (held, never `AllClosedUnmerged`). The cleanup gate and sweeper, which use `PRsAllTerminal`, DO treat the exhausted row as terminal.
- `PRsAllTerminal` never reads an empty set as terminal.
  - *Given* an empty slice, *When* `PRsAllTerminal(rows, legacyNumber)` is called with `legacyNumber=0`, *Then* it returns true only via the explicit `NoPRs` path used by callers that want it; *Given* an empty slice and `legacyNumber=21`, *Then* it returns false (a legacy PR with no link is treated as non-terminal).
- `PRRef` canonicalizes host, owner and repo.
  - *Given* host `""` and `"GitHub.com"`, *When* `NewPRRef` is called, *Then* both equal `github.com`; *Given* `ghe.acme.io`, *Then* it is kept lowercased and differs from `github.com`.
  - *Given* `NewPRRef("", "Acme", "App", 12)` and `NewPRRef("github.com", "acme", "app", 12)`, *Then* the two values are equal and both hold `acme`/`app`; a `PRRef` cannot be built with an upper-case owner or repo because its fields are unexported (the compiler rejects a literal outside `pr_set.go`; no guard test), and a raw ent create of a `PullRequestState` with owner `Acme` fails the column validator.
**Files**: `session/pr_set.go`, `session/pr_set_rules.go`, `session/pr_set_test.go`

##### Task 1.2.1: `PRRef`, `PRState`, `PRRole`, `AttachSource`, `PRView` (S)
- Newtype/sum types with parse functions and `Terminal()` (`PRState` includes `unknown`, which is not terminal). `NewPRRef` lowercases host (via `github.NormalizeHost`), owner and repo itself, mirroring `RepoRef.repoKey()`; it validates non-empty owner/repo like `NewRepoRefWithHost` but does NOT inherit its verbatim-case storage (`github/repo_ref.go:27-37`). Display case is not stored on the ref; the fields are unexported with accessors so no `PRRef{...}` literal compiles outside `pr_set.go`.
- Files: `session/pr_set.go`, `session/pr_set_test.go`

##### Task 1.2.2: `ActivePR` with table tests (S)
- Includes the stacked case (two open, branch matches neither) and the merged-only case.
- Files: `session/pr_set_rules.go`, `session/pr_set_test.go`

##### Task 1.2.3: `ItemPRVerdict` and `PRsAllTerminal` (S)
- Pure functions over `[]PRView` (row data, not a snapshot type, so the sweeper's `InstanceData` path can call them); distinct-by-state collapse (the schema guarantees one state row per canonical `PRRef`; the collapse is a defensive dedup by state id; no precedence rule is needed because there is one state value per state); `unknown` handling; unverified terminal rows (`unknown`, `terminal=true`) are `unknown` (held) in `ItemPRVerdict` always, and count as terminal in `PRsAllTerminal` only when `VerifyExhausted()`, never as merged; the empty-set rule above. Table tests for each verdict and the duplicate/unknown/unverified cases. Also in this task (Phase 4 pre-mortem #4): the pure `IsLiveWork(facts, now, window)` of the Glossary (the caller reads `facts` from `Snapshot()`: unended flag, status, `LastMeaningfulChangeAt`, `TurnEnded`); table tests with an injected clock: active inside the window is live, idle past the window is not, an ended session is not, a zero time is not. The same file defines the consumer-side interface `LiveWorkProbe{IsLive(ctx, sessionUUID, now) (bool, error)}` (nil-safe: a nil probe means no hold); the real implementation, which reads the live instance `Snapshot()`, is T5.2.1's. Maximum hold (Triad iteration 1, engineering 3): the same file defines the pure `LiveWorkHoldExpired(now, latestMergedAt, maxHold) bool` (true when `now - latestMergedAt >= maxHold`; zero `latestMergedAt` is never expired) with table tests; the hold consumer (T5.2.1) stops holding once it is true.
- Files: `session/pr_set_rules.go`, `session/pr_set_test.go`

#### Story 1.3: `PullRequestState` and `SessionPR` entities, repository, headline tests
**As a** poller author, **I want** one state row per PR, link rows from sessions and items, and a one-query bulk load, **so that** a PR is polled once, state never diverges, list RPC latency does not regress and attach is safe to retry.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Idempotent upsert.
  - *Given* empty tables, *When* `UpsertSessionPR` is called twice with `PRRef{github.com,acme,app,12}` for `session_uuid=u1`, *Then* exactly one `PullRequestState` row and one link row exist and `attached_at` is unchanged on the second call.
- Same PR on two sessions: one state row, two links.
  - *Given* `u1` and `u2` both attach `acme/app#12`, *When* links are listed by `[u1,u2]`, *Then* two link rows return, both joined to the same `PullRequestState` id, from at most `2 * ceil(n/500)` SQL statements (chunked by the existing `itemSessionBatchChunk`, `session/storage_backlog.go:357`; 2 for n=2); updating the state once changes the view for both.
- One PR cannot exist as several state rows.
  - *Given* an existing state row for `acme/app#12`, *When* a session link, an item link and a Jules link for #12 are upserted, *Then* `PullRequestState` still has 1 row and `session_pr` has 3.
- Bounded read.
  - *Given* 200 sessions with 3 PRs each, *When* `ListBySessionUUIDs` runs, *Then* the query count recorded by the test driver hook is at most `2 * ceil(200/500)` = 2 (links, then states by id IN, each chunked at 500); with 1,001 uuids it is at most 6.
- Identity is case-insensitive on every path.
  - *Given* `Acme/App#12` and `acme/app#12` upserted through the repository (wave 0 reaches only the repository, `NewPRRef`, tombstone write/check and `ErrZeroPRRef`), *When* both are upserted, *Then* exactly one `PullRequestState` row exists and a tombstone written for `Acme/App#12` blocks an `acme/app#12` upsert. Each later entry point carries its OWN case-insensitivity AC in the task that creates it: backfill T1.5.1, `AttachSessionPR` T2.3.1, terminal scan T2.2.1, discovery T3.3.1, webhook `InvalidateAndRefresh` T3.3.2, `ListSessions` `pr` filter T4.2.6, `FindSessionsByPullRequest` T9.2.1; none is tested in wave 0 (architecture B2).
- Tombstone.
  - *Given* a detach of `acme/app#12` on `u1`, *When* `UpsertSessionPR` for the same pair runs from `branch_discovery` or `backfill`, *Then* no link is created (the detach here is the repository `DeleteSessionPR`, which the RPC `DetachSessionPullRequest` wraps in T4.2.3); `ClearTombstone(owner, ref)` (T1.3.2) followed by `UpsertSessionPR` with `attach_source=manual` clears the tombstone and creates the link. The RPC-level `AttachSessionPullRequest` assertion lives in T4.2.3 (wave 2), not in this wave-0 story (Phase 4 B3).
- `UpdatePullRequestState` is monotonic (test written first).
  - *Given* a row `merged` (`last_polled_at=T2`), *When* an older fetch (`observed_at=T1<T2`) reporting `open` is applied, *Then* the row stays `merged`; *Given* two overlapping fetches `observed_at=T1` (`open`) and `T3` (`merged`) landing in either order, *Then* the row ends `merged`; *Given* a `closed` row and a TICK observation `open` with a later `observed_at`, *Then* it is rejected (sticky), *and* the same observation with `source=webhook_reopened` is accepted; *Given* a `closed` row and a later `merged` observation (reconciler refresh or webhook), *Then* it is accepted (closed-then-merged correction, as today's live `IsPRMerged` check); *Given* a `merged` row and any later observation, *Then* rejected; *Given* a row whose `last_polled_at` is in the future (clock stepped back), *When* an observation at the injected `now` arrives, *Then* it is applied (clamp) and a drop counter is not incremented; *Given* an unverified terminal row (`unknown`, `terminal=true`), *When* any real state is observed, *Then* it resolves; *Given* `PRRef{}`, *Then* every entry point returns `ErrZeroPRRef`.
**Files**: `session/ent/schema/pull_request_state.go`, `session/ent/schema/session_pr.go`, `session/ent/schema/session_pr_tombstone.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr_test.go`, `session/ent/schema/migration_test.go`

##### Task 1.3.1: Ent schemas (M)
- `PullRequestState`: `host`, `owner`, `repo` (all stored canonical lowercase via `NewPRRef`, with an ent validator rejecting upper-case; `url` keeps original case for display), `number`, `url`, `head_branch`, `base_branch`, `state`, `is_draft`, `check_conclusion`, `approved_count`, `changes_req_count`, `terminal`, `opened_at`, `merged_at`, `closed_at`, `created_at` (immutable; the age basis of the orphan GC), `last_checked_at`, `last_polled_at` (the applied `observed_at`, not completion time), `poll_fail_count`, `first_fail_at` (nullable; start of the current counted-failure streak, so "5 failures spanning >= 30 minutes" is checkable; the one column this iteration adds, because the span clause had no stored basis), `poll_error` (text holding the enum `none|auth|rate_limited|not_found|repo_inaccessible|network`, validator-checked), `rate_limit_reset_at`. Unique index `(host, owner, repo, number)`; index `(terminal)` only if T3.2.1 shows a query that needs it. No `outcome_unverified` column: "unverified terminal" is `state=unknown` with `terminal=true`.
- `SessionPR` (link): `pull_request_state_id` (edge/FK), `session_uuid`, `backlog_item_id` (loose strings, default ""; an ent validator requires EXACTLY ONE non-empty), `role`, `pinned`, `attach_source`, `attached_at`. Unique index `(pull_request_state_id, session_uuid, backlog_item_id)`; indexes `session_uuid`, `backlog_item_id`, `pull_request_state_id`. A partial unique constraint (or transactional check) allows at most one `role=primary` per non-empty `session_uuid`. No `abandoned`, no `origin_session_uuid`.
- `SessionPRTombstone`: `session_uuid`, `backlog_item_id` (loose strings, default ""; exactly one non-empty), `host`, `owner`, `repo`, `number` (canonical form, same lower-case validator), `created_at`; unique on all six.
- Run the generate command with `--feature sql/upsert`; do not `git add` generated output.
- Files: `session/ent/schema/pull_request_state.go`, `session/ent/schema/session_pr.go`, `session/ent/schema/session_pr_tombstone.go`, `session/ent/schema/migration_test.go`

##### Task 1.3.2: Repository methods (M)
- Every repository entry point takes a `PRRef` built by `NewPRRef` (the ent columns are never populated from raw strings). `EnsureState(ref, initial)` (insert-if-absent, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; the only state-row write available to attach, backfill and full saves), `UpsertSessionPR` (`EnsureState` then the link in one transaction; `OnConflictColumns`, template `ent_repository_backlog.go:633`; consults the owner's tombstones), `ListBySessionUUIDs` (links joined to states), `ListByItem` (item-direct links joined to states), `ListSessionTombstonesByUUIDs(uuids)` and `ListItemTombstones(itemIDs)` (the bulk tombstone reads T1.5.1, T4.3.0 and T5.1.2 need; every `uuid IN` / `item_id IN` read in this task chunks by the existing `itemSessionBatchChunk` (500, `session/storage_backlog.go:357`), so the bound is `2 * ceil(n/500)` statements for link+state reads and `ceil(n/500)` for tombstones), `ListPollableStates` (the poller's work list: every state with at least one link that is non-terminal, plus unverified terminal states (`unknown`, `terminal=true`) that are not `VerifyExhausted()` (`poll_fail_count < 5` or streak shorter than 30 minutes), item-only states included; ORDER: non-terminal states first, then unverified terminal ones, each by newest `attached_at`), `UpdatePullRequestState(ref, observation)` (the ONLY writer of state columns after insert, including terminal/unknown transitions; invariant I1; MONOTONIC, architecture C2 / adversarial C5). `observation` carries `observed_at` (the injected clock at fetch START) and a `source`. One conditional UPDATE enforces: (a) compare-and-set on time: applied only when `observed_at >= last_polled_at` OR `last_polled_at` is later than the injected `now` (clamp: a stepped-back clock or a future value never freezes the row, adversarial M2). `last_polled_at` stores the APPLIED `observed_at` (fetch start), never the completion time, so a fetch that started later but finished later is not dropped (architecture C5a). The loser is dropped, counted in `ssq_pr_state_observation_dropped_total{reason=stale|sticky|source}` and logged at debug, with a warn after 3 consecutive drops of one row; (b) terminal is STICKY except for the edges of the TRANSITION TABLE below (the single rule; (c) names its two non-obvious entries): a stored `merged`/`closed` is never rewritten to `open`/`unknown` otherwise; (c) the exceptions are an unverified terminal row (`unknown`+`terminal=true`), which may resolve to any real state, and a REOPEN proof: an observation with `source=webhook_reopened` (the `pull_request.reopened` event path of `InvalidateAndRefresh`; the reconciler's closed-row refresh also fetches a terminal row but is limited to `closed -> merged` by the table below) AND `observed_at` strictly later than the row's `last_polled_at` AND a successful fetch showing `open` (a plain `closed->open` seen by a tick can only come from a stale response and is rejected); (d) failure bookkeeping obeys the same time check: only COUNTED failures (T3.2.4) write `poll_fail_count` and `first_fail_at`; a non-counted failure writes neither those columns nor `last_polled_at`, so it neither breaks nor extends the streak, but it DOES write the `poll_error` enum (observation kind `error_note`, same time check; Phase 4 C8, so the UI can say why a row is stale) and any successful observation clears `poll_error` (`VerifyExhausted` = count >= 5 AND `last_polled_at - first_fail_at >= 30 minutes`, i.e. first to LAST counted failure, never to now). TRANSITION TABLE (architecture C5), `observation.source` in {`tick`, `reconciler_refresh`, `webhook`, `webhook_reopened`}: `open -> open|merged|closed` allowed; `closed -> merged` allowed from any source with a later `observed_at` (closed-then-merged correction); `closed -> open` ONLY `webhook_reopened`; `merged -> anything` rejected (sticky); `unknown (terminal=false) -> any real state` allowed; `unknown+terminal=true (unverified) -> any real state` allowed; `open -> unknown` ONLY via the 5-counted-failure rule; terminal rows never move to `unknown`. The reconciler refresh fetches a `closed` row ONCE per `ReconcilePRPending` pass for a pr_pending item whose verdict would otherwise be `AllClosedUnmerged` (a conditional GET, a 304 costs no quota), so the plan does not regress today's closed-then-merged detection; it is AWAITED by the pass (T3.3.2, T5.2.4) and applies only the `closed -> merged` edge (a refresh that sees `open` is dropped, reopen stays webhook-only, Known Risk K1). Add `ResetVerifyCounters()` to this file: sets `poll_fail_count=0`, `first_fail_at=NULL`, `poll_error=''` WHERE `state='unknown' AND terminal=true`; called only by `Storage.RunPRStartupMaintenance` (T1.5.1, service bootstrap only via `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance`; never from `NewEntRepository` or `buildMCPDeps`) and listed in the T2.3.4 caller allow-list. Tests: merged-then-older-open interleaving stays merged; two concurrent fetches write the newer one; reopen via webhook accepted, via tick rejected; unverified terminal resolves to `open`; one test per row of the transition table; closed then reconciler-refresh `merged` accepted, refresh `open` dropped; future `last_polled_at` clamp; a non-counted `error_note` observation writes only `poll_error` (the "a 5xx between two counted failures leaves the streak intact" test moves to T3.2.4, where the classifier exists; architecture N3); `ResetVerifyCounters` touches only unverified-terminal rows. A reopened PR that no webhook reports stays terminal, the same as legacy behavior (iteration 3 minor M3, accepted), `SetPinned` (transaction clears other pins), `DeleteSessionPR` (detach: deletes exactly that owner's link and writes that owner's tombstone in ONE transaction; session and item variants), `ClearTombstone(owner, ref)` (removes one owner's tombstone: the repository half of a user attach, so wave-0 tests need no RPC). In-memory SQLite tests. `EnsureState`'s `initial` is the state the caller actually observed (discovery list call, verified report) or `unknown`, `terminal=false` when it observed nothing; a full save from a snapshot ALWAYS passes `unknown`, `terminal=false` (it observed nothing, so a row recreated after the nightly GC never inherits a stale `PRView.State` such as `merged`; adversarial M6). Every entry point rejects a zero `PRRef` with `ErrZeroPRRef` (T1.3.6). Cap 50 links per session enforced in the attach path.
- Files: `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr_test.go`

#### Story 1.4: The `session_multi_pr` flag
**As an** operator, **I want** one live-settable flag with per-reader scopes, **so that** I can flip the poller, backlog, actions and UI independently.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Flag round-trips through the feature-flag RPC.
  - *Given* the service started with defaults, *When* `UpdateFeatureFlag{name:"session_multi_pr", scope:"reader:poller", enabled:true}` is sent, *Then* `config.SessionMultiPR("reader:poller")` returns true and `"reader:ui"` returns false.
- Config surface proof passes.
  - *Given* the new `flag:session_multi_pr` entry, *When* `go test ./server/services -run TestConfigSurfaceProof` runs, *Then* it passes and fails if the registry entry is removed.
- Scopes depend on each other IN ORDER, applied when the flag is read (the readers depend on each other: the UI list is meaningless if the poller never advances state; the sweeper and actions rely on poller-derived terminal). Verified semantics (Triad iteration 2, read in `server/services/feature_flag_mutation.go`, `feature_flag_service.go` and `config/config.go:2125-2140`): `parseFlagOp` validates only the scope NAME against the registry; the only write-time refusal hook is `SetEnableGuard(name, guard func() string)`, which takes no scope argument; and the existing precedent for ordered scopes (`askuserquestion_structured`, `resolveStage`) applies the order at READ time. This flag follows that precedent; no new write-time machinery.
  - *Given* all scopes off, *When* `UpdateFeatureFlag{scope:"reader:ui", mutation:SET_ENABLED}` is sent, *Then* the call succeeds and the value is stored, but `config.SessionMultiPR("reader:ui")` stays false until `reader:poller`, `reader:backlog` and `reader:actions` are effective; the flag panel's readback shows the stored value and the runbook says to enable in order. *Given* the scopes enabled in the order poller, backlog, actions, ui, *Then* each resolves true in turn. *Given* `reader:poller` is then disabled with the later scopes still set, *Then* every later scope resolves false immediately (the safe direction) and re-enabling the poller restores them.
- The flag also registers `artifact_scan_v2` (default off), proven in the same config-surface test (used by T8.1.6).
- The `refresher_off` kill-switch scope (Phase 4 pre-mortem #3; renamed in Triad iteration 2). VERIFIED: a scope resolves as scoped override, else the explicit global value, else the flag's ONE `defaultValue` (`GetFeatureFlagScoped`, `config/config.go:2135`), and `knownFeatureFlags` holds a single `defaultValue` per flag, so a default-ON scope under a default-off flag cannot be expressed (a scope named plainly `refresher` with no stored override would resolve OFF, and would turn ON whenever the global flag is turned on). The smallest representation is an inverse-named scope read as an EXPLICIT override only.
  - *Given* the service started with defaults, *Then* `config.SessionMultiPRRefresher()` is true (it is `!override("refresher_off")` via `GetFeatureFlagScopedOverride`, never `GetFeatureFlagScoped`, so neither a global-on nor a reader scope changes it); *When* `UpdateFeatureFlag{name:"session_multi_pr", scope:"refresher_off", mutation:SET_ENABLED}` is sent, *Then* it returns false with no other scope enabled or required; `CLEAR_SCOPE` restores true; turning the global flag on or off leaves it unchanged; and the in-order reader scopes are unaffected (the scope is not in the order).
**Files**: `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, `config/session_multi_pr_flag.go`, `server/services/config_surface_proof_test.go`

Also the kill-switch scope `refresher_off` (registered in the scopes list; default off because it is read as an explicit override only), read by `config.SessionMultiPRRefresher()` as `!override("refresher_off")`; a stored `true` is the live kill switch for the state refresher (T3.2.5). Size stays S: the read-time helper replaces the write-time validation the earlier text implied, which is the larger option.

#### Story 1.5: Backfill and drift reconciler
**As an** owner, **I want** existing single-PR sessions and items to appear as one `SessionPR` row with no data loss, **so that** the flag can be flipped on real data.
**Wave split (architecture B1)**: T1.5.1 (backfill, wave 0) needs only E1 repository queries, so the ACs that exercise only the migration pass (seed table, merge, skip, empty uuid, no-resurrect, items, candidate rule, transient-vs-deterministic errors) are verified in wave 0. The ACs marked **[wave 1]** load instances, run a full save or run the reconciler (they need the actor setter, the `ActivePR` snapshot projection, the hydrate and the clearable full save of E2) and are verified by T1.5.2 / T1.5.3 after T2.3.3. ACs marked **[wave 2]** need the poller, sweeper or gate helpers (T3.2.1, T4.3.0) and are asserted in T3.2.1 / T4.3.1 / T5.4.1. Everything in this story runs through `Storage.RunPRStartupMaintenance` (service bootstrap, gated by `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance`), not `NewEntRepository` and not `buildMCPDeps` (architecture C1, Phase 4 B1).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Backfill creates the primary link and seeds state from what is actually stored.
  - *Given* a stored session `refactor-auth` with `github_pr_number=11`, `github_owner=Acme`, `github_repo=App`, `github_host=""`, `github_pr_status_terminal=true` (the only stored status; there is no merged/closed value), *When* the migration runs, *Then* one state row `acme/app#11 state=unknown terminal=true` (unverified terminal) and one link `role=primary attach_source=backfill` exist, and a second run creates none (**[wave 2]** the gate and sweeper treating it as NON-terminal until its verify is exhausted or a poll resolves it is asserted in T4.3.1, T5.4.1 and T3.2.4; the "Closed or merged (unverified)" label in T4.1.2 and T6.1.3); **[wave 2, T3.2.1]** after one poll tick finds it merged, the row reads `state=merged` and is never polled again.
  - *Given* the same session with `github_pr_status_terminal=false`, *Then* the seed is `state=unknown terminal=false` (never `open`).
- Two candidates for one PR merge fail-closed, in either order.
  - *Given* a terminal session and a `pr_pending` item that both reference `acme/app#21`, *When* the pass runs (session first, then item first), *Then* the one state row is `unknown terminal=false` both times; *Given* a terminal session and a `done` item for the same PR, *Then* it is unverified terminal; *Given* the row already exists, *Then* a later top-up changes no state column.
- Unrecoverable host is skipped, not guessed, and stays protected.
  - *Given* a session with number 9 and NO owner/repo (so no `PRRef` can be built; an empty `github_host` alone is github.com and is not a skip, `session.go:153-155`), *When* the migration runs, *Then* no link is created, the skipped count is 1, and the retention sweeper / cleanup gate still treat the session as NOT safe to delete (tests in T4.3.1 and T5.4.1).
- Empty uuid.
  - *Given* two legacy sessions with `uuid=""` on the same PR, *When* the migration runs, *Then* no unique-index collision occurs, each is either assigned a uuid first or counted in `skipped_no_uuid` and logged by title.
- Does not resurrect or duplicate.
  - *Given* a user detached `acme/app#11` from a session (tombstone present) while the legacy columns still carry 11, *When* the process restarts, *Then* no link is recreated; *Given* a session that already has a link representing its legacy number, *Then* the backfill does not touch it and no second `primary` exists; *Given* the pass is run twice, *Then* the second run creates nothing (idempotent without a marker).
- **[wave 1]** Rollback and re-deploy do not lose or overwrite PRs, including through load and save.
  - *Given* a session with links (`#11` primary) and the binary is reverted, the old binary records `#12` only in the legacy columns (`github_pr_number=12`, no link), and the new binary starts, *When* the boot backfill runs (before `LoadInstances`), *Then* `#12` is imported as a link (`attach_source=backfill`, role `followup` because the session already has a `primary`, so the one-primary index cannot reject it and startup does not abort; unless tombstoned); *When* `LoadInstances` builds the snapshot and a full save then runs, *Then* the stored legacy columns read `ActivePR` of {#11, #12} and #12 is never lost; the drift counter is not incremented for this import.
  - *Given* the legacy number is tombstoned and no link represents it, *Then* it is not imported and the reconciler overwrites it with the `ActivePR` projection (no stuck state).
- Items get state from the item status without guessing an outcome.
  - *Given* a `done` item with `pr_number=21`, *When* the migration runs, *Then* an item-direct link exists and the state row is unverified terminal (resolved once by the poller); *Given* a `pr_pending` item, *Then* `unknown terminal=false`; *Given* an `archived` item with `pr_number=30`, *Then* `unknown terminal=false` (resolved later by the poller; never a guessed `open` or `merged`).
- Backfill failure never loses a PR, never boot-loops, and its read cost is bounded (adversarial C6, C1; architecture C3).
  - *Given* an injected TRANSIENT write failure (SQLite busy, commit error) on 4 consecutive attempts while importing `#12` for a session that already has links, *When* `RunPRStartupMaintenance` runs with an injected sleeper, *Then* it retries 3 times, logs, increments `ssq_pr_maintenance_failed_total` and returns nil (the service starts), no link, state or mirror change from that candidate persists, the gate and sweeper still fail closed for the unimported number, a full save leaves the session's legacy columns untouched (`LegacyPRUnrepresented`, **[wave 1]**), and unverified-terminal rows read as non-terminal (`VerifyExhausted` forced false until a pass has succeeded in this process); *When* the failure is removed, *Then* the 5-minute ticker's next pass imports `#12` and stops. A database that cannot be opened still aborts startup (existing behavior, before this step). *Given* a POISONED row (a session whose owner fails the ent validator or whose attach would exceed the 50-link cap, and an item with `pr_number=5` and an empty `pr_url`) next to a healthy candidate, *When* `RunPRStartupMaintenance` runs, *Then* it returns nil (startup proceeds), the healthy candidate is imported, the poisoned rows are rolled back and counted (`skipped_invalid=1`, `skipped_item_bad_ref=1`) with a log of the count and the first 10 titles, and a second boot behaves identically (no loop); an unrecognized error type is retried and then continues booting like any transient error. *Given* an item with `pr_number=7` and `pr_url` `https://ghe.acme.io/acme/app/pull/7`, *Then* the ref is `ghe.acme.io/acme/app#7`; with `pr_url` for #8 but `pr_number=7` it is the skip class. **[wave 1]** *Given* a skipped session whose legacy number is unrepresented and untombstoned (no owner/repo), *When* a full save and the reconciler run, *Then* `github_pr_number`/`github_pr_url` are unchanged (the `Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented` flag survives load -> save -> load, asserted through two consecutive full saves) and the skip is counted, not drift. *Given* the skipped item (`skipped_item_bad_ref`) in `done`, *Then* the cleanup gate and sweeper step 4 report it NOT safe (tests in T4.3.1/T5.4.1). *Given* 10,000 sessions of which 40 have `github_pr_number > 0`, *Then* the pass reads only those 40 rows' needed columns (query-count hook: one candidate query, one links query) and the skipped log is a count plus at most 10 titles.
- Only the service path does PR maintenance (architecture C1, Phase 4 B1, B4). *Given* unverified-terminal rows with `poll_fail_count=3` and a second `NewEntRepository` call on the same database (as `ssq-hooks` and the CLI subcommands make) while the SQLite lock is held beyond the busy timeout, *Then* that call neither changes a counter nor imports a link nor returns a maintenance error; *Given* `buildMCPDeps` (the plain `BuildCoreDeps()` with `RunPRMaintenance` false, the stdio MCP fallback) against the same database, *Then* it leaves counters and links untouched, issues none of the candidate queries (query-count hook) and cannot fail MCP init on a busy database; *Given* the service `core-deps` phase and `BuildDependencies` (`RunPRMaintenance` true) with a recording fake, *Then* `RunPRStartupMaintenance` runs exactly once, after `Storage` exists and before the poller, sweeper and cleanup ticker are constructed (wave 0 asserts only what exists today; a sweeper built afterwards sees `poll_fail_count=0` on every unverified-terminal row). **[wave 2, T3.2.5]** The refresher is constructed after `RunPRStartupMaintenance`, so it never sees pre-reset counters.
- The item pass is bounded (architecture C7). *Given* 5,000 `done` items with `pr_number>0`, all already represented by an item-direct link, *When* the pass runs, *Then* it issues no write and its read statements stay within `c * ceil(5000/500)` per query kind (query-count hook, the constant `c` fixed in the test); *Given* 2,500 unrepresented items, *Then* `maxBackfillWritesPerBoot` (2,000) are imported, `deferred=500` is logged, the 500 stay NOT safe in the gate and sweeper (an unrepresented legacy number), and the next run imports them.
- Item candidate never re-creates a session-derived or detached PR (architecture N3, C1).
  - *Given* a `pr_pending` item with `pr_number=30` and #30 linked only to its latest work session, *When* the pass runs twice, *Then* no item-direct link exists; *Given* the item's only reference to #30 is its legacy `pr_number` (pre-feature or rollback), *Then* one item-direct link (`attach_source=backfill`) is created once. *Given* #30 reachable only through the current round's session link and then the repository `DeleteSessionPR` (session variant, T1.3.2: a SESSION tombstone, no item tombstone; the RPC `DetachSessionPullRequest` that wraps it is asserted in T4.2.3), `pr_number=30` still stored, *When* the backfill runs (any later boot), *Then* no item-direct link is created for #30. (The set-level consequence is the Story 5.1 AC.)
- A stale terminal flag does not make a new PR look safe (adversarial C7), including for sessions with no link (adversarial C2).
  - *Given* a session whose `github_pr_number` was changed to open #B by `scanAndLinkPRURL` while `github_pr_status_terminal=true` stayed set, *When* the backfill seeds it, *Then* the row is `unknown`, `terminal=true` and is treated as NON-terminal by the gate and sweeper until verify is exhausted **[wave 2, asserted in T4.3.1/T5.4.1]**; after one poll that finds #B open the row is `open terminal=false` **[wave 2, T3.2.1]**. **[wave 1, T2.2.1]** *Given* the legacy writer in `scanAndLinkPRURL` (before it is routed through `AttachPR`), *Then* it clears `GitHubPRStatusTerminal` whenever it changes the number AND persists the clear immediately through the existing title-keyed `UpdateGitHubPRStatusTerminal` (`ent_repository.go:1156`, which works for any session, link or not). **[wave 1]** *Given* a fork or empty-uuid session (no link possible) that gets a new number B while the stored flag is `true`, *When* the process restarts through the real save and `LoadInstances` path, *Then* the reloaded flag is `false`, the legacy poller polls B, **[wave 2, T4.3.0/T4.3.1]** and `LegacyOnlyPRSafe` treats B as not safe while it is open.
- **[wave 1]** Drift reconciler.
  - *Given* a session whose legacy `github_pr_number=11` while `ActivePR` is 12, *When* the reconciler runs (no scope needs to be on), *Then* `ssq_session_pr_mirror_drift_total` increments by 1 and the legacy columns are corrected to 12 through the actor setter, not by a direct storage write.
- **[wave 1, T1.5.4]** Rehearsal on a copy of the production database (pre-mortem #1, #6, #7, #15).
  - *Given* a `sqlite3 .backup` copy of `~/.stapler-squad/workspaces/d685c4b1a423cca3/sessions.db` on DISK (never tmpfs), *When* the opt-in harness (`SSQ_REHEARSAL_DB` set, skipped otherwise) runs `RunPRStartupMaintenance`, the bulk load and the sweeper/gate evaluation against it with a recording `WorktreeCleaner` and session deleter that remove nothing, *Then* maintenance finishes well inside the 600 s start-limit window; with an injected busy fault on its first 4 attempts it still returns nil; the backfill counts (about 560 links, 2 sessions `skipped_invalid`; recorded in the PR, not asserted as constants) and the synthesized URLs for the rows with an empty `github_pr_url` are recorded; `ListSessions` p99 with PR views is within +5 ms of the pre-change p99 on the same copy; `ssq_db_pool_wait` before and after is recorded. The harness also starts and stops the maintenance retry ticker on the copy (Triad iteration 2, pre-mortem P1 #1): with an injected failing first pass it asserts the ticker runs, then cancels the context and asserts with `goleak.VerifyNone` (`IgnoreCurrent`) that no goroutine remains, and that a success stops it; the ticker is started through its own entry point, not through `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions`. A full manual boot on the copy is forbidden: its rows point at the LIVE worktree paths, so the retention sweeper and cleanup tickers would act on them. What the harness cannot show (the real dependency wiring and ticker start in a booted server) is Known Risk K13, with a manual first-deploy rehearsal step in the runbook (T11.1.1). The live database is read once, by the backup, and never written.
**Files**: `session/session_pr_backfill.go`, `session/storage.go`, `server/dependencies.go`, `session/session_pr_backfill_test.go`

##### Task 1.5.1: Backfill migration (M)
- Pattern: `session/github_pr_url_backfill.go` (shape only; NOT registered next to it); `[Migration]` log prefix; returns nil if the table is missing. Error handling is NOT best-effort-and-swallow (the template's behavior is deliberately not copied): transient errors are retried and then swallowed so the service keeps booting (Phase 4 pre-mortem #1), deterministic per-row failures are a counted skip class (Migration Plan, "Failure handling"). It is exposed as `Storage.RunPRStartupMaintenance(ctx)` (the import, then `ResetVerifyCounters()` from T1.3.2) and called from `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions` (`server/dependencies.go`, after `storage := sessionService.GetStorage()`) ONLY when the new `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance` is true, set at the `core-deps` phase (`main.go:384`) and in `BuildDependencies`; the plain `BuildCoreDeps()` of `buildMCPDeps` (`main.go:1871`) leaves it false. It runs before `BuildServiceDeps` builds the poller, sweeper and cleanup tickers (the refresher, T3.2.5, is built later still; its ordering clause is asserted there), so exhausted rows are never read as terminal before the reset (ordering AC in Story 1.5). Transient errors are retried 3 times with backoff, then logged, counted and swallowed so the service keeps booting; a 5-minute ticker re-runs the pass until one succeeds, and until then unverified-terminal rows are never read as exhausted (Migration Plan, "Failure handling"). It is NOT registered in `startupMigrations` (architecture C1: those run in every `NewEntRepository` caller, including hook and CLI processes). Rules in the Migration Plan: NO run-once marker; candidates are sessions whose legacy `PRRef` is not represented by one of their own links (never-linked AND rollback case) plus items with an UNREPRESENTED LEGACY NUMBER (`pr_number > 0`, ref built by the item URL parser of the Migration Plan, not represented by any link of the item or of any of its sessions, any round, and not carrying a tombstone of the item or of any of its sessions; helper exported for T5.1.2; architecture N3, C1); transient errors are retried 3 times and then swallowed (the service keeps booting; Phase 4 pre-mortem #1), deterministic per-row failures and classified skips are counted and the pass continues; reads are chunked and writes capped (`maxBackfillWritesPerBoot`, Migration Plan); the item helpers `ItemHasUnrepresentedLegacyPR` and `PRCoveredByTombstone(item, sessions)` are exported here for T4.3.0/T5.1.2/T5.2.3 (architecture C5); column-narrow candidate query (Migration Plan); tombstone check on canonical refs; at-most-one primary (an import into a session that already has a primary is role `followup`); per-pass `map[PRRef]seed` with non-terminal-wins merging; seeds written by `EnsureState` only (never updates an existing row); empty-uuid handling (look for the existing uuid-assignment path first). Canonical-identity test (moved from T1.3.6, B2): a session with owner `Acme`/repo `App` and an item with `pr_url` `https://github.com/Acme/App/pull/12` resolve to ONE `PullRequestState` row `acme/app#12`, and a tombstone written for `Acme/App#12` blocks the import. Seeded state rows whose stored URL is empty get `url` synthesized as `https://{host}/{owner}/{repo}/pull/{n}` at seed time (display case may differ; a poll corrects it; pre-mortem #15), so chips are never link-less after the first deploy. The `BuildOptions` field and its two call sites are part of this task, with the fake-recorded ordering test.
- Ticker lifecycle (Triad iteration 1, engineering 2; BUG-084 class): the 5-minute retry ticker is started from `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions`, which integration tests also call, so it is bound to a context that is cancelled by the server's stop path (the same shutdown hook that stops the other tickers; no `context.Background()` and no `time.Tick`), stops itself after the first success, and its goroutine exits before the stop function returns. AC: a test starts maintenance with a failing fake, asserts the ticker runs, calls the server stop and asserts with `goleak.VerifyNone` (scoped to this ticker's goroutine with `goleak.IgnoreCurrent()` taken before start, to avoid the BUG-084 cross-test tmux false positives, `docs/bugs/fixed/BUG-084-server-shutdown-goleak-false-positive-cross-test-tmux-goroutines.md`) that no goroutine remains; a second test builds dependencies with `RunPRMaintenance=false` and asserts no ticker starts.
- Files: `session/session_pr_backfill.go`, `session/storage.go`, `server/dependencies.go`, `main.go`, `session/session_pr_backfill_test.go`

##### Task 1.5.3: Rollback and re-deploy test (S, wave 1, after T2.3.3)
- Simulated revert: write a legacy-only PR to a session that already has links (as the old binary would), then run the real sequence `NewEntRepository` -> `RunPRStartupMaintenance` -> `LoadInstances` -> full save (the order the service bootstrap uses: `BuildCoreDepsWithOptions` with `RunPRMaintenance` true, then `BuildRuntimeDeps`), and assert the Story 1.5 rollback AC (import before projection, no overwrite, no marker needed). Variants: tombstoned legacy value is overwritten by the projection; flag-off.
- Files: `session/session_pr_backfill_test.go`, `session/session_pr_mirror_reconciler_test.go`

##### Task 1.5.2: Mirror drift reconciler (XS, wave 1, after T2.3.3)
- Needs T2.1.1/T2.1.2 (actor setter, `ActivePR` projection), T2.3.2 (`Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented`) and T2.3.3 (clearable full save); it is NOT wave 0 (architecture B1). Runs at startup and on a slow ticker, unconditionally. Per session: compare the legacy columns with the `ActivePR` projection; on difference correct them by calling an actor setter (so it serializes with the poller and `AttachPR`), persist from the snapshot at persist time, increment the metric and log. No import step and no direction rule (the boot backfill imports). A TOMBSTONED legacy value is drift and is overwritten; an unrepresented, untombstoned one (`Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented`, a skipped backfill class) is NOT overwritten: the session is skipped and counted (`ssq_session_pr_mirror_drift_total{kind="unrepresented_skipped"}` as a label on the existing counter). No revival or Start calls (noarchivedrevival not triggered). Add a `-race` test that runs direct `ApplyPRUpdate` calls (the wave-2 poll core does not exist yet; T3.2.2 adds the poll-core variant), concurrent `AttachPR` and the reconciler together and asserts the final legacy columns equal `ActivePR` of the final snapshot (an older snapshot never overwrites a newer one).
- Files: `session/session_pr_mirror_reconciler.go`, `session/session_pr_mirror_reconciler_test.go`

##### Task 1.5.4: Boot rehearsal on a copy of the production database (S, wave 1, after T1.5.3)

Evidence task, no live-database write: take the copy with `sqlite3 <db> ".backup <dest>"` (a read of the live file only; never `cp` of a WAL database, never tmpfs, short disk path under `~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/rehearsal/`). Add an opt-in harness `session/session_pr_rehearsal_test.go` (skipped unless `SSQ_REHEARSAL_DB` is set) that opens the copy through `NewEntRepository`, runs `RunPRStartupMaintenance` (clean, then with an injected busy fault on the first 4 attempts), the maintenance retry ticker (started with a failing fake, stopped by context cancel, `goleak`-checked; Story 1.5 rehearsal AC), the bulk load, one sweeper cycle and the gate evaluation with a recording `WorktreeCleaner` and session deleter that remove nothing, and benchmarks `ListSessions` with PR views loaded before and after the change (budget +5 ms p99; record `ssq_db_pool_wait`). Do NOT boot a full manual instance on the copy: its rows point at the live worktree paths and the sweeper and cleanup tickers would delete them. Also run the BASELINE queries on the same copy (Triad iteration 2, product j; read-only, never the live database): (1) the number of sessions whose `session_artifacts` PR URLs hold more than one distinct PR URL (queries as in `research/artifacts.md` section 2), as the PR-history baseline; (2) the number of backlog items with status `done` and a GitHub issue `external_url`, and for the 50 most recent of them the live issue state through read-only `gh issue view --json state` calls (GitHub is read, nothing is written), as the issue-close baseline (how many finished items still have an open issue). The numbers go into the PR description and into requirements.md under "Baseline (PR side and issue close)"; they are the denominators for the adoption keep/cut rule (Owner confirmations pending #12). Record in the PR and in the runbook (T11.1.1): backfill counts, maintenance duration, the synthesized-URL count, the expected post-boot numbers (about 560 rows, about 28 cold-drain ticks at 20 per tick, a blocked-items gauge near 300 that falls), and the p99 delta. Prerequisites: T1.5.1, T1.5.3.

### Epic 2: Instance writes, snapshot and legacy mirror
**Goal**: One actor setter writes PRs and the legacy mirror; all nine legacy writers go through it; new direct writes fail CI.

#### Story 2.1: `AttachPR` and friends in the actor
**As a** developer, **I want** one upsert setter, **so that** the poller and `report_pr_created` cannot lose each other's PR.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Concurrent attach keeps both.
  - *Given* a session with no PRs, *When* two goroutines call `AttachPR` for `acme/app#12` (source `branch_discovery`) and `acme/app#13` (source `report_pr_created`) under `-race`, *Then* `Snapshot().GitHub.PRs` holds both, in attach order.
- Snapshot slice is a copy.
  - *Given* a published snapshot with PRs `[#12]`, *When* `AttachPR` adds `#13`, *Then* the earlier snapshot still has length 1.
- Mirror projection, independent of the flag.
  - *Given* links `#11 merged`, `#12 open` and every scope off, *When* `AttachPR` completes, *Then* `Snapshot().GitHub.GitHubPRNumber` is 12 and a log line `session active pr changed from=11 to=12` is emitted; flipping a scope on or off republishes nothing and changes no snapshot value.
- Attach before the session row exists, and with an empty uuid (verified in T2.3.1, which owns the persistence it needs).
  - *Given* a create-from-URL path that calls `AttachPR` before the first save, *When* the instance has no `session` row yet, *Then* the in-memory snapshot holds the PR, the DB write is deferred (the instance is marked `prPersistPending`), and the first full save writes state, link and mirror in one transaction; if that save fails the PR is retried by the next save and the failure is logged. *Given* a session with `uuid=""` at runtime, *When* `AttachPR` runs, *Then* it returns `ErrNoSessionUUID` to the DB layer, the legacy scalar write path is used for that session (logged once with the title), no link is created, and the sweeper/gate keep protecting it via the \"legacy number, no link\" rule.
- Legacy overwrite semantics are preserved where they were correct.
  - *Given* `GitHubPRNumber=11` open and `AttachPR` for #12 (no branch match), *When* it completes, *Then* the scalars show 12 (newest non-terminal, same as the old last-writer result) and the #11 link is untouched.
**Files**: `session/instance_pr_set.go`, `session/instance_actor_setters.go`, `session/instance_snapshot.go`, `session/instance_pr_attach_test.go`

##### Task 2.1.1: Snapshot field (M)
- `GitHubIntegration.PRs []PRView`; two new bools on `Instance` AND `InstanceSnapshot.GitHub` (`LegacyPRUnrepresented`, `HasPRLinks`; copied by `buildSnapshot`, never re-derived there, and copied back out by `ToInstanceData` at `storage.go:387, 650, 700` / `instance_serialization.go:86-100`, because a value placed only on a loaded `InstanceData` is rebuilt away by the first full save, architecture C2); deep copy in `buildSnapshot`; legacy scalars always computed from `ActivePR` when the session has any link (`buildSnapshot` stays a pure builder: no config reads, no scope check); update the "single authoritative field list" header comment.
- Files: `session/instance_snapshot.go`, `session/instance_snapshot_test.go`

##### Task 2.1.2: Setters in a new file (M)
- `AttachPR`, `ApplyPRUpdate(ref, PRStatusUpdate)`, `MarkPRTerminal`, `PinPR`, `DetachPR` live in a NEW file `session/instance_pr_set.go` (still `Instance` methods, same lock and snapshot protocol as `setGitHubPRURLLocked`: lock, merge inside lock, `buildSnapshot`, `snapshot.Store`). `instance_actor_setters.go` (already ~30 setters) keeps only the thin `SetGitHubPR` / `SetGitHubPRNumber` wrappers. The wrappers build the `PRRef` from the session's resolved `GitHubHost/Owner/Repo` and call `AttachPR`; when no owner/repo is resolved (or the uuid is empty) they write the legacy scalar only and create no link, like the empty-uuid case (Phase 4 B2: today's `SetGitHubPRNumber` writes only the number, VERIFIED `instance_actor_setters.go:296-312`). `AttachPR` and `DetachPR` also recompute `HasPRLinks` and `LegacyPRUnrepresented` (an attach that represents the legacy number, or a detach that tombstones it, clears the latter). These setters mutate the in-memory snapshot only (`ApplyPRUpdate`/`MarkPRTerminal` apply an observation the poller already wrote to the state row). The storage write is the T2.3.1 transaction (link rows and mirror columns, never state columns: invariant I1), issued after the actor returns and built from the snapshot at persist time. Reads use `Snapshot()` (`.claude/rules/instance-lock-free-reads.md`).
- Files: `session/instance_pr_set.go`, `session/instance_actor_setters.go`, `session/instance_pr_attach_test.go`

##### Task 2.1.3: Role assignment rules (XS)
- A session with no `primary` gets `primary`; otherwise `stacked` when `base_branch` equals another attached PR's head, else `followup` (this covers a PR attached after an earlier one merged, backfill imports into a session that already has a primary, and discovery in Story 3.3). `rework` and `external` do not exist (Phase 4 C11: no task could assign them); `unknown` is only a parse fallback for an unrecognized stored value. Table test.
- Files: `session/pr_set_rules.go`, `session/pr_set_test.go`

#### Story 2.2: Route every legacy writer through `AttachPR`
**As a** maintainer, **I want** the nine writers audited and migrated, **so that** the relation and the mirror cannot diverge.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Terminal scan attaches instead of overwriting.
  - *Given* a session already holding `acme/app#11`, *When* terminal output contains `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/12`, *Then* both rows exist and `#12` has `attach_source=push_scan` (flag on). With the flag off the writers still go through `AttachPR` (the old setters are wrappers), so links accumulate too; the intended flag-off behavior is that the legacy scalars show the `ActivePR` projection (newest non-terminal), which equals the old last-writer-wins value except when two PRs are open at once and the older one matches the current branch. This difference is deliberate and is pinned by a test.
- (The create-PR-after-merge AC lives in Story 4.2 with T4.2.4, wave 2; iteration 6 moved it out of this story because it needs T4.2.4.)
- Artifact scanner does not seed.
  - *Given* a blob whose `PRURLs` contain `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/99` and a session with NO PR, *When* `OnScanComplete` runs with the flag ON and again with it OFF, *Then* no `SessionPR` row and no legacy number change occurs in either case (Phase 4 B2: the old seed calls `SetGitHubPRNumber`, which this plan turns into an `AttachPR` wrapper, so a conditional skip would let a flag-off session create a phantom, polled PR, the scenario ADR-004 rejected).
- Terminal scan matches the session's repo and host before attaching.
  - *Given* a session on `acme/app` (host `github.com`) whose terminal prints `https://github.com/other/lib/pull/7`, *When* the scan runs, *Then* no link is created and a debug line names the mismatch; *Given* output `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/12` on a `git push` / `gh pr create` result line, *Then* it attaches with `attach_source=push_scan`; a bare mention of `.../pull/12` in non-push output does not attach.
- Ratchet is field-scoped, so it does not fail on unrelated lines.
  - *Given* a new non-test file that assigns `inst.GitHubPRURL =` (or `GitHubPRNumber`, `GitHubPRStatusTerminal`, `GitHubOwner`, `GitHubRepo`), *When* `make pr-field-guard` runs, *Then* it fails; *Given* the unmodified repo plus this epic's changes, *Then* it passes while `inst.RetryMaxAttempts =` / `inst.Status =` in `session_driver.go` do not trip it; the target is proven red-then-green by a test fixture or a documented one-line run in the commit message.
**Files**: `session/session_driver.go`, `server/services/pr_creation_service.go`, `server/services/session_service_lifecycle.go`, `server/services/session_service_create.go`, `server/services/session_creation_pipeline.go`, `session/instance.go`, `session/session.go`, `server/dependencies.go`, `Makefile`

##### Task 2.2.1: Terminal scan writer (M)
- FIRST, in the legacy writer as it stands (also flag off): clear `GitHubPRStatusTerminal` whenever the block changes the PR number, regardless of `HasPRLinks`, so a merged PR A's `true` no longer rides onto an open PR B (adversarial C7; characterization test pins it), AND persist the clear at once through the existing title-keyed `UpdateGitHubPRStatusTerminal(title, false)` (`ent_repository.go:1156` via `Storage.UpdateGitHubPRStatusTerminal`, `storage.go:792`; `session_driver.go` holds no storage handle today (VERIFIED grep), so T2.2.1 injects the same persist callback the poller already uses; works for fork and empty-uuid sessions that have no link, where the full-save set-only path would never clear the stored `true`, adversarial C2). If that write fails the Instance keeps a `terminalClearPending` bit and the next full save writes `false`. This uses no E2 code, so T2.2.1 has no dependency on T2.3.3. Then replace the raw `inst.mu.Lock()` block with `AttachPR`; with the flag on, replace the `prURLLinked` bool with a set of already-attached URLs so a second push URL is scanned. VERIFIED gap this closes: `scanAndLinkPRURL` takes any match from `scanTerminalForPRURL` without comparing owner/repo to the session's (`session_driver.go:953-967`); the new code requires the URL's host, owner and repo to equal the session's resolved `GitHubHost/Owner/Repo` (a session with no resolved repo attaches nothing from a scan) and reads only push/`gh pr create` output lines (decision: no separate `unverified` tier, because the repo match plus push-line restriction already removes the unrelated-PR case). Tombstoned refs are skipped. No `Start`/revive call is added (noarchivedrevival unaffected; confirm with `make lint-custom`). Canonical identity (moved from T1.3.6): a terminal line with `https://github.com/Acme/App/pull/12` for a session on `acme/app` attaches to the one state row `acme/app#12`, and a tombstone for `Acme/App#12` suppresses it.

#### Story 2.3: Persistence write and load paths
**As a** developer, **I want** dual-write and a bulk load, **so that** the legacy columns stay readable and the list RPC has no N+1.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Legacy columns can be cleared when the flag is on.
  - *Given* a session whose only row is detached, *When* `Storage.PersistPRMirror(uuid, "", 0, false)` runs, *Then* `github_pr_number` is 0 (today's `if data.GitHubPRNumber > 0` update path cannot do this).
- Older binary compatibility.
  - *Given* data written with the flag on and two rows, *When* an old-schema reader (test using only `InstanceData` JSON tags) loads the session, *Then* it sees the active PR in `github_pr_number` and `github_pr_url`.
- Bulk load.
  - *Given* 200 sessions, *When* `LoadInstances` runs, *Then* the link+state queries issued number at most `2 * ceil(n/500)` (= 2 for 200).
- Link, state and mirror are one transaction.
  - *Given* an injected failure after the link upsert but before the mirror update, *When* `AttachSessionPR` runs, *Then* neither the link, the state change nor the mirror columns are visible afterwards (all-or-nothing).
- A resumed session clears the persisted terminal flag through the normal save path.
  - *Given* a session persisted with `github_pr_status_terminal=true` (all PRs terminal), *When* `AttachPR` adds an open #12 and any full save runs (`ent_repository.go` create/update paths at ~412 and ~758), *Then* the DB column reads false afterwards and `ListInstanceDataWithWorktree()` returns `GitHubPRStatusTerminal=false`.
- Skipped-class flags survive the first full save (architecture C2).
  - *Given* a session whose legacy number is unrepresented (skipped backfill class), *When* `LoadInstances` then a full save then `LoadInstances` again runs, *Then* `github_pr_number`/`github_pr_url` are unchanged after each step and `Instance.LegacyPRUnrepresented` is still true (the value lives on `Instance` and the snapshot, not on a loaded `InstanceData`).
- Full saves never write state rows (invariant I1, adversarial N2).
  - *Given* a backfill-seeded state `unknown terminal=true` loaded into session `u1`'s snapshot, *When* the row is updated to `open` through `UpdatePullRequestState` (T3.2.1 repeats this through the poll core) and then ANY full save of `u1` runs (normal save, the `prPersistPending` flush, a suspended or archived session holding a stale snapshot), *Then* the state row still reads `state=open terminal=false`. A guard test fails if a non-test file other than `session/pr_status_poller.go` calls `UpdatePullRequestState` or its `Storage` wrapper, and `make pr-state-write-guard` fails on a direct ent-client write to `PullRequestState` outside the repository file (T2.3.4).
**Files**: `session/ent_repository.go`, `session/storage.go`, `session/instance_serialization.go`

##### Task 2.3.1: Dual write, clearable mirror, one transaction (M)
- Add `Storage.AttachSessionPR`, `PersistPRMirror`, keyed by `session_uuid` (not `Title`; `UpdateGitHubPRNumber` is title-keyed today). The mirror update needs the `session` row: when it does not exist yet `AttachSessionPR` returns `ErrSessionNotPersisted` and the caller marks `prPersistPending` so the first full save (which carries the snapshot's `PRs`) writes everything in one transaction; an empty uuid returns `ErrNoSessionUUID` (legacy write path, see Story 2.1 AC). Persists are SERIALIZED PER SESSION (a per-uuid mutex or a version check on the snapshot) so a slower writer holding an older snapshot cannot land last. Every full save also idempotently upserts the snapshot's LINKS and ensures their state rows exist with `EnsureState` (insert-if-absent); it NEVER updates a `PullRequestState` column (invariant I1), which is the retry path for a persist that failed after the actor returned without letting a stale snapshot overwrite a fresher poll. The `EnsureState`, the link upsert and the mirror columns on the `session` row (`github_pr_number`, `github_pr_url`, `github_pr_status_terminal`) are written in ONE ent transaction (same database). Authoritative store on crash: the DB; the snapshot is rebuilt from it on load. The values written are read from the snapshot at persist time, never from a value captured before the actor call. Before copying any narrow-writer pattern, grep non-test callers (memory `instinct_persistence_writer_caller_check`); state the crash-loss window in the doc comment. This task also adds the thin `Storage.UpdatePullRequestState` wrapper (no production caller until T3.2.1). Canonical identity (moved from T1.3.6): `AttachSessionPR` given `Acme/App#12` and `acme/app#12` yields one state row and, for one session, one link.
- Files: `session/storage.go`, `session/ent_repository.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go`

##### Task 2.3.2: Bulk hydrate on load (S)
- `LoadInstances` calls `ListBySessionUUIDs` once and hydrates `PRs` on each live instance before the first snapshot publish, and sets the `Instance` fields `LegacyPRUnrepresented` (legacy number > 0, no link for that `PRRef`, no tombstone; while true, full saves do not touch `github_pr_number`/`github_pr_url` and the reconciler skips the session, T1.5.1) and `HasPRLinks` (the full-save path cannot otherwise know whether a session has links, per the adversarial minor on T2.3.3), published through the snapshot and copied out by `ToInstanceData` (T2.1.1). Test: load -> full save -> load -> full save leaves the legacy columns untouched for a skipped session (the flag survives the first save). This covers snapshot-backed readers only; `InstanceData`-backed readers (retention sweeper, `unfinished_work_service`, approval) use T4.3.0.
- Files: `session/storage.go`, `session/instance_serialization.go`

##### Task 2.3.3: Clearable terminal flag on full saves (S)
- Root cause (VERIFIED): the full-save paths at `ent_repository.go:412` and `:758` only call `SetGithubPrStatusTerminal(true)`, so they can set but never clear it; clearing happens only through the title-keyed `UpdateGitHubPRStatusTerminal` (`:1156`). Fix: the full-save update path writes `SetGithubPrStatusTerminal(data.GitHubPRStatusTerminal)` unconditionally for sessions where `HasPRLinks` is true AND `LegacyPRUnrepresented` is false (a skipped, unrepresented legacy number keeps today's set-only behavior and `github_pr_number`/`github_pr_url` are left untouched, as in T2.3.2 and the Story 2.3 AC; both are `Instance` fields, set by T2.3.2 at load and by `AttachPR`, carried out through `ToInstanceData`; an in-transaction link count is the fallback) (and `SetGithubPrNumber`/`SetGithubPrURL` likewise, so `PersistPRMirror(uuid, "", 0, false)` and a detach can clear them), while sessions without links keep today's set-only behavior so an old in-memory value cannot erase data; their stale-`true` clear is the immediate title-keyed write of T2.2.1 (or the `terminalClearPending` bit), not this path. Test through the real save path, not only the narrow writer. Add the hook-path test (pre-mortem #13, the claim was only INFERRED): drive `cmd/ssq-hooks`' actual `NewEntRepository` + save path (`cmd/ssq-hooks/main.go:633`) against a session with a legacy number and no link, and assert `github_pr_number`, `github_pr_url` and the terminal flag are unchanged.
- Files: `session/ent_repository.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr_test.go`

##### Task 2.3.4: Single-writer guard and stale-snapshot test (M)
- Headline test (red first): `TestFullSave_DoesNotOverwritePolledState`: seed `unknown terminal=true`, a direct `UpdatePullRequestState` call (the wave-2 poll core is not available yet) writes `open`, run each full-save path (create, update, `prPersistPending` flush, archived and suspended sessions with stale snapshots), assert the row stays `open`. Caller allow-list test for `UpdatePullRequestState` covering BOTH the repository method and its `Storage.UpdatePullRequestState` wrapper in `storage.go` (only `pr_status_poller.go` may call either; fails with a message pointing at invariant I1), and for `ResetVerifyCounters` (only `session/session_pr_backfill.go`'s `RunPRStartupMaintenance` and its `Storage` wrapper in `storage.go` may call it, listed like the `UpdatePullRequestState` wrapper, architecture N4; a call from `ent_repository.go`/`NewEntRepository` or `ent_repository_migrations.go` fails). Direct ent writes are covered by a Makefile grep ratchet `pr-state-write-guard` (wired into `make ready` beside `pr-field-guard`): it fails on `PullRequestState\.(Create|Update|UpdateOne|UpdateOneID|Delete|DeleteOne|DeleteOneID)` or an `OnConflict`/`ClientTx` use on that entity in any non-test `.go` file except `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go` (which holds `UpdatePullRequestState`, `EnsureState` and `ResetVerifyCounters`) and `session/session_pr_gc.go` (DELETE only, never Update) and generated `session/ent/`. Same limits and typed-analyzer follow-up as T2.2.6 (precedent `tools/lint/norawdb`). The test also asserts `EnsureState` leaves an existing row byte-identical, and the monotonic rules of T1.3.2 hold; the through-the-poll-core variant and the allow-list's positive case move to T3.2.1 (wave 2).
- Files: `session/ent_repository_session_pr_test.go`, `session/pr_state_writer_guard_test.go`, `Makefile`
