## Phase 2: Behavior (Wave 2)

### Epic 3: PR-aware poller and tracking core
**Goal**: The poller polls open PRs (not sessions), resumes after a merge, and `pr_tracking.go` acts on an explicit or active PR.

#### Story 3.1: Refactor-first (behavior identical)
**As a** maintainer, **I want** the poller and `pr_tracking.go` decomposed before they change, **so that** the modified functions pass gocognit/funlen and behavior is provably unchanged.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- All existing poller tests pass unchanged.
  - *Given* the pre-refactor test suite for `session/pr_status_poller_test.go`, *When* `go test ./session -run PRStatusPoller` runs after the extraction, *Then* every test passes without edits to assertions.
- Complexity gate.
  - *Given* the extracted functions, *When* `make ready-complexity-gate` runs, *Then* no new finding appears for `checkAllSessions`, `fetchAndUpdatePRStatus` or `pr_tracking.go` methods.
**Files**: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/pr_tracking.go`

##### Task 3.1.1: Extract poller helpers (M)
- Extract `pollableSessions` (the filter at `pr_status_poller.go:306-345`), `discoverPRsForBranch`, `pollOne(ref)` from `fetchAndUpdatePRStatus`. No behavior change. Interplay with open BUG-023 (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-023-pr-status-poller-mutex-churn.md`: one `sync.Mutex` serializes instance-list reads, auth state and poll writes): this plan does NOT fix BUG-023 and leaves it open. The new code must not make it worse: no GitHub request and no ent call is made while holding `p.mu` (the extracted helpers copy what they need under the lock and release it first), and the T3.2.1/T3.2.5 state loops take no `p.mu` at all because they read `PullRequestState` rows, not the instance list. AC: a test with a blocked fake GitHub client proves `p.mu` is free while a poll is in flight. If T3.1.1 touches the same lock sites, converting auth state to `atomic.Value` is optional collateral per `feedback_fix_collateral_debt`, as its own commit.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`

##### Task 3.1.2: Extract `resolvePR` (S)
- One `resolvePR(ref *PRRef) (PRRef, error)` used by `RefreshPRInfo`, `GetPRComments`, `GetPRDiff`, `PostComment`, `SetCommitStatus`, `MergePR`, `ClosePR`, `GeneratePRContextPrompt`; no new parameter yet.
- Files: `session/pr_tracking.go`

#### Story 3.2: Poll open PRs, not sessions
**As an** operator, **I want** a merged PR A to stop being polled while PR B is polled, **so that** status and actions are never stale.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Headline tests green (Task 1.3.4).
- Merged PR is not re-polled.
  - *Given* rows `#11 merged terminal`, `#12 open`, *When* three ticks run, *Then* GitHub calls for #11 are 0 and for #12 are 3 (or 304s), and `ssq_pr_poll_total{state="open"}` increments 3.
- Session resumes after PR B appears (asserted with T3.3.1's discovery, which uses T3.2.2).
  - *Given* a session with only `#11 merged terminal`, *When* branch discovery finds `acme/app#12` for the current branch, *Then* `#12` is attached, the session terminal flag becomes false, and the next tick polls #12.
- Two sessions, one PR (the single-fetch half is T3.2.1's; the per-session fan-out is T3.2.2's `applyPRUpdate`).
  - *Given* sessions `u1` and `u2` both linked to `acme/app#12` open, *When* a tick runs, *Then* one GitHub call is made, the one `PullRequestState` row is updated once, and both sessions' snapshots are refreshed from it.
- Item-only PRs are polled.
  - *Given* a Jules or backfilled item-direct link to `acme/app#40` with no session link and state `open`, *When* a tick runs, *Then* #40 is fetched and its state updated (no session snapshot is needed), so `ItemPRVerdict` and the cleanup gate see current state.
- Sessions with an empty uuid or colliding Titles keep working.
  - *Given* a legacy session with `uuid=""` and a pollable legacy PR, *When* a tick runs, *Then* its backoff state is keyed by uuid when present and by Title otherwise, and no entry collides with another session's.
- Cold start is bounded.
  - *Given* 120 `unknown` states (60 non-terminal, 60 unverified terminal) after the first `reader:poller` flip, *When* ticks run, *Then* at most `maxColdPollsPerTick` (default 20) are fetched per tick, ALL non-terminal ones before any unverified terminal one (the blocking rows resolve first), each group newest `attached_at` first, and the ETag cache is populated as they go.
- Persistent failure becomes `unknown`.
  - *Given* a non-terminal PR whose fetch fails with a COUNTED failure (classifier in T3.2.4) on 5 consecutive polls with `last_polled_at - first_fail_at >= 30 minutes`, *When* the 5th failure lands, *Then* the state becomes `unknown` with `poll_error` set, `ssq_pr_poll_failures_total` increments, a notification is raised once, and the session/item is not treated as terminal; a later successful fetch restores the real state. A rate-limit response records `rate_limit_reset_at` and does NOT count toward failures. *Given* an unverified terminal row whose fetch fails with a counted failure five times with the first-to-last span >= 30 minutes (PR not found on a reachable repo, or 401/403 on a host whose token works for other PRs), *Then* verify is exhausted: it now counts as terminal, is dropped from the work list, and raises NO notification (no storm on first deploy). Classifier cases, each a test (adversarial C5): (1) a 403 with `Retry-After`, or `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0`, or a body naming a secondary/abuse rate limit, or a 429 -> NOT counted, records `rate_limit_reset_at`; (2) a 401, or a missing token for a host, when that host has had NO successful fetch within the last 15 minutes (token-wide: every PR on it failing at once) -> NOT counted, a host-level `auth` condition is logged once and `ssq_pr_poll_failures_total{reason="auth"}` increments; the same 401 when the host succeeded recently -> counted; (3) a 404 or 403 on a PR whose repository metadata GET (`/repos/{o}/{r}`) also fails 404/403, or a 403 with an `X-GitHub-SSO` header -> NOT counted (`reason="repo_inaccessible"`; cannot tell deleted from invisible, so the row stays held, Known Risk K5); a 404 with the repo GET 200 -> counted; (4) 5xx, timeouts and network errors never count and do not touch the streak or `last_polled_at`; they write `poll_error=network` only (test moved here from T1.3.2: counted failures at t0 and t0+20m with a 5xx between them keep the streak, and a third counted failure at t0+31m exhausts the row). *Given* a boot-time outage (every fetch times out, or a GHE token not loaded yet) lasting an hour, *Then* no row is exhausted, none is stranded as terminal, and `ResetVerifyCounters` has zeroed `poll_fail_count`/`first_fail_at` of unverified terminal rows once at service boot, inside `RunPRStartupMaintenance` (called from the service bootstrap only, `BuildOptions.RunPRMaintenance`) and before any poller, sweeper or cleanup ticker exists (ordering AC: a sweeper built after the service core-deps phase never sees a stale exhausted row as terminal; hook, CLI and MCP-fallback processes never reset it; while the first pass has not succeeded rows read as not exhausted); there is NO nightly reset, and `now` alone never exhausts a row (count 5, first_fail_at 40 minutes ago, last counted failure 2 minutes after it -> NOT exhausted); the count of never-verified rows is a gauge (`ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows`) named in the runbook, which also notes that frequent restarts reset verification each time (safe direction: a retained worktree is not a gate bug).
- Permanently failing PRs back off.
  - *Given* a state failing every poll, *When* ticks run, *Then* the next-attempt delay doubles with `poll_fail_count` (base 1 tick, cap 6 hours, injected clock), the state is `unknown` after the 5th counted failure and then polled only on the cold cap with the same backoff, and a success resets `poll_fail_count`. Backoff for NON-counted failures (5xx, timeouts, rate limit, host auth) is in-memory per state id in the poller; the row gets only the `poll_error` enum (`rate_limited`, `auth`, `repo_inaccessible`, `not_found` or `network`, Phase 4 C8), cleared by the next success, so the UI can say why a row is stale.
- Superseded rounds' PRs are still refreshed.
  - *Given* a state linked only to an earlier (superseded) work session and a state linked to the current round, both `open`, *When* a tick runs, *Then* both are fetched, so the cleanup gate that holds the earlier session's worktree sees current state; a state whose owner session was deleted is collected by the nightly GC (T1.3.5) and is at most polled until then.
- Rows stay fresh with every flag scope off.
  - *Given* all `session_multi_pr` scopes off and a state `open` whose PR merged on GitHub (non-active PR of a session, an item-only link, and a PR of a suspended session), *When* the state refresher runs, *Then* each row becomes `merged terminal` and the sweeper and gate see it, with no scope enabled.
- Item-only PR host token.
  - *Given* an item-only state on host `ghe.acme.io`, *When* it is polled, *Then* the request uses the host-aware client/token for that host (the same per-host resolver the session path uses, via `newGHRequestForHostWithToken`); with no token for the host the poll counts as a failure toward `unknown` rather than falling back to github.com.
- Rate-limit.
  - *Given* a session with an open PR on its current branch, *When* a tick runs, *Then* zero branch-list calls are made for it; a session with no open PR makes at most one list call per tick.
- Flag-off double fetch is bounded.
  - *Given* all scopes off and one active open PR, *When* an interval elapses, *Then* the legacy per-session loop and the refresher each make at most one conditional GET for it (the second is a 304 from the shared ETag cache, no quota cost) and no extra 200 body is fetched; a call-count test pins `<= 2` requests per PR per interval. No component skips on `last_polled_at`: the legacy loop does not write state rows, so that skip never could have applied (architecture C-a, adversarial C9).
**Files**: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/pr_multi_poller_test.go`

##### Task 3.2.1: Poll non-terminal `PullRequestState` rows (M)
- T3.4.1 (audit) is a hard predecessor. The work list is `ListPollableStates` (T1.3.2: non-terminal states plus unverified terminal ones that are not `VerifyExhausted()`, ordered non-terminal first; item-only states included), not a per-session loop over snapshots, so each PR is polled exactly once and item-only PRs are covered. Failure backoff: exponential on `poll_fail_count` (base one tick, cap 6 hours), applied to every state and to `unknown` ones on top of the cold cap. The tick interval is read from the poller config; the runbook states the cold drain time as `ceil(N / maxColdPollsPerTick)` ticks. The fetch core calls `UpdatePullRequestState` (invariant I1: the only state writer). Under `reader:poller`, snapshot refresh and discovery use this loop; a session's snapshot is refreshed through `inst.ApplyPRUpdate` for every live session linked to the polled state. With every scope off the legacy per-session loop still drives snapshots and discovery unchanged, and this same fetch core runs unconditionally as the rows-only refresher of T3.2.5. Use the shared `ETagCache()`; honor `IsSuspended()` and archival (record-only for archived sessions). Per-session discovery/backoff state (`noPRPollAfter` and friends, today keyed by `Title`, `pr_status_poller.go:300-337`) is keyed by uuid, falling back to Title only when uuid is empty. `maxColdPollsPerTick` bounds `unknown`/never-polled states from the first deploy (the refresher is unconditional). Tests here also cover the through-the-poll-core stale-snapshot assertion and the allow-list positive case deferred from T2.3.4.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`

##### Task 3.2.2: Apply per-PR results and derive session terminal (M)
- `applyPRUpdate` writes the `PullRequestState` row once (`UpdatePullRequestState`), then calls `inst.ApplyPRUpdate` for each linked live session. These are one state write plus N per-session mirror writes with NO enclosing transaction; the doc comment on `applyPRUpdate` states the accepted gap (a crash between them leaves the state row ahead of some mirror columns, repaired by the always-on reconciler, T1.5.2) and that each session's persist is serialized per session (T2.3.1) so an older snapshot cannot land after a newer one; merged/closed calls `MarkPRTerminal`; session terminal = at least one link, all linked states terminal and discovery idle, persisted to `github_pr_status_terminal` via the T2.3.1 transaction and the T2.3.3 clearable save. Turn the headline test green. `applyPRUpdate` RETURNS the set of state ids that just went terminal and calls nothing else; T5.4.3 (a later task) defines `PRTerminalListener` and the nil-safe call at the end of `applyPRUpdate` (architecture C8). This task also removes the skip from `TestPoller_MergedAThenOpenB_PollsAndActsOnB` (T1.3.4).
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/instance_pr_set.go`

##### Task 3.2.4: `unknown` state on persistent failure and the error classifier (M)
- Track `poll_fail_count`/`poll_error`/`rate_limit_reset_at` on `PullRequestState`; N=5 COUNTED failures with `last_polled_at - first_fail_at >= 30 minutes` (first to LAST counted failure, never to now; constants, injected clock) moves a non-terminal state to `unknown`. One pure function `classifyPollError(resp, body, hostHealth) -> counted|rate_limit|auth|repo_inaccessible|transient` implements the four AC cases of Story 3.2 (secondary rate limits, token-wide 401/missing token via a per-host in-memory `lastSuccessAt` with a 15-minute window, repo-metadata probe on 404/403, SSO header, 5xx/network); only `counted` writes `poll_fail_count`/`first_fail_at`; every class writes the `poll_error` enum (`rate_limited`, `auth`, `repo_inaccessible`, `not_found`, `network`; `transient` maps to `network`) and rate limits also `rate_limit_reset_at`; a non-counted failure neither breaks nor extends the streak. The repo-metadata probe is made at most on a counted-candidate 404/403 (at most 5 per row); its request is built with `NewConditionalRequestNoCache` (host-aware base URL, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}`) and never with a raw `http.NewRequest*` (`.claude/rules/norawghrequest.md`; the `norawghrequest` analyzer in `make lint-custom` is the gate, and the T3.2.4 test asserts the probe hits the fake server's host-aware URL with the host's token). Recovery restores state and clears `first_fail_at`; unverified terminal rows never notify (see AC) and become effectively terminal only when `VerifyExhausted()` (same N and span); their counters are reset once per boot by `ResetVerifyCounters` (T1.5.1), not by the refresher and not nightly (architecture C4). `unknown` is surfaced in the UI (Task 6.1.3 error chip) and in the stuck detector (T5.2.2 reports it, naming each owner that holds the PR). The exit is detach, one exact link at a time: a session-linked PR uses `DetachSessionPullRequest` (T4.2.3); an item-direct PR (Jules, manual item link, archived-item backfill) uses `DetachItemPullRequest` (T4.2.7). Each deletes exactly that owner's link and writes that owner's tombstone in one transaction, with an audit log. A PR held by both a session and the item needs both detaches (or the item detach alone when only the verdict should ignore it).
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/instance_pr_set.go`, `session/pr_multi_poller_test.go`

##### Task 3.2.5: Flag-independent state refresher (S)
- Decision (flag-off leak): the state-row refresh runs ALWAYS, from the first deploy; the flag gates readers only. Reason: the cleanup gate and sweeper are deliberately not flag-gated, but the legacy poller skips suspended sessions, forks and terminal sessions (`pr_status_poller.go:310-326`) and refreshes only the session's active PR, so with the flag off rows would sit at attach-time defaults and block cleanup and retention forever. The alternative (gate the gate, with a documented fallback to the legacy check) was rejected because it would reintroduce the fan-in of legacy readers and an untested second code path on a data-loss site.
- Implementation: the refresher is the T3.2.1 loop (`ListPollableStates`, one fetch per state, ETag cache, backoff, cold cap) started unconditionally, except that every tick first reads `config.SessionMultiPRRefresher()` (T1.4.1) and skips the tick, logging once per change, when it is off (Phase 4 pre-mortem #3: the live kill switch; rows then go stale, which only withholds deletes). With every scope off it only updates `PullRequestState` rows (through `UpdatePullRequestState`, the single writer) and reports newly terminal states through the same `applyPRUpdate` return value and T5.4.3 call site; it does not drive snapshots, discovery or session-terminal derivation (those stay behind `reader:poller`). The legacy per-session loop and the legacy merge-detection paths (`ReconcilePRPending`, `closeIfSupersededByMain`) are NOT changed to write state rows: a merge they observe is picked up by the refresher within one poll interval. Flag off (`reader:backlog` off), `ReconcilePRPending` and `reconcileBouncingItems` run exactly today's single-PR code: no verdict, no live-work hold. With `reader:backlog` on they use the verdict and the hold (T5.2.4); that is the only path that differs (owner rule R3, Wave 2 slot A part 2). Cost: see the "double fetch is bounded" AC (at most one extra conditional GET per PR per interval).
- The refresher does NOT reset counters. Verification restarts after every boot because `RunPRStartupMaintenance` (service bootstrap only) calls `ResetVerifyCounters` (T1.5.1/T1.3.2) before the refresher exists; this keeps `UpdatePullRequestState` and `ResetVerifyCounters` as the only state-column writers (I1) and removes the race between a boot-time UPDATE and a concurrent poll. Ordering clause (moved here from Story 1.5, Phase 4 B4): the refresher is constructed after `RunPRStartupMaintenance`, asserted with a recording fake over the service bootstrap sequence.
- Fork sessions (architecture C6): the legacy poller skips `GitHubIsFork` sessions (`pr_status_poller.go:322`, upstream lookup is Phase 2) and `GitHubIsFork` is NOT an ent `session` column (VERIFIED: no match in `session/ent/schema/session.go`; it lives only in the `InstanceData` JSON, `storage.go:76`), so the boot backfill cannot see it. Behavior: live `AttachPR` on a fork session attaches NO link and keeps the legacy scalar path (like the empty-uuid case; the "legacy number, no link" rule protects it); a fork session the backfill already linked polls under the session's own owner/repo, 404s, becomes `unknown` after exhaustion of the 5 counted failures and holds the gate until the user detaches it. Accepted: forks rarely carry a PR number because the legacy poller never fills one; the exit is `DetachSessionPullRequest` (the runbook names this case).
- Tests: flag off, a merged non-active PR, an item-only PR and a suspended session's PR all become terminal after one refresher tick; flag off, snapshots and `GitHubPRStatusTerminal` of the session are unchanged by the refresher; flag off, a session with two links where the legacy loop observes the active PR merged (instance field `true`) keeps `true` through `buildSnapshot` and a full save, and the persisted value is not cleared by stale `PRView.State=open` (architecture C1); the legacy characterization tests still pass; `refresher_off` scope set, a tick makes zero GitHub calls and writes nothing, and back on the next tick polls.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/pr_multi_legacy_test.go`

##### Task 3.2.3: Slow discovery for all-terminal sessions (S)
- `terminalDiscoveryBackoff` = 10x `NoPRBackoff`; applies only to non-suspended, non-archived sessions with a live branch; injected clock test; log line on resume.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`

#### Story 3.3: Discovery, webhooks, metrics
**As an** operator, **I want** new PRs on a session's branch recorded and webhooks routed per PR, **so that** PR B is not invisible.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Discovery attaches without overwriting.
  - *Given* a session with `#11` and a new open PR `#12` on its current branch, *When* discovery runs, *Then* `AttachPR(source=branch_discovery)` records #12 with the role T2.1.3 derives (`followup`: #11 is the primary) and `#11` remains.
- Webhook matches by PR.
  - *Given* sessions tracking `#11` (merged) and `#12` (open) and a webhook for `acme/app#12`, *When* `InvalidateAndRefresh(PRRef)` runs, *Then* the session's row 12 refreshes.
- Metrics.
  - *Given* a session that attaches its third PR, *When* attach completes, *Then* `ssq_session_pr_count` observes 3.
**Files**: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `server/` webhook handler, `session/instance_pr_set.go`

##### Task 3.3.1: Discovery attaches (M)
- `discoverPRsForBranch` replaces the `prNumber == 0` guard (`pr_status_poller.go:419-427`) with: list PRs for the current branch (same list-ETag key `owner/repo/branch`), attach any unattached, non-tombstoned one whose repo equals the session's; cap 50. Canonical identity (moved from T1.3.6): a branch-list result naming `Acme/App#12` resolves to the one `acme/app#12` state row and honors a tombstone written for either case.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`

##### Task 3.3.2: `InvalidateAndRefresh(PRRef)` (S)
- Change signature (`pr_status_poller.go:151`) and callers; match rows, not the single field. It runs one fetch through the poll core's per-state path (never writes a row itself) and returns after the observation was applied, dropped or failed, bounded by a 10s timeout (the webhook handler calls it in its own goroutine and does not wait; the reconciler awaits it, T5.2.4); a `pull_request.reopened` webhook passes `source=webhook_reopened`, the only observation allowed to reopen a terminal row (T1.3.2). `ReconcilePRPending` uses it as its merge trigger with `source=reconciler_refresh`, which may fetch a `closed` row once per pass and apply only `closed -> merged` (T1.3.2 transition table, T5.2.4). Canonical identity (moved from T1.3.6): `InvalidateAndRefresh` given `Acme/App#12` or `acme/app#12` refreshes the same row. Wiring (architecture C6): the lifecycle listener receives a consumer-side `PRRefresher` interface (`Refresh(ctx, ref, source) error`, bounded 10 s) implemented by `PRStatusPoller`; a nil refresher falls back to today's synchronous `IsPRMerged`; the callers `session/poller_invalidation_adapter.go:41` and `server/services/github_webhook_pr_fix.go:702` change with the signature (VERIFIED callers: those two); `ReconcilePRPending` stops refreshing for the rest of a pass after the first timeout (per-pass cap), so a GitHub outage costs at most one 10 s wait per pass.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/poller_invalidation_adapter.go`, `server/services/github_webhook_pr_fix.go`, webhook caller under `server/`

##### Task 3.3.3: Logs and metrics (S)
- Add the metrics and log lines from the Observability Plan, including the `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows` gauge. Also the K10 and K2 monitoring as tasks, not runbook text only: the dashboard JSON `docs/observability/grafana/dashboards/session-pr-tracking.json` with the panels and the two warning alert rules listed in the Observability Plan (use the `observability-grafana-dashboards` skill for the dashboard conventions). ### Epic 4: API surface and server readers
**Goal**: The proto carries the PR list (with per-PR poll-health fields), PR actions take an optional target, list/search RPCs accept a `pr` filter, and server readers use the active PR or "all terminal" through a bulk query that also serves `InstanceData`-backed readers.

#### Story 4.1: Proto and adapter
**As a** web/mobile client, **I want** the PR list on `Session` plus legacy scalars, **so that** old clients still render one PR.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Additive proto.
  - *Given* the change, *When* `make proto-breaking` runs against `origin/main`, *Then* it passes, and `Session.github_pr_number=25` / `github_pr_url=26` remain populated from the active PR.
- Adapter.
  - *Given* a snapshot with `#11 merged`, `#12 open`, *When* `instance_adapter.go` maps it, *Then* `pull_requests` has 2 entries with `is_active=true` on #12 and `github_pr_number=12`.
- Poll-health enum (Phase 4 C8).
  - *Given* a row whose last non-counted failure was a rate limit, *When* the adapter maps it, *Then* `poll_error=RATE_LIMITED` and `rate_limit_reset_at` are set (the S2 chip reads "Rate limited until HH:MM"); a `repo_inaccessible` row maps to `REPO_INACCESSIBLE` ("Can't reach this PR on GitHub"); a healthy row maps to `NONE`.
**Files**: `proto/session/v1/types.proto`, `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `server/adapters/instance_adapter.go`

##### Task 4.1.1: Messages and fields (S)
- `message SessionPullRequest {host, owner, repo, number, url, state (open|closed|merged|unknown), is_draft, role, head_branch, base_branch, created_at, merged_at, closed_at, is_active, pinned, attach_source, check_conclusion, approved_count, changes_requested_count, poll_error (enum `PollError`: NONE, AUTH, RATE_LIMITED, NOT_FOUND, REPO_INACCESSIBLE, NETWORK, mapped from the persisted column; Phase 4 C8), last_polled_at, rate_limit_reset_at, outcome_unverified}` (`outcome_unverified` is derived by the adapter as `state==unknown && terminal`, not stored; the three poll-health fields feed the UX row states "Can't refresh", "Rate limited until HH:MM" and "last seen 2d ago" in `design/ux.md` S2);
- `ListSessionsRequest` (and the search/browse requests in T9.2.1 and T10.1.1) gains an optional `PullRequestRef pr` filter so the omnibar action "Find sessions for this PR" can route to `/artifacts?pr=<owner/repo#N>` and the session list can filter by PR; the response carries the matching link's role and state per session;
- `Session.program` already exists (`types.proto`, field 7, VERIFIED), so no proto change is needed; T7.2.1 adds a `transcript_supported` boolean on `SessionArtifacts` (derived server-side from the program and the history linker's supported set) so the tab can tell "waiting for transcript link" from "this program never gets one" without hard-coding program names in the client; `message PullRequestRef {host, owner, repo, number}`; `repeated SessionPullRequest pull_requests = 100` on `Session`; same repeated field on the backlog item message (next free number there); optional `PullRequestRef pr` on `GetPRCommentsRequest`, `PostPRCommentRequest`, `MergePRRequest`, `ClosePRRequest` (and the diff/ask-to-fix request messages); `MergePRRequest.method` (enum `MERGE|SQUASH|REBASE`, default MERGE, never squash unless explicitly requested; the S3 dialog's method select, Phase 4 N7); `RefreshSessionPullRequests{session_id}` returning the refreshed list (the S2 header Refresh button, T4.2.3); `message PrHold {kind (OPEN_PR|UNVERIFIED|UNKNOWN|SUPERSEDED_ROUND|SESSION_ACTIVE|MAX_HOLD), PullRequestRef pr, owner_session_id, idle_window_seconds, last_activity_at}` and `repeated PrHold pr_holds` on `GetSessionResponse`'s session and on the backlog item message (detail responses only, never list responses, so K11 is not affected), which feed the held lines of design/ux.md "Held states" (the client never recomputes a hold). Run `make proto-gen`, `make proto-lint`, `make proto-breaking` (needs reachable `origin/main`).

#### Story 4.2: PR action RPCs target the right PR
**As an** operator, **I want** merge, close, comment, diff and ask-to-fix to act on an explicit PR or the active one and refuse a PR not attached to the session, **so that** I never merge the wrong PR.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Default is the active PR, never the stale one.
  - *Given* a session with `#11 merged` and `#12 open`, *When* `MergePR` is called with no `pr`, *Then* GitHub receives a merge for #12.
- Explicit PR must belong to the session.
  - *Given* the same session and `MergePR{pr: acme/app#99}`, *When* handled, *Then* the response is `FailedPrecondition` and GitHub is not called.
- Unset selector never means PR 0.
  - *Given* a request with `pr` unset and a session with no PRs, *When* handled, *Then* the response is `FailedPrecondition "no pull request"`, not a call for number 0.
- Create-PR is allowed after a merge.
  - *Given* `#11 merged` and branch `feat-b` with no PR, *When* `CreatePullRequest` runs with `reader:actions` on, *Then* it creates and attaches a new PR; with an open PR on `feat-b` it returns that PR as `ExistingPrUrl`.
- Pin, detach and manual attach.
  - *Given* a session with #11 and #12, *When* `PinSessionPullRequest{#11}` is called, *Then* `ActivePR` returns #11 until `PinSessionPullRequest{unset}`.
- Refresh covers unverified rows.
  - *Given* a session with a backfill-seeded unverified-terminal row #9 (`unknown`, terminal) and an open #12, *When* `RefreshSessionPullRequests` runs, *Then* both rows are re-polled and returned; *Given* a verified `merged` #11, *Then* it is not polled.
- Detach is reversible by attach.
  - *Given* `DetachSessionPullRequest{u1, #12}` followed by `AttachSessionPullRequest{u1, #12 URL}`, *When* branch discovery runs next, *Then* #12 is linked again (the manual attach cleared the tombstone) and no duplicate row exists.
- PR-mutating RPCs are not reachable from the dispatched-agent MCP surface.
  - *Given* the MCP tool registry, *When* the unreachable-list test runs, *Then* none of the five attach/detach/pin/refresh/item-detach RPC names is reachable from a tool.
- Detach removes exactly one link.
  - *Given* session `u1` linked to an `unknown` #12 and the item also holding a direct link to #12, *When* `DetachSessionPullRequest{u1, #12}` runs, *Then* only `u1`'s link is deleted, a `u1` tombstone is written in the same transaction, the item-direct link is untouched, and a repeat of branch discovery on `u1` does not recreate the link (this last clause is asserted in T3.3.1's test, which owns discovery).
**Files**: `server/services/github_service.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `server/services/pr_creation_service.go`, `server/review_queue_manager.go`, `server/services/session_service_pr_refs.go`

- Files: `server/services/session_service_pr_refs.go`, `server/services/github_service.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`

##### Task 4.2.3: Pin, detach, attach RPCs (M)
- `PinSessionPullRequest`, `RefreshSessionPullRequests` (the S2 header Refresh: awaits `InvalidateAndRefresh` for the session's OPEN, `unknown` and unverified-terminal rows (a seeded "Closed or merged (unverified)" row IS refreshable, so the S2 Refresh step works on it; a VERIFIED merged or closed row stays untouched and never spins), bounded 10 s, returns the refreshed list; Phase 4 N7, Triad iteration 1 UX 3. The only behavior change to T3.3.2: an unverified-terminal row is re-polled with `source=user_refresh` and the T1.3.2 transition table lets it move to open, merged or closed; a user refresh that fails keeps the row unverified and increments `poll_fail_count`; a successful fetch resolves the row and resets the counter, as any fetch does. Verified by `TestUpdatePullRequestState_ReconcilerRefreshOnUnverifiedTerminal`; a fetch never reports `unknown`, so no refresh leaves a row unverified with a zeroed counter), `DetachSessionPullRequest` (user "Not mine" and the exit for an `unknown` or stale-open PR; deletes that session's link and writes the session tombstone in ONE transaction; audit log line), `AttachSessionPullRequest` (user supplies a PR URL; verifies it exists through `NewConditionalRequest`, `source=manual`; clears the owner's tombstone; allowed on a session whose item is already `done`, which holds cleanup until the PR is terminal and does not reopen the item). There is no separate waive RPC: detach is the one exit. Handlers live in the NEW file `session_service_pr_refs.go`; `session_service_delegates.go` (973 lines) gets routing lines only. Extend the existing session service (a new service bumps a hard-coded count in `terminal_write_callers_test.go`). Pin undo stays client-side (re-send the previous pin state); no server undo RPC. Detach undo is also client-side: the toast's Undo calls `AttachSessionPullRequest` (`source=manual`), which clears the tombstone; the UI also offers "Track a PR by URL" with the same RPC (T6.2.6). Metric `ssq_pr_pin_total{action}` is emitted here. Caller identity (Triad iteration 1, engineering 6): these RPCs are UI-only. This task creates `server/mcp/tools_pr_rpc_unreachable_test.go`, which enumerates the MCP tool registry and fails if any tool name or handler routes to `PinSessionPullRequest`, `RefreshSessionPullRequests`, `DetachSessionPullRequest` or `AttachSessionPullRequest` (T4.2.7 appends `DetachItemPullRequest`), and T5.3.0 step 2 records where the Connect handlers are mounted relative to the MCP credential; if the MCP session credential is accepted by the Connect middleware these five RPCs also reject it server-side (a handler-level test with the MCP credential expects `PermissionDenied`). INFERRED until T5.3.0 reads the middleware. Classify all four in `server/services/terminal_write_rpcs_test.go` (`notTerminalWriteRPCs`); run `go test -tags sinkguard -short -run UIRpcSurface ./server/services`. No "gate" in test file names. The RPC-level assertions for the Story 1.3 and Story 1.5 tombstone cases live here (wave 2, Phase 4 B3): a user `AttachSessionPullRequest` clears the owner's tombstone and creates the link; `DetachSessionPullRequest(session, #30)` writes a session tombstone so the backfill does not recreate #30.

##### Task 4.2.7: Item-scoped detach RPC (S)
- Prerequisites (architecture C8): T4.2.3, T5.1.2, T5.2.3, T5.4.1, because its tests assert the verdict and the gate. `DetachItemPullRequest{item_id, pr}` on the existing session/backlog service (handler in `session_service_pr_refs.go`; same classification and registry steps as T4.2.3). It deletes the item-direct link if one exists and writes the item tombstone in one transaction; the tombstone also filters the state out of the derived item PR set (so a session-derived path to the same PR stops counting for the item's verdict) and stops Jules/manual re-attach. Its UI copy and undo are the same as the session detach (design/ux.md S3, UX-S3-8): the dialog says the PR will not be added back automatically; Undo re-attaches through the existing manual item-link RPC.

#### Story 4.3: Server readers (retention is the data-loss site)
**As an** operator, **I want** the retention sweeper and other readers to respect all PRs, **so that** a worktree is never deleted while a follow-up PR is open.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Retention reads `InstanceData` plus a bulk link query (the sweeper has no live `Instance`; VERIFIED `session_retention_sweeper.go:81, 132`).
  - *Given* archived `InstanceData` with no live instance, links `#11 merged terminal` and `#12 open`, *When* `SessionRetentionSweeper.baseSafeToDelete` runs, *Then* the session is not eligible; with both terminal it is eligible.
- Empty never means all-terminal.
  - *Given* archived `InstanceData` with `GitHubPRNumber=9`, `GitHubPRStatusTerminal=false` and no link rows (backfill skipped its host), *When* the sweeper runs, *Then* it is NOT eligible; *Given* the same with `GitHubPRStatusTerminal=true` and no rows, *Then* it is eligible only via the legacy check (the persisted flag), not via the empty row set; *Given* `GitHubPRNumber=0` and no rows, *Then* eligible.
- An `unknown` PR blocks deletion.
  - *Given* a link whose state is `unknown` and `terminal=false`, *When* the sweeper runs, *Then* the session is not eligible; *Given* a backfill-seeded state `unknown terminal=true` (unverified terminal), *Then* it blocks until verify is exhausted (a stale legacy flag must not make an unpolled PR look safe; adversarial C7), and does not block after exhaustion.
- An item-direct PR protects every session of the item (adversarial C2).
  - *Given* an archived work session past retention whose own links are all terminal (or absent with `GitHubPRNumber=0`) and an item with an open item-direct #22 (agent-reported, Jules or manual), *When* the sweeper runs, *Then* the session is NOT eligible; with #22 merged it is eligible; with the item lookup failing it is NOT eligible; a session with no `ItemSession` row is evaluated on its own links only. *Given* one session UUID with `ItemSession` rows for items X (terminal PRs) and Y (open item-direct #22), *Then* it is NOT eligible (every item of the session is checked, not only the newest, adversarial M1); *Given* an item with `pr_number=5`, no link anywhere and an empty `pr_url` (skip class `skipped_item_bad_ref`), *Then* its sessions are NOT eligible.
- The fail-closed behavior is not flag-gated (it only ever withholds a delete).
  - *Given* every `session_multi_pr` scope off and an open linked PR, *When* the sweeper runs, *Then* the session is not eligible.
- Approval gating uses the active PR.
  - *Given* `#11 merged` and `#12 open` with `check_conclusion=failure`, *When* the CI-failure approval gate runs, *Then* it reads #12's conclusion.
**Files**: `server/services/session_retention_sweeper.go`, `server/services/approval_service.go`, `server/services/approval_handler.go`, `server/services/debug_snapshot.go`, `server/services/unfinished_work_service.go`

##### Task 4.3.0: Bulk storage queries and guard helpers (M)
- `Storage.ListSessionPRViewsByUUIDs(uuids []string) (map[string][]PRView, error)`: one links query plus one states `IN (...)` query per call (`2 * ceil(n/500)` statements, chunked by the existing `itemSessionBatchChunk`, `session/storage_backlog.go:357`), returning row data (`PRView`), not snapshots, so it serves `InstanceData`-backed readers. Sibling `Storage.ListItemGuardViewsByItemIDs(itemIDs []string) (map[string]ItemGuardView, error)` with `ItemGuardView{Direct []PRView; LegacyUnrepresented bool}` serves the sweeper's step 4 and the gate's clauses (ii)/(iii) through ONE data path (architecture C5b): `Direct` is the chunked two-statement read over `backlog_item_id IN` with item tombstones removed; `LegacyUnrepresented` is computed from the item rows' `pr_number`/`pr_url` by T1.5.1's exported `ItemHasUnrepresentedLegacyPR`, only for items with `pr_number > 0` that no item-direct link represents, using the chunked `ListItemSessionsForItems`, `ListBySessionUUIDs` and bulk tombstone reads of T1.3.2. No skip rows are recorded: after a successful import the number is represented, so "unrepresented" is exactly "skipped or deferred", and it fails closed. Test with 200 uuids / 200 item ids (2 statements) and 1,001 (6) through the query-count hook. Also in this task (architecture C7, adversarial M1): `Storage.ListItemIDsBySessionUUIDs(uuids) (map[string][]string, error)` returning ALL distinct item ids per session uuid in one `item_session` query (VERIFIED the existing `ListItemSessionsBySessionUUIDs`, `storage_backlog.go:450-468`, keeps only the NEWEST row per uuid, so it cannot answer "every item of a session"), and the shared `LegacyOnlyPRSafe(d InstanceData) bool` in `session/pr_legacy_safe.go` (persisted terminal flag true -> safe, false -> not) plus `LegacyNumberRepresented(d InstanceData, rows []PRView) bool` (true when a link row carries `d`'s repo and number; architecture C5a) so T4.3.1 and T5.4.1 import it from a task that precedes both and can run in parallel. Predecessor of T4.3.1 and of the item cleanup gate (T5.4.1). Prerequisites: T1.3.2 and T1.5.1 (both wave 0: the repository reads and the exported unrepresented-legacy helper).
- Files: `session/ent_repository_session_pr.go`, `session/storage.go`, `session/pr_legacy_safe.go`, `session/ent_repository_session_pr_test.go`

##### Task 4.3.1: Retention sweeper all-terminal, fail closed (M)
- Test first (red), committed the way T1.3.4 does it (Phase 4 C5): the red output is recorded in the commit message and the tests land with `t.Skip("red until T4.3.1 implementation")` in the same push as the implementation that removes the skip (never pushed alone, so no later `go test ./server/services` is red). Tests run against `session.InstanceData` with no live instance, then `sweep()` builds one `map[uuid][]PRView` per cycle next to `byUUID` via T4.3.0, and `baseSafeToDelete` (`session_retention_sweeper.go:132`) evaluates this ORDERED decision list, first match wins:
  1. The session has NO link rows and `d.GitHubPRNumber == 0`: eligible (no PR at all).
  2. The session has NO link rows and `d.GitHubPRNumber != 0` (legacy number, no link): decided by the persisted legacy flag only. `d.GitHubPRStatusTerminal == true` -> eligible (the flag is the evidence, never the empty row set); `false` -> NOT safe. `PRsAllTerminal` is not called.
  3. The session has link rows: call `PRsAllTerminal(rows, legacyNumber)`; not safe if any linked state is non-terminal (including `unknown` with `terminal=false`); eligible otherwise, EXCEPT (architecture C5a) when `d.GitHubPRNumber > 0` and `LegacyNumberRepresented(d, rows)` is false (a rollback or skipped legacy number the links do not carry, e.g. links {#11 terminal} and legacy #12): then the session is eligible only if `LegacyOnlyPRSafe(d)` is true; test first: links {#11 merged}, legacy #12, flag false -> NOT eligible. (The legacy number is passed only so a caller that accidentally hands an empty slice still gets "not terminal"; step 3 never receives an empty slice.)
  4. ITEM-LEVEL CHECK (adversarial C2, DATA LOSS): after steps 1-3 say "eligible", the session is still NOT eligible if it belongs to an item (every item, via `ListItemIDsBySessionUUIDs`, one batched call per sweep) whose item-direct links (T4.3.0 sibling, item tombstones removed) contain a non-terminal state, or if that lookup errors (fail closed). "Belongs to an item" means EVERY item of the session (`ListItemIDsBySessionUUIDs`, T4.3.0; not the newest-only `ListItemSessionsBySessionUUIDs`), and an item with `pr_number > 0`, no link of the item or its sessions and a skip-class ref (`skipped_item_bad_ref`) also makes the session not eligible (fail closed, Known Risk K6; read from `ItemGuardView.LegacyUnrepresented`, T4.3.0, which also covers items deferred by the per-boot cap). This is clause (ii) of the cleanup gate applied to the sweeper; without it an archived work session past retention is deleted with its worktree while the item's agent-reported, Jules or manual PR is open (VERIFIED: the current `baseSafeToDelete` reads only `d.GitHubPRNumber`/the flag, `session_retention_sweeper.go:124-139`, and `DeleteSession` then `Destroy` removes the worktree). A session that belongs to no item skips this step. Test FIRST (red): archived work session, own links terminal or absent, item with open item-direct #22 -> not eligible; #22 merged -> eligible; lookup error -> not eligible.
  The cases are tested in this order against `InstanceData` with no live instance, so the legacy flag check cannot be bypassed by the empty-slice rule. Step 2 and gate clause (iii) are one shared helper `LegacyOnlyPRSafe(d InstanceData)` (defined in T4.3.0, `session/pr_legacy_safe.go`; persisted terminal flag true -> safe, false -> not), so the sweeper and the gate cannot disagree about "legacy number, no link" (architecture C5; the flag is evidence only, the same exposure as today). Not behind the flag. The same bulk map is passed to the shared-worktree sibling evaluation. The persisted terminal flag the legacy rule reads is kept honest by T2.3.3.
- Files: `server/services/session_retention_sweeper.go`, `server/services/session_retention_sweeper_test.go`

### Epic 5: Backlog multi-PR handling
**Goal**: An item with 2+ PRs reaches done by the owner's rule and is not stuck on an old PR; cleanup waits for all PRs.

#### Story 5.1: Pin current behavior and add the seam
**As a** maintainer, **I want** characterization tests and a `PRSetReader` seam that derives the item's PR set at read time, **so that** `backlog_lifecycle_pr.go` is not restructured and no PR fact is stored twice (no session-to-item mirror).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Single-PR behavior is unchanged with the flag off.
  - *Given* a `pr_pending` item with `pr_number=21` whose PR is merged, *When* `ReconcilePRPending` runs flag-off, *Then* the item moves to `done` exactly as today.
- Seam falls back.
  - *Given* flag on and an item with legacy `pr_number=21` that no link of the item or of any of its sessions represents (the "unrepresented legacy item number" of the Migration Plan), *When* `itemPRs(item)` is called, *Then* it returns one synthesized PR snapshot for #21 with state `unknown` (not terminal), so an unmigrated item is never read as "all PRs terminal". *Given* `pr_number=30` where #30 is represented only by a SUPERSEDED round's session link, *Then* nothing is synthesized (the mirror value is stale, not unmigrated). *Given* `pr_number=30` where #30 carries a tombstone of the item OR of any of the item's sessions (for example `DetachSessionPullRequest` of a session-only #30, which leaves the stored number in place), *Then* nothing is synthesized and the next boot's backfill creates no item-direct link (architecture C1). *Given* `pr_number` matching a `PRRef` already in the derived set, *Then* the fallback returns immediately without running the all-rounds link query (short-circuit; query-count hook). *Given* a retention-swept session whose tombstones were deleted with it and `pr_number` still set on an `archived` item, *Then* the fallback or the next boot re-imports it as `unknown` (Known Risk K9, benign for `done`).
- Distinct PRs, one state.
  - *Given* #21 linked directly to the item and to the item's latest work session, *When* `itemPRs(item)` is called, *Then* it returns exactly one PR (one `PullRequestState`) and "k of N" counts it once.
- The set is derived, never stored.
  - *Given* #30 reaches the item only through the latest work session `s1`, *When* `s1`'s link is detached (or the session is deleted), *Then* the next `itemPRs` read no longer contains #30 and no other row had to change.
- Current round = the latest `work` session by `created_at`.
  - *Given* round 1 work session `s1` with an open #30 and the item reopened to round 2 work session `s2` (created later) with #31, *When* `itemPRs(item)` runs, *Then* it contains #31 and not #30; #30 stays linked to `s1`, is still polled, and still holds `s1`'s worktree (Story 5.4); if #31 merges the verdict is `Done` regardless of #30.
  - *Given* work session `w1` that ended normally (`ended_at` set) with #30 linked, followed by its own `review` session `r1` created later, *When* `itemPRs` runs, *Then* #30 still counts (only a later WORK session supersedes; `ended_at` is never read).
  - *Given* two work sessions with identical `created_at`, *Then* the larger `id` wins (deterministic, not semantic), and a fixture with `started_at` NULL on every row gives the same answer.
  - A detached link does not resurrect an earlier round (adversarial C6). *Given* round 1 `s1` with MERGED #30 (session-only) and round 2 `s2` (latest) whose only link, an open #31 of a wrongly discovered PR, the user detaches (`s2` now has no link but has a session tombstone), *When* `itemPRs` runs and `ReconcilePRPending` evaluates the verdict, *Then* #30 does NOT re-enter the set, the set is empty (`NoPRs` / stuck `PRPendingNoPR`) and the `pr_pending` item is NOT completed on #30; *Given* `s2` that has never linked and has no tombstone, *Then* #30 stays in the set (the C4 guard below). *Given* `s2` is deleted outright, *Then* the fallback applies and logs `item current round fell back to earlier session` (Known Risk K7).
  - A live PR is never hidden by a newer work session that has no link yet (adversarial C4). *Given* round 1 work session `s1` linking open #30 (session-only), *When* a newer work `ItemSession` `s2` is created by `AttachSessionToItem` or a rework respawn and has no link yet, *Then* `itemPRs` still returns #30 and the verdict is `Open`, not `NoPRs`; *When* `s2` links #31, *Then* #30 leaves the set; *When* instead `s2` is detached from the item before it links anything, *Then* #30 stays.
  - The common case, an item-direct first PR then rework (adversarial C3). *Given* #30 reported by the agent (`report_pr_created`, item-direct, never superseded) and a round-2 work session linking #31, *When* #31 merges and #30 is still open, *Then* the item stays `pr_pending` and the stuck report names #30 with `DetachItemPullRequest` as the exit; *When* #30 is closed unmerged or detached, *Then* the verdict is `Done`. *Given* rework reuses the same branch so round 2 links the SAME #30, *Then* the set is {#30}.
  - `item.pr_number` follows the set (T5.2.3). *Given* #30 reachable only through the current round's session link and `pr_number=30`, *When* the boot backfill runs twice and a later work session links #31, *Then* no item-direct link for #30 exists after either run, #30 leaves the set, and the next reconcile tick sets `pr_number=31`. *Given* a SUPERSEDED round's #30 still in `pr_number` while the new round has no link yet, *When* the backfill runs, *Then* no item-direct link is created.
- A session tombstone does not hide a PR another live contributor holds (architecture C4).
  - *Given* rework rounds `s1` (older) and `s2` (current) both linking the SAME open #30 and the repository `DeleteSessionPR(s1, #30)` (what `DetachSessionPullRequest` wraps, T4.2.3; an `s1` tombstone, `s1`'s link deleted), *When* `itemPRs` runs, *Then* #30 stays in the set via `s2`'s live link and the verdict is `Open`; *Given* #30 held by an item-direct link AND a session link and only the session detaches, *Then* #30 stays via the item-direct link; *Given* the repository `DeleteItemPR` for #30 (an ITEM tombstone; the RPC `DetachItemPullRequest` is asserted in T4.2.7), *Then* it leaves the set even though `s2` still links it (explicit operator choice, T4.2.7). The legacy-fallback, backfill-candidate and `pr_number` recompute tests keep the wider item-OR-any-session tombstone rule.
- A reused branch needs no special rule.
  - *Given* round 2's session links the SAME state #30 that round 1's link points at, *When* the verdict runs with #30 open, *Then* #30 counts via round 2's link and the item stays `pr_pending`.
- Detach and item tombstones act on exactly the link they name.
  - *Given* #30 `unknown` reachable both through an item-direct link and through the current round's session link, *When* the repository item detach `DeleteItemPR(item, #30)` (wrapped by the RPC `DetachItemPullRequest`, T4.2.7) runs, *Then* the item-direct link is deleted, an item tombstone is written, and #30 leaves the item set (the tombstone filters the session-derived path too); the session link remains.
  - *Given* #30 reachable only through the session link, *When* the repository `DeleteSessionPR(s, #30)` runs, *Then* #30 leaves the item set unless an item-direct link exists, and nothing else keeps the state "live".
- A manual attach on an unrelated session does not feed the item.
  - *Given* a session not in the item's `ItemSession` rows and not linked to the item, *When* the user attaches #50 to that session, *Then* `itemPRs(item)` is unchanged.
- A broken session link does not hide item-direct PRs.
  - *Given* the item's session link is broken (memory `instinct_backlog_session_link_broken_mid_review`) and an item-direct link to #21, *Then* `itemPRs` still returns #21.
RPC boundary (Phase 4 B3, C1): T5.1.x assert detach through the repository variants of T1.3.2 (`DeleteSessionPR`, `DeleteItemPR`); the RPC-level assertions live in T4.2.3 and T4.2.7, and T4.2.7 already depends on T5.1.2, so no cycle.
**Files**: `session/backlog_item_prs.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr_characterization_test.go`

##### Task 5.1.1: Characterization tests (M)
- Pin `ReconcilePRPending`, `reconcileBouncingItems` (`backlog_lifecycle.go:2058-2082`), `reconcilePRPendingWithoutPRItems`, `FindPRPendingItems`; no wall-clock thresholds (injected clock).
- Files: `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr_characterization_test.go`

##### Task 5.1.2: `PRSetReader` and `itemPRs` (M)
- Prerequisites: T5.1.1, T1.3.2, T1.5.1. Interface in `session/` (consumer side). Implementation = the Item PR set glossary rule, computed per call from `ListByItem(itemID)` plus `ListBySessionUUIDs([latestWorkSession])` (T1.3.2) minus the item's tombstones, deduplicated by state id. Current round: among `ListItemSessions` rows with `Role == work`, sort by `CreatedAt` desc then `ID` desc and walk them newest first with the glossary rule: skip a session with no link and no session tombstone, stop at the first with a link (the round) or with a tombstone and no link (the round, contributing nothing), so a detach cannot resurrect an earlier round (one `ListBySessionUUIDs` plus one `ListSessionTombstonesByUUIDs` call (T1.3.2) over all work-session uuids, at most 3 statements); none qualifying means only item-direct links; log `item current round fell back to earlier session` whenever the walk skips a session. The reader's set subtracts the item's OWN tombstones only (architecture C4: a session tombstone means that session's link is already deleted and must not hide a PR another live contributor holds); the wider `PRCoveredByTombstone` test (item OR any of its sessions) is used by the T1.5.1 candidate rule, the legacy fallback and the T5.2.3 recompute (one shared helper in `session_pr_backfill.go`). No new column, no stored flag, no writer. Legacy fallback returns one `unknown`-state PR from `pr_number` only for an unrepresented, untombstoned legacy item number with a buildable ref (helper and item URL parser defined in T1.5.1, shared with the backfill candidate rule); it short-circuits when `pr_number` already matches a `PRRef` in the derived set. Do not depend on a live session link (memory `instinct_backlog_session_link_broken_mid_review`): the `backlog_item_id` route works with the link broken.
- Files: `session/backlog_item_prs.go`, `session/storage_backlog.go`

##### Task 5.1.3: Round-membership tests across the reopen paths (M)
- One test per path that creates a later work `ItemSession` (reopen/rework respawn at `backlog_service_triage.go:1261`, stale respawn, bounce, `AttachSessionToItem` at `backlog_service_sync.go:111`) asserting (a) the earlier round's PR STAYS in the set until the new session has a link, (b) leaves once it does, with no call to any "abandon" step (none exists), (c) does NOT return when the new session's only link is detached (session tombstone); one asserting a work-then-review sequence keeps the PR; one for the item-direct first-PR-then-rework case and one for the boot backfill case (no item-direct link is created for a session-derived PR; the `pr_number` RECOMPUTE is asserted in T5.2.3, which depends on this task; architecture C3d), run against an item whose only PR is session-derived. `ended_at` writers (`storage_backlog.go:592, 612`) are deliberately not part of the rule, so no enumeration of them is needed.
- Files: `session/backlog_item_prs_test.go`, `server/services/backlog_service_triage_test.go`

#### Story 5.2: Reconcilers use the verdict
**As an** operator, **I want** an item to finish when at least one PR merged and none are open, **so that** an old merged PR neither completes it early nor strands it.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Old merged PR plus new open PR keeps `pr_pending`.
  - *Given* an item with `acme/app#21 merged` and `#22 open`, *When* `ReconcilePRPending` runs with `reader:backlog` on, *Then* the item stays `pr_pending` and #22 is checked.
- Done rule.
  - *Given* `#21 merged` and `#22 closed` unmerged, *When* reconcile runs, *Then* the item goes `done`; *Given* only `#22 closed`, *Then* it does not go `done` and falls into the existing superseded/closed handling (`closeIfSupersededByMain`).
- Empty set is stuck.
  - *Given* a `pr_pending` item with no rows and `pr_number=0`, *When* the stuck detector runs, *Then* `StuckReasonPRPendingNoPR` is raised (unchanged semantics, condition = empty set).
- Item scalars remain a mirror.
  - *Given* rows #21 merged, #22 open, *When* the item PR set changes, *Then* `item.pr_number` is 22 and `FindPRPendingItems` (`PrNumberGT(0)`) still returns the item. The recompute happens in the writer's statement for item-direct changes and at the end of the next `ReconcilePRPending` pass for session-link changes (T5.2.3); an empty set never zeroes the stored number.
- A pass that refreshes a closed row defers closed handling (architecture C3).
  - *Given* an item whose only PR #22 is `closed` in the row but merged on GitHub, *When* `ReconcilePRPending` runs, *Then* it awaits the `reconciler_refresh`, the row becomes `merged` and the item goes `done` in that pass with no `closeIfSupersededByMain` call; *Given* the refresh times out, *Then* nothing closes or archives in that pass.
- A merged PR does not complete an item whose agent is still working (Phase 4 pre-mortem #4; N and the hold are owner-confirmed, Owner confirmations pending #2).
  - *Given* an item `pr_pending` whose only PR #21 is merged (verdict `Done`) and whose current-round work session is unended and was active 2 minutes ago (injected clock, `liveWorkIdleWindow` 10 minutes), *When* `ReconcilePRPending` runs, *Then* the item stays `pr_pending` with reason `merged, session still active`, no `CleanupTerminalItem` and no archival run, and the pane and worktree are intact; *When* the session has been idle longer than the window or has ended, *Then* the next pass moves the item to `done`. *Given* the live-work lookup errors, *Then* the item is held (no transition) and the error is logged. *Given* the session attaches a second PR #22 after #21 merged, *Then* the verdict is `Open` and the item was never `done`.
- A never-idle session cannot block done forever (Triad iteration 1, engineering 3).
  - *Given* verdict `Done` with the first merge 61 minutes ago (injected clock, `liveWorkMaxHold` 1 hour) and a work session that is still live (active 1 minute ago), *When* `ReconcilePRPending` runs, *Then* the item moves to `done`, a warning log and one notification are emitted, and (Triad iteration 2, VERIFIED contradiction fixed) the live session is NOT archived and its tmux pane is NOT killed, `CleanupTerminalItem` reports the worktree `Blocked` (live work) and no worktree is removed; the item detail shows the warning-style MAX_HOLD line. *Given* the same with the first merge 59 minutes ago, *Then* the item stays `pr_pending`.
  - *Given* that done item with the live session still active, *When* the 60 s `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` sweep and the 60 s `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` sweep run, *Then* neither archives, kills nor removes anything; Neither sweep settles the item while the session is skipped (`reconcileTerminalItemSessions` sets `clean=false` for a RULE L skip, so `terminalSettled.settle` is not called and the item is re-read every 60 s pass); *When* the session has been idle past the window or has ended, *Then* the very next 60 s pass (NOT the pass after the 10-minute `terminalSweepSettleTTL`) archives it, kills its pane (a no-op if ended) and removes the worktree.
- The second merge-to-done path honors the same guard (Triad iteration 2). Cost rule (review C4): `reconcileBouncingItems` consults the item's rows and the local probe BEFORE `IsPRMerged`, so a held item (rows `Done`, session live) costs no GitHub call per pass; the guard is consulted again after head-branch verification when the rows lagged.
  - *Given* an `in_progress` or `review` item that `reconcileBouncingItems` finds with a merged PR (`IsPRMerged` true, head branch verified) and a current-round work session live (active 2 minutes ago), *When* the pass runs, *Then* no `transitionBouncingItemToDone`, no recorded PASS verdict, no cleanup and no archival run, the item is not flagged bouncing, and the next pass re-evaluates; *When* the session is idle past the window or ended, *Then* it moves to `done` as today; *Given* the first merge is older than `liveWorkMaxHold`, *Then* it moves to `done` with the same warning and notification as `ReconcilePRPending`; *Given* a probe error, *Then* it is held. (The branch of the same function that finds a commit shipped to main WITHOUT a PR is unchanged: it has no merge time to hold against, and the cleanup filter below still protects a live session on that path.)
**Files**: `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`, `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, `session/ent_repository_backlog.go`

##### Task 5.2.1: Live-work probe, `heldByLiveWork` helper and merge-to-done characterization (M)
- Prerequisites: T1.2.3, T5.1.2 (split from the old T5.2.1 in Triad iteration 3 so each half is M). FIRST STEP (Phase 4 pre-mortem #4): read today's whole merge-to-done path (`backlog_lifecycle_pr.go` from the `IsPRMerged` call at ~1662 through the done transition and `cleanupTerminalItemSync`) and record in the PR what it does with a live work session (VERIFIED for ~1655-1700: nothing; pin it with a characterization test beside T5.1.1's). Then implement, in this task and without changing any reconciler's behavior yet: (1) the real `LiveWorkProbe` in `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, from the instance `Snapshot()`, wired into the lifecycle listener (a setter in `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`; a nil probe means today's behavior); (2) the single guard helper `heldByLiveWork(ctx, itemID, prs) (held bool, maxHoldExpired bool)` in `session/backlog_lifecycle_live_work.go` (held and maxHoldExpired are mutually exclusive): it takes the item's newest work session, asks the probe whether it is live, holds on a probe error from a non-real probe, and reports `maxHoldExpired` from `LiveWorkHoldExpired` (T1.2.3); EXPIRY WINS over an error hold. Off unless `reader:backlog` is on and a probe is wired (R3). The helper only decides; the callers (T5.2.4 for `ReconcilePRPending`, T5.2.2 for `reconcileBouncingItems`) act on it, and the guard sits in the verdict consumer, never in `ItemPRVerdict`. Test U-152. Code read for the probe (Triad iteration 1, engineering 10; VERIFIED by reading `session/instance_snapshot.go` and `session/instance_status.go`): `InstanceSnapshot` carries `Status` (`BasicStatus` is Creating, Active, Paused, Stopped, Hibernated), `LastMeaningfulChangeAt time.Time`, `TurnEnded{Excerpt, Source, At}` and `ArchivedAt *time.Time` (`instance_snapshot.go:98, 154, 160-161, 261`). Mapping (owner rules R1/R2): ended = status Paused, Stopped or Hibernated, or `ArchivedAt != nil`; Working = an unended Creating or Active session; idle = `now - LastMeaningfulChangeAt >= liveWorkIdleWindow`. `TurnEnded` IS used, precisely: it is true when a turn end was recorded and nothing meaningful changed after it (`Instance.TurnEnded.At` is set and `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` is not after it; `RecordTurnEnd` touches `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` to the same instant, so later output, an approval or a status change makes the session Working again). A Working, mid-turn session is live regardless of the idle window, but capped: with no meaningful change for `liveWorkWorkingCap` (1 hour, injected clock) it is NOT live, counted as `ssq_pr_live_work_hold_total{result=cap_working}` and logged. The probe returns `(false, nil)` for an absent, deleted, archived or ended session; only a transient storage failure is an error and counts as live, capped at 1 hour from its first failure (then not live, `result=cap_error`, logged); the probe never returns an error itself, so every consumer sees the capped answer. The live-work subject is the item's NEWEST work session (not the PR round walk, whose skip of link-less sessions is right for PR attribution and wrong for liveness: the agent still working has usually not opened a PR yet). `LiveWorkProbe` has no existing implementation (grep for `LiveWorkProbe`/`IsLiveWork` finds only this plan); NOT verified: whether `LastMeaningfulChangeAt` advances on terminal output of a long silent tool run (if it does not, a working agent can look idle after 10 minutes; the worker records that in the PR and the owner decision #2 covers it). The maximum hold itself (`liveWorkMaxHold`, default 1 hour, a constant overridable in `config.json`, not an env var, measured from the FIRST merge among the item's merged PRs, never the latest, so a chain of follow-up PRs cannot extend it; a merged row without `merged_at` uses `last_polled_at`, and with neither the first time the hold was observed, so a missing timestamp cannot hold forever; `LiveWorkHoldStart`) is T1.2.3's `LiveWorkHoldExpired`; the warning, metric and notification it triggers are T5.2.4's.
- Files: `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go`

##### Task 5.2.4: `ReconcilePRPending` per PR with the live-work hold and maximum hold (M)
- Prerequisites: T1.2.3, T3.2.1, T3.2.5, T3.3.2 (architecture C8), T5.1.2, T5.2.1. Add the LIVE-WORK GUARD to the verdict consumer, not to `ItemPRVerdict`: when the verdict is `Done`, call `heldByLiveWork` (T5.2.1); if held, leave the item `pr_pending` with reason `merged, session still active` and write nothing else (the next pass re-evaluates); a probe error also holds; a nil probe or `reader:backlog` off means today's behavior (no hold, no verdict). Loop `itemPRs`; `IsPRMerged`/`GetPRStatus` per open PR; head-branch verification (~1632-1702) per PR; transition by `ItemPRVerdict`. IMPLEMENTED SUBSET (Wave 2 slot A part 2): with `reader:backlog` on, once `IsPRMerged` says the tracked (active) PR merged and head-branch verification passed, the reconciler awaits a `reconciler_refresh` of that PR's row (bounded 10 s, `PRRowRefresher`, wired to the poller's `Refresh`), reads the verdict from the rows only, stays `pr_pending` unless it is `Done`, then applies the live-work hold. CI/review/conflict handling still acts on the mirror's active PR rather than looping every open PR (the mirror recompute keeps that PR the open one). Extract before editing any function over the gocognit limit. Single truth source (architecture C7): the verdict is computed ONLY from state rows, which only the poll core writes (I1). A merge the reconciler observes through `IsPRMerged` is a TRIGGER, not an input: it calls `InvalidateAndRefresh(PRRef)` (T3.3.2), which is AWAITED (bounded 10s) so the poll core has written the row before the pass re-reads it; if the wait times out or fails the item transitions on the next pass (safe direction: later, never earlier), and a test pins that the reconciler never overlays its own observation on the verdict. Closed-then-merged is preserved WITHOUT a same-pass race (architecture C3): when the verdict would be `AllClosedUnmerged`, the pass first calls `InvalidateAndRefresh(ref, source=reconciler_refresh)` once for each `closed` row (T1.3.2 transition table allows only `closed -> merged` from this source), re-reads the item's PR set and recomputes the verdict, and runs `closeIfSupersededByMain`/archive handling ONLY on that recomputed verdict; a refresh that timed out, failed or was dropped (GitHub says `open`; reopen is webhook-only) counts as HELD for this pass (no closed handling at all). Tests: PR closed, then merged on GitHub with no webhook -> item reaches `done` in the SAME pass; refresh times out -> no `closeIfSupersededByMain`, no archive, item unchanged, next pass retries; refresh returns merged while another PR is open -> `Open`. Live-work cases: see the Story 5.2 AC (held while active, released when idle or ended, held on probe error, released after the maximum hold). Maximum hold: `liveWorkMaxHold` (default 1 hour, a constant overridable in `config.json`, not an env var) measured from the first merge among the item's merged PRs (`LiveWorkHoldStart` then `LiveWorkHoldExpired`, T1.2.3); once expired the pass lets the verdict `Done` proceed (expiry wins over a probe error), logs a warning, increments `ssq_pr_live_work_hold_total{result=max_hold}`, sends one `notify()` (deduplicated per item) ("session still active 1 hour after merge; item marked done, local files kept until the session ends") and the item detail shows the MAX_HOLD line (design/ux.md); the live session itself is protected by RULE L (T5.4.6), so the escape for a never-idle session is bounded for the item and explicit (End session) for the pane and worktree.
- RULE L tests committed here (Triad iteration 3): the archive and pane-kill assertions of the Story 5.2 max-hold and sweep ACs and of I-117, I-130, I-131 and I-135 are written now against the real functions with recording fakes and committed with `t.Skip("red until T5.4.6")` (the T1.3.4 convention); T5.4.6 removes the skips. The hold, warning, notification and metric assertions run in this task.
- Files: `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go`

##### Task 5.2.2: Bouncing and stuck detectors (S)
- `reconcileBouncingItems` uses the verdict and the SAME live-work guard as T5.2.1 (Triad iteration 2: VERIFIED `session/backlog_lifecycle.go:2060-2090` scans `in_progress`/`review` items, where a live work session is the normal case, calls `IsPRMerged` and then `transitionBouncingItemToDone`, which records a PASS verdict, moves the item to done and runs `cleanupTerminalItemSync`, so it can fire for an item with a live session). The guard is one helper (`heldByLiveWork(ctx, item) (held bool, maxHoldExpired bool)`, defined in T5.2.1 and called by both reconcilers) consulted after the head-branch verification and before `transitionBouncingItemToDone`: held means `continue` (no verdict recorded, no bouncing flag, re-evaluated next pass); max hold expired lets the transition run with the warning and notification. Test row I-132. `reconcilePRPendingWithoutPRItems` condition = empty set; `FindPRPendingItems` unchanged. An item whose only blocker is an `unknown` PR is reported by the stuck detector with the PR named and the owner actions (detach), naming BOTH detaches when a session link also holds it (item detach alone filters the verdict but a session link still holds that session's worktree, adversarial M5). The report also lists (this part needs T5.4.2's blocked set and lands after it) done/archived items whose cleanup is `Blocked` by an open PR, with the holding owner and how long it has been blocked (adversarial M4; reads the same blocked set as `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items`). `server/mcp/tools_stuck_report.go:70,189,200` reads the set via `itemPRs` (ratchet entry flips to `via-itemPRs`, T2.2.5).
- Files: `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go`, `server/mcp/tools_stuck_report.go`

##### Task 5.2.3: Atomic mirror update (S)
- The CAS UPDATE at `ent_repository_backlog.go:2012-2041` writes status plus the active PR's `pr_url/pr_number` together; keep that atomicity (no reader may see `pr_pending` with `pr_number=0`).
- WHEN `item.pr_number`/`pr_url` (the `ActivePR` mirror of the derived item set) is recomputed: (1) in the same statement as any item-writer change to the set (`setPRAndTransition`, item-direct attach or detach, Jules attach); (2) at the end of each `ReconcilePRPending` pass per item (it already loops `itemPRs`; a session-link change such as discovery on the current round lands here within one tick, no new listener). Rule: write only if `ActivePR(itemPRs)` is non-nil and differs; an EMPTY set (including a set emptied by a detach whose tombstone covers the stored `pr_number`) leaves the stored value untouched and ignored (the tombstone exclusion above stops it being re-synthesized or re-imported) (a round whose PR is not linked yet must not zero a `pr_pending` item's number). The mirror is never read as input by the set derivation or the backfill (the unrepresented-legacy-number rule exists precisely so a stale mirror is not re-imported).
- This task owns the recompute helper; T5.3.1, T5.3.3 and T5.3.4 (the item-direct writers) call it in their own statements.
- Files: `session/ent_repository_backlog.go`

#### Story 5.3: Writers attach instead of reassigning
**As an** agent, **I want** `report_pr_created` for a second PR to add it, **so that** follow-up PRs are recorded without an override.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Additive report.
  - *Given* an item `pr_pending` with `#21 merged` and flag on, *When* `report_pr_created{pr_number:22, pr_url:.../pull/22}` is called by the item's linked session, *Then* #22 is attached as `followup`, the item stays `pr_pending`, and no `override_reason` is required (head-branch verification still applies as today).
- Correction path unchanged.
  - *Given* `replaces_pr_number:21` with override reason, author verified and #21 not merged, *When* called, *Then* the old reassignment behavior runs; with #21 merged it is refused (`ErrPRReassignmentNotAllowed`).
- Caller identity (test written first, Task 5.3.0).
  - *Given* a dispatched agent whose session is not linked to item X, *When* it calls `report_pr_created` for X, *Then* the handler rejects it server-side regardless of `--allowedTools`; *Given* the linked session, *Then* it is accepted.
- Jules.
  - *Given* a completed Jules session with `Outputs` containing PRs #31 and #32, *When* `applyCompletedState` runs, *Then* two item-direct links exist with `attach_source=jules` and the `jules multiple pr outputs` warning is gone.
- BUG-058 stance (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-058-pushAndCreatePR-non-atomic-pr-field-write-and-transition.md`): this plan NARROWS it and leaves it open.
  - *Given* `pushAndCreatePR` or `shipViaAgentOrFallback` creates a PR, *When* the PR-field write runs, *Then* the `pr_number`/`pr_url` write and the item-direct link plus state row upsert are one transaction (a forced failure of either leaves neither, and the item stays in `review`); the separate `resolveToPRPending` transition stays a second call (the window where `pr_number` is set while status is still `review`, which no detector alerts on, is unchanged), so BUG-058 is not closed; a test pins that `pr_pending` never has an empty link set and `pr_number=0`.
- Item-direct link is written with the flag OFF (architecture C6).
  - *Given* every scope off and a `pr_pending` item, *When* `report_pr_created{pr_number:22,...}` runs, *Then* an item-direct link and a state row for #22 exist, the refresher polls #22, and the gate and sweeper step 4 treat the item as holding an open #22; the legacy reassign rules are unchanged. (Assertion ownership, Phase 4 C3: the refresher half is T3.2.5's fixture, the sweeper half T4.3.1's and the gate half T5.4.1's; T5.3.1 depends on all three.)

Item-direct link writes are UNCONDITIONAL (architecture C6): every item PR writer (report, the `replaces_pr_number` correction, Jules, manual link, `pushAndCreatePR`, `RecordPRCreatedOutOfBand`) upserts the item-direct link (`EnsureState` + link, tombstone-checked) in the same transaction as its `pr_number` write whether or not the flag is on, and the correction path also deletes the item-direct link of the replaced number (no tombstone).
