#### Story 5.4: One cleanup gate at the worktree-removal choke point, re-run, and progress sync
**As an** operator, **I want** every backlog-driven path that removes a worktree to wait while a PR tied to that worktree is not terminal, and to run again when the last one is, **so that** no worktree is deleted under an open PR and none leaks afterwards.

Path inventory (VERIFIED by grep of definitions and callers). Scope: BACKLOG-DRIVEN and AUTOMATED removal. All of paths 1-6 reach worktree removal only through ONE function, `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` (`server/services/backlog_service.go:1357`; `cleanupItemWorktrees` is its one-line wrapper, `:1339`), so the gate is called once, at the top of that function, and no entry point can skip it. Other removers also exist (`Instance.Destroy`, `Instance.Pause`, start-failure rollback, the session stopper); rows 9-14 classify them so the inventory is complete (headline claim, stated precisely: no AUTOMATED path deletes a worktree under a non-terminal PR; rows 10-14 are explicit operator or agent actions that warn instead of block):

| # | Path | Call site | Removes worktree? | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Manual archive RPC | `backlog_service_lifecycle.go:547` -> `cleanupItemWorktrees` -> `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` | yes | choke point |
| 2 | Inline terminal-transition cleanup | `backlog_service_lifecycle.go:1063` -> `CleanupTerminalItem` (`backlog_service.go:1420`) -> `cleanupItemWorktrees` | yes | choke point |
| 3 | `WorktreeCleaner` hook | `session/backlog_lifecycle.go:811` `cleanupTerminalItemSync` -> `CleanupTerminalItem`; callers `backlog_lifecycle_pr.go:646, 1761, 2414` and `backlog_lifecycle.go:2002` | yes | choke point, via `CleanupTerminalItem` |
| 4 | 60s worktree safety net | `backlog_lifecycle_archive.go` `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` (~178-215) -> `cleanupTerminalItemSync` | yes | choke point; must NOT call `terminalSettled.settle` when blocked (else a blocked item is not retried until the settle TTL) |
| 5 | Reopen / rework | `backlog_service_triage.go:1294` `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept(priorSessions, worktreePath)` | yes (prior rounds) | choke point; a prior round's own live PR keeps that round's worktree (behavior change, safe direction; Unresolved Questions) |
| 6 | Tombstone orphan work sessions | `backlog_service_triage.go:3678` `cleanupItemWorktrees(freed)` | yes | choke point |
| 7 | Session retention sweeper | `server/services/session_retention_sweeper.go:132` | yes (via `DeleteSession`) | T4.3.1 (own data shape; the session's own links PLUS the item-direct links of its item, step 4, adversarial C2) |
| 8 | Session archival (archive + kill tmux pane) | `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` (`backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:115`), `archiveItemWorkSessions` (`backlog_service.go:1448`; also `backlog_service_triage.go:1301, 3564`) | NO (worktree and branch survive) | deliberately ungated by open PRs (OOM 2026-07-29; Owner confirmations pending #4); EXCEPT a live current-round work session is skipped, and the sweep leaves that item unsettled so it retries every pass (RULE L, T5.4.6) |
| 9 | `DeleteSession` -> `Instance.Destroy` by the retention sweeper | `session_retention_sweeper.go` | yes | gated by T4.3.1 (the sweeper decides before calling) |
| 10 | `DeleteSession` / `Instance.Destroy` / `Instance.Pause` by an explicit user RPC | `session_service_*` handlers | yes | INTENTIONALLY UNGATED: an explicit, named user action on one session; the UI confirm dialog and the MCP response warn when an open PR exists (T5.4.5). The gate exists to stop automation destroying work, not to veto the operator |
| 11 | MCP `pause_session` / `destroy`/stop tools an agent can call | `server/mcp/tools_lifecycle.go:410` (pause), `:510` (`inst.Destroy()`) | yes | INTENTIONALLY UNGATED but not silent: already protected by `refuseIfWorktreeSharedWithOtherLiveSession`; T5.4.5 makes the tool result list open/`unknown` PRs of the target session and logs it. Escalate to a hard gate only if the soak shows an agent doing this (owner decision in Unresolved Questions) |
| 12 | Diagnose flows | `session_service_diagnose.go:172, 239` | yes (INFERRED from the adversarial review, not opened) | INTENTIONALLY UNGATED: explicit operator-initiated action; worker confirms in T5.4.5 and downgrades to "gated" if the call turns out to be automatic |
| 13 | Start-failure rollback `Cleanup` | `session/instance.go:1816, 2058, 2490, 2517` | yes (the worktree this start just created) | INTENTIONALLY UNGATED: no PR can be attached to a worktree the failing start just created; gating would leak half-created worktrees |
| 14 | `stopLiveWorkAndReviewSessions` (adversarial C1): operator force-reset and backward-to-ready | `backlog_service_triage.go:1342-1366` -> `sessionStopper.StopSessionByUUID` (`session_service_liveness.go:179-190`) -> `Kill` = `Destroy` -> `CleanupWorktree` (refuses only a dirty worktree, `instance.go:2270-2287`); callers `forceResetItem` (`backlog_service_triage.go:577`, `:1385`) and the backward transition (`backlog_service_lifecycle.go:1090`). `CancelTriage` (`backlog_service_trigger_triage.go:937`) uses the same stopper for triage role only (no worktree expected) | yes, for a LIVE (unended) work or review session | INTENTIONALLY UNGATED, like rows 10-13: both callers are explicit operator state changes whose purpose is to stop the live work, the dirty-worktree refusal still protects uncommitted changes, and the PR and branch are pushed so only a clean checkout is lost. Not silent: T5.4.5 makes the force-reset result and a log line list the open or `unknown` PRs of every session it stops. Escalate to a hard gate only if the soak shows it used as a routine path (owner decision, Unresolved Questions). VERIFIED by `git grep -n "StopSessionByUUID\|stopLiveWorkAndReviewSessions" -- '*.go' ':!*_test.go'`: the only production callers of `StopSessionByUUID` are `stopLiveWorkAndReviewSessions` (call sites `backlog_service_lifecycle.go:1090`, `backlog_service_triage.go:1385`) and `CancelTriage` (`backlog_service_trigger_triage.go:937`) |

Design: one predicate `session.PRCleanupGate.Blocked(sessions []ItemSessionSummary)`, called at the top of `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept`. It reports blocked, with a reason that names each holding owner (session uuid or "item"), when ANY of these holds: (i) a link owned by any `work`-role session in `sessions` points at a state that is non-terminal (including `unknown` with `terminal=false`); (ii) a direct link of the item(s) those sessions belong to (`is.BacklogItemID`) points at such a state, after removing states carrying an item tombstone (this covers an agent-reported, Jules or manual item PR that no removed session links; adversarial C5); (iii) a session has a legacy PR number that no link row of that session carries (`LegacyNumberRepresented` false, T4.3.0; this includes the rollback case where the session HAS other links; read from `InstanceData`, already fetched in that function) AND `LegacyOnlyPRSafe(d)` is false (imported from `session/pr_legacy_safe.go`, T4.3.0), OR an item has `pr_number > 0`, no link of the item or its sessions and no tombstone covering it, with a ref the backfill could not build, could not import or deferred under the per-boot cap (`skipped_item_bad_ref` / `skipped_item_invalid` / `deferred`; read from `ItemGuardView.LegacyUnrepresented`, T4.3.0; there is no terminal flag for items, so this fails closed, with the exit in Known Risk K6), i.e. its persisted `github_pr_status_terminal` is not `true` (the SAME helper as sweeper step 2, so the two cannot disagree; architecture C5. A flag-true session the backfill could not link is therefore released instead of being retained forever); (iv) any lookup errors (fail closed). Blocked means NO worktree is removed by that call: the gate is deliberately coarse because the sessions of one item share a worktree path across rework rounds (`exceptPath`), so releasing "only the terminal session" could delete a directory another session's open PR still needs. `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` returns a `CleanupOutcome` (`Done`, `Blocked(reason)`, `Failed`); `cleanupItemWorktrees` passes it up; `CleanupTerminalItem` runs the worktree step, then runs `archiveItemWorkSessions` over the sessions MINUS the live current-round work session (RULE L, defined in T5.4.6: the same `IsLiveWork` the gate's live-work clause uses, so the pane and the worktree of a live session are held together; archival stays ungated by open PRs, OOM 2026-07-29), and returns the worktree step's outcome. `WorktreeCleaner.CleanupTerminalItem(ctx, itemID)` returns nothing today (`backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:50-54`) and `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` settles unconditionally after the call (`:208-212`), so T5.4.2 updates the interface, its implementation and every caller (`backlog_service_lifecycle.go:1063`, `session/backlog_lifecycle.go:813`, test fakes); settle only on `Done`; `Blocked` is retried on the next tick, `Failed` on the settle TTL. The block is logged and counted (`ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_total{path}` increments when an item or session first becomes blocked on that path, deduplicated through the blocked set, and the repeated 60s log line is deduplicated the same way; gauge `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items{path}` reports how many are blocked now). The PR clauses are not behind the flag (they only withhold deletion); the live-work clause and RULE L are gated by `reader:backlog` and a wired probe (R3: flag off, a nil-probe path equal to today's behavior), as is the verdict used by `ReconcilePRPending`. Wave 2 refinements (owner-approved review C7-C9): the gate is re-checked immediately before each worktree removal, not only at the top (C8); `CleanupOutcome` carries `LiveOnly`, and the sweep settles `Done`, `Failed` and PR-held `Blocked` outcomes for the TTL (the terminal listener, T5.4.3, releases a PR-held block promptly) and retries only a live-only `Blocked` every pass (C7); the rework path (row 5) passes `GateOptions{IgnoreLive: true}` because the just-spawned current-round session is live by construction, so only PR clauses may hold prior rounds' worktrees (C9). One shared probe instance reaches the lifecycle listener, `BacklogService` and the gate: `wireLiveWorkProbe` (server/dependencies_live_work_probe.go) builds and asserts it at startup and sets `ssq_live_work_probe_wired` (R3/C2); `ssq_rule_l_unsettled_items` counts items the 60 s sessions sweep left unsettled because RULE L skipped a live session (M5).

Re-run: (a) the 60s `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` re-evaluates every done/archived item each tick and a blocked item is not settled, so it cleans on the next tick after the last PR is terminal; (b) additionally, when the poller takes the last non-terminal PR of a done/archived item (or its session) terminal, it calls `WorktreeCleaner.CleanupTerminalItem` directly (T5.4.3) so cleanup is prompt rather than waiting for the tick.

PR attached to a `done` item: allowed (item-direct or session link). It does not reopen the item or change status; it blocks worktree removal and retention until terminal, then paths 3 and 4 clean it. Decision recorded in the Unresolved Questions for owner confirmation.

**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Every removal path respects the gate (one test per path, each with one open PR).
  - *Given* a `done` item with `#21 merged` and `#22 open` (linked to its work session), *When* each of: the archive RPC (1), a terminal transition via `TransitionBacklogItemStatus` (2), `cleanupTerminalItemSync` from the PR-merge-done path (3), one `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` tick (4), a reopen with a prior round that owns an open PR (5), and the tombstone sweep (6) runs with `reader:backlog` on AND off, *Then* the worktree directory still exists, `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_total{path}` incremented once for that path (a second tick leaves the counter unchanged and the gauge at 1), and for paths 2 and 3 the item's work sessions are still archived (`archiveItemWorkSessions` ran) while only the worktree removal was withheld.
- An item-owned open PR is visible to every path.
  - *Given* an open #22 attached ONLY as an item-direct link (agent `report_pr_created`, Jules or manual attach) and a work session with no link to it, *When* the archive RPC (path 1) runs, *Then* the worktree is retained and the reason names the item.
- A blocked cleanup is released promptly, and a live-only block is never settled (review C7 replaces "a blocked cleanup is not settled").
  - *Given* a `done` item blocked by open #22, *When* `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` ticks twice, *Then* the PR-held `Blocked` outcome settles for the TTL (no per-minute gate queries for a permanently blocked item) and, when #22 merges, the terminal listener (T5.4.3) runs the cleanup at once; *Given* an item blocked only by a live session, *Then* the outcome is `LiveOnly`, `settle` is not called and the next tick re-evaluates it.
- Gate fails closed.
  - *Given* a `done` item with `pr_number=5`, an empty `pr_url` and no link anywhere, *Then* the worktree is retained, the reason names the item, and the stuck report's cleanup-blocked list shows it; *Given* a `done` item whose only PR is `unknown` (non-terminal), or a session with a legacy PR number, no link and a persisted terminal flag that is not `true`, or a lookup error, *When* any path runs, *Then* the worktree is retained; *Given* the same legacy-number session with the flag `true`, *Then* gate and sweeper both treat it as safe (one shared helper).
- Cleanup re-runs after the last PR goes terminal.
  - *Given* the blocked item above, *When* #22 merges (poller marks it terminal), *Then* `CleanupTerminalItem` is invoked without waiting for the 60s tick and the worktree is removed; and *Given* the hook is unavailable, *Then* the next `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` tick removes it.
- A PR attached after done.
  - *Given* a `done` item with all PRs terminal and its worktree already cleaned, *When* a session attaches open #60, *Then* the item stays `done`, nothing is deleted while #60 is open, and when #60 merges any remaining worktree is cleaned.
- A superseded round's open PR holds that round's worktree, and detach releases it.
  - *Given* a reopened item whose round-1 session `s1` links open #30 while round 2 merged #31, *When* the item reaches `done`, *Then* the item's verdict ignores #30 (Story 5.1) but `CleanupTerminalItem` returns `Blocked` naming `s1`; *When* the repository `DeleteSessionPR(s1, #30)` (or #30 merges/closes) happens, *Then* the next attempt removes the worktree.
- A live work session holds the worktree (Phase 4 pre-mortem #4).
  - *Given* an item whose PRs are all terminal but whose current-round work session is unended and active inside `liveWorkIdleWindow`, *When* any of paths 1-6 runs, *Then* `Blocked` names the live session and the worktree stays; once the session is idle past the window or has ended, the next attempt removes it; a probe error blocks. (Archival stays ungated by open PRs, row 8, but skips the live session itself, RULE L: after the max hold the item is `done` while the session is live, and the pane is neither archived nor killed until it goes idle or ends. Test I-130/I-131.)
- A live session keeps its pane as well as its worktree (Triad iteration 2, RULE L).
  - *Given* a `done` item (reached by manual archive, the PR-merge path after the max hold, or the shipped-without-PR bounce path) whose current-round work session is live, *When* `CleanupTerminalItem` and one `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` tick run, *Then* `ArchiveSessionByUUID` and `KillTmuxPaneOnly` are NOT called for that session (recording stopper), its review-role and earlier-round sessions ARE archived as before, the worktree is `Blocked`, and the sweep did NOT settle the item (a skip sets `clean=false`); *When* the session is idle past the window or ended, *Then* the next 60 s tick (inside the 10-minute settle TTL, injected clock) archives it and kills its pane; *Given* a probe error, *Then* it is skipped (treated as live) and still unsettled.
- Terminal-listener cleanups are bounded (pre-mortem #6).
  - *Given* 300 done/archived items whose last PR just resolved to `merged` in one drain, *When* `PRTerminalListener` fires, *Then* at most `maxListenerCleanupsPerTick` (20, FIFO) call `CleanupTerminalItem` that tick and the rest are left to the 60 s `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` re-evaluation; `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items{path}` carries `reason="unverified_drain"` for items held only by not-yet-verified seeded rows, so the boot drain is distinguishable from real blocks.
- Progress comment lists all PRs.
  - *Given* an item with two PRs, *When* `backlog_github_progress_sync.go` posts, *Then* the comment links both.
**Files**: `session/pr_cleanup_gate.go`, `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, `server/services/backlog_service.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go`, `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `server/dependencies.go`, `server/services/backlog_github_progress_sync.go`

##### Task 5.4.1: `PRCleanupGate` predicate with failing tests per path (M)
- Implement `session/pr_cleanup_gate.go` (`Blocked(sessions)`, and a read-only `OpenPRsOfSession` used by T5.4.5) over T4.3.0 (including its `LegacyOnlyPRSafe`; T5.4.1 and T4.3.1 are therefore independent of each other), `ListByItem` and the item tombstones, treating unverified terminal rows as terminal only when `VerifyExhausted()`; write the per-path tests above (paths 1-6 with the flag on and off, plus the item-owned, fail-closed, superseded-round, live-work and re-run cases) FIRST (red) against the real functions with fakes for git/storage; includes the `InstanceData` skipped-host case. The tests that drive the real archive RPC, transition, 60 s tick, reopen and tombstone sweep only pass after T5.4.2 wires the gate, so they are committed with `t.Skip("red until T5.4.2")` (the re-run cases with `t.Skip("red until T5.4.3")`), the red output is recorded in the commit message, and T5.4.2 / T5.4.3 remove their skips (the T1.3.4 convention, Phase 4 C5); the live-session archive and pane-kill assertions (I-130) carry `t.Skip("red until T5.4.6")` and T5.4.6 removes that skip, so no later `go test ./session ./server/services` is red. The gate also blocks while the item's current-round work session is live (`IsLiveWork` through the `LiveWorkProbe` interface of T1.2.3, a fake here; a probe error blocks). ##### Task 5.4.2: Wire the gate at the single choke point and change the `CleanupOutcome` interface (M)
- Call the gate once at the top of `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` (before the `GetWorktreeDataBySessionUUID` removal steps; it also protects the scaffolding cleanup that follows), and return the `CleanupOutcome`. `CleanupTerminalItem` forwards the worktree step's outcome (its archival step is unchanged here: the RULE L filter is T5.4.6). Interface change in one commit: `WorktreeCleaner.CleanupTerminalItem(ctx, itemID) CleanupOutcome` (`session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:50-54`), the `BacklogService` implementation, `cleanupTerminalItemSync` (`session/backlog_lifecycle.go:811-813`, which must propagate the outcome to the archive loop), the inline caller `backlog_service_lifecycle.go:1063` (ignores the outcome except for logging) and every test fake (VERIFIED callers: implementation `backlog_service.go:1420`, interface `backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:53`, fake `backlog_lifecycle_test.go:5173`, the one test file touched). `reconcileTerminalItemWorktrees` calls `settle` for every outcome except a `LiveOnly` `Blocked`. Dedupe the blocked counter and log per T5.4.1. Run the per-path tests green (remove the T5.4.1 skips except the live-session archive and pane-kill assertions, which wait for T5.4.6); the gate's live-work clause is tested here with the fake probe of T5.4.1.

##### Task 5.4.6: RULE L, live current-round work sessions are neither archived nor pane-killed, and the real probe reaches the gate (M)
- Prerequisites: T5.2.1 (probe and `heldByLiveWork`), T5.2.4 (the skipped RULE L tests), T5.4.2 (the interface and gate). Adds `SplitLiveCurrentRound` to `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, calls it in `CleanupTerminalItem` (`backlog_service.go`, around `archiveItemWorkSessions`, so the pane and the worktree of a live session are held together) and in `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` (`session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go`, with the unsettled-while-skipped rule below), and passes the real `LiveWorkProbe` of T5.2.1 into the gate's construction (the construction site in `server/dependencies.go`, which the worker confirms at the start: it must be the SAME probe instance the lifecycle listener holds). Removes the `t.Skip("red until T5.4.6")` markers of T5.2.4 and T5.4.1 (I-117 archive assertions, I-130, I-131, I-135). Archival stays ungated by open PRs (OOM 2026-07-29, Owner confirmations pending #4).
- RULE L (Triad iteration 2, tightened in iteration 3; VERIFIED by reading `server/services/backlog_service.go:1420-1466` and `session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:115-165`): `CleanupTerminalItem` calls `archiveItemWorkSessions` unconditionally, which `ArchiveSessionByUUID`s and `KillTmuxPaneOnly`s every work/review session of a done item, and `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` repeats that every 60 s; so before this rule "the worktree is kept until the session ends" was unreachable (the pane was killed at max hold). Rule: a work-role session for which the real `LiveWorkProbe` reports `IsLiveWork` (the item's current-round work session, unended, active inside `liveWorkIdleWindow`; a probe error counts as live) is SKIPPED by both terminal-item archival call sites, and so is neither archived nor pane-killed; the sweep must actually retry it. VERIFIED by reading `session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go:146-158` and `session/terminal_sweep_settled.go:12`: `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` keeps `clean := true` unless an archive or kill call fails, and a clean item is `settle`d for `terminalSweepSettleTTL` = 10 minutes, during which `isSettled` skips it entirely. A skip that left `clean` true would therefore archive the live session up to 10 minutes late. Rule: a session skipped by RULE L sets `clean = false` (no `settle` call), exactly like a failed archive, so the item is re-read and re-evaluated on EVERY 60 s pass until its live session goes idle or ends, and it is archived and its pane killed on the first pass after that, not after the TTL. Cost: one `ListItemSessions` read and one probe per such item per pass, bounded to terminal items that still have a live current-round work session (normally zero or one; Known Risk K12). The skip is implemented once as a helper in `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, `SplitLiveCurrentRound(ctx, probe, now, sessions) (archivable []ItemSessionSummary, skippedLive bool)` (a nil probe skips nothing; the callers pass nil unless `reader:backlog` is on, R3), used by `CleanupTerminalItem` (inline: ignores `skippedLive`) and by `reconcileTerminalItemSessions` (`if skippedLive { clean = false }` before the settle test); `archiveItemWorkSessions` itself and its rework-respawn callers (`backlog_service_triage.go:1301, 3564`) are unchanged, so rework behaves as today. The skip applies on every terminal path (a manual done or archive with a live agent now keeps the agent until idle too), which is consistent with the gate's live-work clause that already held the worktree on those paths. Review-role sessions and non-current-round sessions are archived as before. Tests: I-130 (inline path), I-131 and I-135 (sweep: unsettled while skipped, archived on the next pass inside the TTL). OOM exposure (2026-07-29): bounded by the idle window for any agent that stops working; an agent that never idles is a live agent, kept until the operator ends it, with the one `notify()` above as the signal (Known Risk K12).
- Files: `session/pr_live_work_probe.go`, `server/services/backlog_service.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle_archive.go`, `server/dependencies.go`

##### Task 5.4.3: Re-run on last-PR-terminal (S)
- This task DEFINES `PRTerminalListener` (consumer-side interface in `session/`, implemented by the lifecycle listener), its setter and the nil-safe call at the end of `applyPRUpdate`, passing the state ids T3.2.2 returns (prerequisites: T5.4.2, T3.2.2; architecture C8); the listener resolves the owners of those states' links (sessions -> their items through the `session_uuid` index on `ItemSession`; item-direct owners directly), keeps done/archived items, and calls `cleanupTerminalItemSync`, which re-checks the gate. Idempotent; no revive or Start calls. The listener only enqueues the state ids and returns; a bounded worker (queue of 64 batches, a full queue drops the batch for the 60 s sweep) resolves owners and runs the cleanups, so no removal runs on the poll goroutine (review C7). Bounded drain (pre-mortem #6): at most `maxListenerCleanupsPerTick` (20) cleanups start per batch, in item-id order; the rest wait for the 60 s re-evaluation. The `reason="unverified_drain"` label of `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items` is NOT implemented. Removes the T5.4.1 re-run skips.
- Files: `session/pr_status_poller.go`, `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`

Row 14 and rows 10/11 warn: the MCP pause/stop results carry `warnings`, and `stopLiveWorkAndReviewSessions` logs one structured line and publishes a warning notification per item (the Connect responses have no text field, so the force-reset response text is replaced by the notification; adding a field is a proto change this plan does not make). #### Story 5.5: Offer to close the GitHub issue when a finished item is re-imported
**As an** operator, **I want** re-importing a GitHub issue whose backlog item is already finished to tell me so and let me close the issue with one click, **so that** finished work does not leave an open issue behind and nothing is ever closed silently.
Design (owner request 2026-11-03): `ImportGitHubIssue` already fetches the issue and finds the existing item by `external_url` (`server/services/backlog_service_sync.go:283-295`, VERIFIED) with `issue.State` in hand. A finished item with an open issue gets a structured OFFER in the response; the close is a separate, explicit RPC that reuses the forward-sync close action (`CloseIssue`, `forwardSyncCloseCommentWithPR`, `ReadIssueState` skip-if-closed in `backlog_github_forward_sync.go`). User value (Triad iteration 1): when an item reaches `done` the forward-sync subscriber already closes the issue (sourceless items since commit `36ef043bc`), so in the normal case the offer never appears; it exists for the leftovers that path never retries (no token at that moment, a failed close, an item finished before that code shipped), found at the moment the user re-imports the issue and is looking at it. Baseline: the user notices a stale open issue and closes it by hand on GitHub. Scope: github.com issues with a token for the host; a GHE issue is reported (`blocked_reason=unsupported_host`) but never closed, exactly as forward-sync skips it (`resolveSourcelessCloseAction`). Behind scope `reader:backlog` of `session_multi_pr` (flag off: the response is today's, field unset). Consumers (VERIFIED): only the web UI reads `already_existed` (`page.tsx:605`, the bulk picker's duplicate counter; the single-URL form at `:558` ignores it); the MCP `import_github_issue` never calls this RPC and has no dedup path, so it is unchanged: a human confirmation click has no MCP equivalent and an agent-callable issue close would break "never a silent close".
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Finished predicate.
  - *Given* items `done` with no PRs; `done` with #21 merged and #22 closed unmerged; `done` with #21 merged and #22 open; `done` with only #22 closed; `done` with #21 merged and an unverified-terminal #23; `pr_pending` with every PR merged; `archived`; `duplicate`, *When* `ItemFinished` is evaluated, *Then* it is true only for the first two and false for all others.
- The offer is made on re-import, never a close.
  - *Given* a finished item whose issue is still open on github.com with a keychain token and `reader:backlog` on, *When* `ImportGitHubIssue` finds the existing item (`already_existed=true`), *Then* the response carries `issue_close_offer{issue_url, issue_number, pr_urls}` and the issue is NOT closed (zero GitHub write calls).
  - *Given* the flag off, or the item not finished, or the issue already `closed`, *Then* `issue_close_offer` is unset and the response is today's. *Given* a GHE host, or no token for the host, on a finished item with an open issue, *Then* the offer is set with `blocked_reason` (`unsupported_host` / `no_token`) so the UI explains why there is no button.
- One-click close, decided server-side.
  - *Given* an offer, *When* the user confirms, *Then* `CloseFinishedItemIssue{item_id}` re-evaluates `ItemFinished` and the live issue state (a client cannot force a close of an unfinished item), closes the issue, posts the forward-sync comment naming the implementing PR and returns `CLOSED`; *Given* the issue is already closed, *Then* `ALREADY_CLOSED` and no write; *Given* the item is no longer finished, *Then* `NOT_FINISHED`; *Given* a GitHub error, *Then* `FAILED` with the message and the forward-sync watermark untouched. Scope off returns `FailedPrecondition`.
- Reuse, not a copy.
  - The close core is extracted once and called by both `handleForwardSyncCloseWith` and the new RPC; `make ready-complexity-gate` (dupl) passes and the existing forward-sync tests pass unchanged.
- Policy split: the extracted core does not narrow today's auto-close (Triad iteration 2).
  - VERIFIED: today's SOURCELESS auto-close (`resolveSourcelessCloseAction`, `backlog_github_forward_sync.go:303`) parses the URL with `ParseGitHubRefWithHosts(url, nil)`, whose host alternation is `github.com` only (`github/url_parser.go:235-246`), so a sourceless GHE issue is NOT auto-closed today (the instruction "any host with a token, GHE included" holds only for SOURCE-LINKED items, whose action comes from the source plugin config). The core `executeCloseAction` makes no host decision: it runs whatever action its resolver returns, so the refactor changes no resolver.
  - *Given* a sourceless done item whose `external_url` is `https://ghe.acme.io/acme/app/issues/5` with a token for that host, *When* `handleForwardSyncCloseWith` runs before and after the refactor, *Then* both resolve a nil action and make zero GitHub calls (the existing behavior is pinned, not widened); *Given* a sourceless `github.com` issue with a token, *Then* it still closes through the core exactly as before; *Given* a source-linked item (whose source config lists a GHE host), *Then* it still closes through the core (the `3ae9e6e78` routing test passes unchanged). The github.com-only limit of this story applies to the OFFER and the new RPC only (`resolveSourcelessCloseAction` is their resolver); neither path widens or narrows the other. Test I-133.
- Classification and registry.
  - `CloseFinishedItemIssue` is in `notTerminalWriteRPCs` (it writes a GitHub issue, never a pane; `ImportGitHubIssue` is already there, VERIFIED `terminal_write_rpcs_test.go:55`), the sinkguard `UIRpcSurface` test is green and the registry entry has hand-set `tested/testIds`.
**Files**: `proto/session/v1/backlog.proto`, `session/backlog_item_prs.go`, `server/services/backlog_service_sync.go`, `server/services/backlog_github_forward_sync.go`, `web-app/src/components/backlog/CloseIssuePrompt.tsx`, `web-app/src/app/backlog/page.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useBacklogService.ts`

##### Task 5.5.1: `ItemFinished` and the close offer on re-import (S)
- Prerequisites: T1.2.3, T1.4.1, T5.1.2, T5.2.1. Pure `ItemFinished(status, prs []PRView)` in `session/backlog_item_prs.go` (Glossary: Finished item; built on `ItemPRVerdict`, so an unverified-terminal row reads as held and blocks). In the existing-item branch of `ImportGitHubIssue` (`backlog_service_sync.go:283-295`), when scope `reader:backlog` is on: compute `ItemFinished(item, itemPRs(item))` (a `PRSetReader` error means no offer, logged), compare `issue.State == "open"` and, when both hold, fill `IssueCloseOffer` (issue URL and number, the item's merged PR URLs; `blocked_reason` is `unsupported_host` for a non-github.com host via the same `ParseGitHubRefWithHosts(url, nil)` test as `resolveSourcelessCloseAction`, or `no_token` when `GetKeychainTokenForHost(host)` is empty, or `source_managed` when `item.SourceID != ""`: since `3ae9e6e78` a source-linked item must honor its source's `ForwardSyncEnabled` opt-in, so the offer is made only for sourceless items and a source-linked item is reported, never closed from here). Metric `ssq_issue_close_offer_total{outcome=offered|blocked}` is emitted here. Proto: `message IssueCloseOffer {issue_url, issue_number, repeated pr_urls, blocked_reason}` and `IssueCloseOffer issue_close_offer = 5;` on `ImportGitHubIssueResponse` (`proto/session/v1/backlog.proto:1943`, highest used field 4, VERIFIED; re-confirm free at `make proto-gen`, run `make proto-breaking`; additive, old clients ignore it). Never writes to GitHub. Test the predicate table and the offer matrix with a fake issue fetcher; add the new test ids to `docs/registry/features/backend/ImportGitHubIssue.json`.
- Files: `proto/session/v1/backlog.proto`, `session/backlog_item_prs.go`, `server/services/backlog_service_sync.go`, `session/backlog_item_prs_test.go`, `server/services/backlog_github_rpc_test.go`, `docs/registry/features/backend/ImportGitHubIssue.json`

##### Task 5.5.2: `CloseFinishedItemIssue` RPC on the forward-sync close core (M)
- Prerequisite: T5.5.1. Account for the two newest commits (VERIFIED with `git show --stat` and by reading `server/services/backlog_github_forward_sync.go:164-235`, 2026-11-03): `36ef043bc` added the sourceless close path (`resolveSourcelessCloseAction`, `sourcelessCloseDeps`, `forwardSyncCloseCommentWithPR`, 225 changed lines in that file) and `3ae9e6e78` changed the routing in `handleForwardSyncCloseWith` to `switch { case current.SourceID == "": sourceless; case current.ExternalID != "": source-linked }`, so a source-linked item is never closed through the sourceless action. Consequences: (1) the RPC and the offer apply only to `SourceID == ""` items (enum value `SOURCE_MANAGED` otherwise); (2) typed-outcome refactor: `handleForwardSyncCloseWith` returns nothing and swallows errors today, so the tail after `action` is resolved (`ReadIssueState` skip-if-closed, `action.update`, the best-effort `PostIssueComment`, `persistForwardSyncWatermark`, the `RecordSourceSyncFailure` call only when `action.sourceID != ""`) moves into `executeCloseAction(ctx, storage, current, action) (closeResult, error)` (no host or token decision inside it; policy stays in the resolvers: today's sourceless auto-close is github.com-only, verified in the Story 5.5 policy-split AC, and the new RPC and offer are limited the same way by using the same resolver, so neither narrows the other; I-133) with `closeResult{Outcome: Closed|AlreadyClosed, IssueUpdatedAt}` and a non-nil error only when `action.update` fails; a `ReadIssueState` error still proceeds to the close, as today; `handleForwardSyncCloseWith` keeps its signature, its resolution `switch` and its swallow-and-log behavior and calls the new function, and its existing tests (including the `3ae9e6e78` routing test in `backlog_github_forward_sync_test.go`) stay green UNCHANGED, which is the refactor's proof. The RPC resolves its action with `resolveSourcelessCloseAction` only, maps nil to `NO_TOKEN`/`UNSUPPORTED_HOST` by the same tests as T5.5.1, and maps `closeResult` and the error to the enum. Metric `ssq_issue_close_offer_total{outcome=closed|failed}` is emitted here. New RPC `CloseFinishedItemIssue(CloseFinishedItemIssueRequest{item_id}) returns (CloseFinishedItemIssueResponse{outcome, message})` (enum `CLOSED|ALREADY_CLOSED|NOT_FINISHED|UNSUPPORTED_HOST|NO_TOKEN|SOURCE_MANAGED|FAILED`) on the backlog service, handler in `backlog_service_sync.go`; it loads the item, re-evaluates `ItemFinished` and the live issue state, and only then calls the shared core; scope `reader:backlog` off returns `FailedPrecondition`; one audit log line (item id, issue URL). Classify in `notTerminalWriteRPCs` and run `go test -tags sinkguard -short -run UIRpcSurface ./server/services`; registry entry `CloseFinishedItemIssue.json` with hand-set `tested/testIds`; `// +api:` marker. The comment names `current.PrURL` (the item's mirrored active PR); the progress-sync comment lists every PR (T5.4.4).
- Files: `proto/session/v1/backlog.proto`, `server/services/backlog_github_forward_sync.go`, `server/services/backlog_service_sync.go`, `server/services/terminal_write_rpcs_test.go`, `server/services/backlog_github_forward_sync_test.go`, `docs/registry/features/backend/CloseFinishedItemIssue.json`

## Phase 3: UI (Wave 3)

### Epic 6: Web UI for N PRs
**Goal**: Three-tier disclosure: one badge plus "+N" on card/row, a detail "Pull requests" list, and an action switcher where more than one PR is open. Works on mobile and desktop.

**Why follow `research/ux.md`'s three tiers unchanged**: the list answers "is anything waiting on me" with one badge; N badges would add noise at N>=3 (ux.md §5 pitfalls). I found no reason to deviate; the only additions are the Pin/Detach row actions (owner decision on pin) and a text label on the compact badge (WCAG 1.4.1).

#### Story 6.1: Shared components
**As a** developer, **I want** one chip and one list component, **so that** card, row, detail and worktree list stay consistent and under the jscpd threshold.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- State is not color-only.
  - *Given* a `merged` PR in compact mode, *When* rendered, *Then* the chip contains an icon with distinct shape and visible text "Merged" (or a `title`), and the contrast test over `CHIP_TOKEN_PAIRS` passes for the new states.
- Disclosure.
  - *Given* 5 PRs (1 open, 4 merged), *When* the list renders on desktop, *Then* the open PR and 2 most recent merged rows show and a `<button aria-expanded="false">` reads "2 earlier PRs".
- Semantics.
  - *Given* the active PR #12, *When* the list renders, *Then* its `<li>` has `aria-current="true"` and every link has a unique accessible name containing `#12`, `acme/app` and "opens in new tab".
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/shared/PullRequestChip.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/shared/PullRequestChip.css.ts`, `web-app/src/components/shared/PullRequestList.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/shared/PullRequestList.css.ts`, `web-app/src/lib/unfinished/prChipTokens.ts`, `web-app/src/lib/pr/pullRequests.ts`

(attempt N of 5)" while counting and "Couldn't verify after 5 attempts. #### Story 6.2: Surfaces
**As an** operator, **I want** every PR surface to show the active PR and reach the others, **so that** history is visible and actions are explicit.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Card shows one badge plus count.
  - *Given* a session with 3 PRs, *When* `SessionCard` renders, *Then* exactly one PR badge appears with a "+2" button, and clicking it opens the detail PR section.
- Detail list.
  - *Given* the same session, *When* `SessionDetailView` renders, *Then* a "Pull requests" section lists 3 rows with state text and the default-target hint.
- Legacy session unchanged.
  - *Given* a migrated session with one PR, *When* rendered, *Then* one row, no role chip, no disclosure.
- Switcher.
  - *Given* two open PRs, *When* the VCS widget renders on desktop, *Then* a segmented control defaults to the active PR; at the mobile width it is a native `<select>`.
- Backlog progress.
  - *Given* an item in `pr_pending` with 2 of 3 PRs merged, *When* `PullRequestSection` renders, *Then* it shows "2 of 3 PRs merged" and the row list.
- Up Next.
  - *Given* a `UserPR` whose `PRRef` matches one tracked session, *When* `PRCard` renders, *Then* a muted "session: refactor-auth" chip appears; *Given* it matches two sessions, *Then* a "2 sessions" control lists both links.
- Anchor.
  - *Given* the card "+2" button, *When* clicked, *Then* the URL gains `#pull-requests` and the section heading has focus.

## Phase 4: Artifacts (E7 in Wave 0, E8 Wave 2 except T8.2.2 in Wave 3, E9 Wave 3, E10 Wave 4)

### Epic 7: Artifacts tab reliability (A1)
**Goal**: The tab always shows a truthful state and stops losing data.

#### Story 7.1: Confirm the cause
**As a** maintainer, **I want** a read-only check on real data, **so that** the fix targets the actual failure.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Evidence recorded.
  - *Given* `~/.stapler-squad/logs/staplersquad.log` and the SQLite DB opened read-only, *When* the checks run, *Then* the PR description lists the count of sessions with empty `session_artifacts` but non-empty `history_file_path`, and the `[ArtifactExtractor]` warning counts by message.
**Files**: none (read-only)

##### Task 7.1.1: Read-only diagnosis (XS)
- Grep for `[ArtifactExtractor]` (`seek failed`, `persist failed`, `scanner error`, `fingerprint failed`); `sqlite3` read-only counts as in `research/artifacts.md` §2. This task captures the BASELINE of requirements.md (Triad iteration 1, product 1): (a) sessions with empty `session_artifacts` but a non-empty `history_file_path`, as a count and as a share of sessions with a history file (the "stuck at Extraction pending" share); (b) warning counts by message; (c) sessions whose newest PR or commit is missing from a blob because of a cap. The numbers go in the PR description and are the denominator of the success metric (the same query re-run after T7.2.1 returns 0 for (a) apart from sessions with no transcript). If (a) is near zero the empty-scan hypothesis is false and T7.2.1 is re-scoped to the dominant warning class before any E7 code is written.
- Files: none

#### Story 7.2: Fix the defects
**As an** operator, **I want** an honest tab, **so that** "pending" never means "forever".
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Empty scan is distinguishable.
  - *Given* a transcript fixture with no matches, *When* the scan completes, *Then* `inst.Artifacts != nil` with `ScanState=none_found`, and the tab renders "No artifacts found" instead of "Extraction pending".
- Late link triggers a scan.
  - *Given* a session whose history file existed before linking, *When* `SetHistoryInfo` runs, *Then* the file is enqueued and a scan completes without a restart.
- Subagent transcripts count.
  - *Given* `agent-abc.jsonl` whose parent conversation is session `refactor-auth`, *When* the scan runs, *Then* a `gh pr create` result inside it is recorded on `refactor-auth`.
- Caps keep the newest.
  - *Given* 60 distinct external URLs in order, *When* merged, *Then* the stored 50 are the last 50.
- Manual rescan.
  - *Given* a session in any state, *When* `RescanSessionArtifacts` is called, *Then* the scan position resets and a scan runs; the RPC is classified as not a terminal write.
**Files**: `session/artifacts/scan.go`, `session/artifacts/store.go`, `session/artifacts/types.go`, `session/history_linker.go`, `server/adapters/instance_adapter.go`, `server/services/session_bundle_service.go`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ArtifactsTab.tsx`, `proto/session/v1/types.proto` (tasks T7.2.1 to T7.2.5 split these; no task touches more than 4)

##### Task 7.2.1: Persist empty scans and scan state (M)
- Split in Triad iteration 2 (was 6 non-test files; now 4 here and 3 in T7.2.5). `scan.go:63-67`: persist a blob with `LastScannedAt` and `ScanState` (`not_linked`, `scanning`, `none_found`, `has_data`, `failed`); the adapter nil check (`instance_adapter.go:152`) maps the blob to the response; proto `SessionArtifacts.scan_state` and `transcript_supported` (additive; `transcript_supported` is false for programs that never get a history file, so the tab can say "this program has no transcript" instead of "waiting for transcript link"). Treat the blob as immutable after publish (shared pointer in snapshots). Tests: Go table tests for the five states, the empty-scan fixture and the adapter mapping; `make proto-gen` and `make proto-breaking`.
- Files: `session/artifacts/scan.go`, `session/artifacts/types.go`, `server/adapters/instance_adapter.go`, `proto/session/v1/types.proto`

The bundle read path (`session_bundle_service.go:412` nil check) returns the new state; the tab shows four states plus the `transcript_supported` distinction, a fifth `failed` state that keeps the last good data beneath a `role="alert"` with Retry, a "Last scanned N min ago" status line from `LastScannedAt`, and the UX-S7-4 escape: after 60 s of `scanning` (a client timer, no new field; design/ux.md) it shows "Taking longer than usual" with Rescan and "Cancel waiting" (returns to the previous data; tested with fake timers; Phase 4 N4). T7.2.4 adds the Rescan button to the same component afterwards.
- Files: `server/services/session_bundle_service.go`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ArtifactsTab.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ArtifactsTab.test.tsx`

- Files: `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `server/services/session_service_artifacts.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ArtifactsTab.tsx`

### Epic 8: Richer structured capture (A2)
**Goal**: Typed, timestamped, privacy-safe artifact records, grouped by PR in the tab.

#### Story 8.1: Blob v2 and extractor
**As an** operator, **I want** PRs, branches, commits, issues, plan docs, CI links and commands paired to their results, **so that** the tab answers "what did this session do".
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Pairing.
  - *Given* a transcript with a Bash `tool_use` (`id=t1`, `gh pr create --base feat-a --head feat-b`) and a `tool_result` for `t1` containing `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/12`, *When* scanned, *Then* one PR record `{acme/app#12, source=created, branch=feat-b, base=feat-a, firstSeenAt=<line timestamp>}` exists.
- Authored vs mentioned.
  - *Given* a `gh pr view 99` result containing `.../pull/99` with no `gh pr create`, *When* scanned, *Then* the record is `mentioned`.
- Host-aware.
  - *Given* `https://ghe.acme.io/acme/app/pull/5`, *When* scanned, *Then* a PR record with host `ghe.acme.io` exists.
- Commits.
  - *Given* output `[feat-b 1a2b3c4] fix: token refresh`, *When* scanned, *Then* a commit record `{sha:1a2b3c4, subject:"fix: token refresh", branch:"feat-b"}` exists.
- Other kinds.
  - *Given* a Write `tool_use` with `file_path=project_plans/x/plan.md`, a `/actions/runs/123` URL and `Fixes #45`, *When* scanned, *Then* a plan_doc, ci_link and issue record exist.
- Redaction.
  - *Given* a URL `https://x.io/cb?token=abcd1234` and a command containing `--token abcd1234`, *When* stored, *Then* the token value is replaced by `[redacted]` in the blob and the bundle export.
- v1 readable.
  - *Given* a stored v1 blob with `pr_urls`, *When* loaded, *Then* it renders as before and `pr_urls` stays populated in proto.
- An older binary still reads a v2 blob (revert-PR rollback safety).
  - *Given* a v2 blob written by this version, *When* the v1 decoder (kept as a test fixture of the pre-change struct) loads it, *Then* it succeeds, unknown fields are ignored and `pr_urls`, `branches`, and the other v1 lists are still populated; the v2 writer always keeps writing the v1 lists.
- Illegal combinations are unrepresentable.
  - *Given* the Go types, *When* the package compiles, *Then* `CommitRecord` has no `Host` and `PRRecord` has no `SHA`; decoding `{"kind":"commit","host":"x"}` drops the stray field at the single parse point and `{"kind":"unknown"}` is skipped with a counted warning.
- Rescan has a kill switch.
  - *Given* flag `artifact_scan_v2` off, *When* the process starts with a stored scan version below the new one, *Then* no history file is re-enqueued by the version bump; *Given* it is flipped on, *Then* the paced backfill queue starts; flipped off mid-run, *Then* no further files are enqueued.
- Blob size is bounded (pre-mortem #9).
  - *Given* the largest real transcript on this machine (`find ~/.claude/projects -name '*.jsonl' -size +50M`; a fixture of the same shape if unavailable), *When* scanned, *Then* the stored blob is at most 128 KB, kinds are capped individually with the oldest dropped first, command result text is at most 512 B, and `ssq_artifact_blob_bytes` observes it; `artifact_scan_v2` is flipped only after a dry run on a database copy reports the p99 blob size (runbook).
**Files**: `session/artifacts/types.go`, `session/artifacts/extractor.go`, `session/artifacts/scan.go`, `session/artifacts/jsonl.go`, `session/artifacts/redact.go`, `session/artifacts/*_test.go`

##### Task 8.1.1: Blob v2 types and migration read (M)
- Versioned blob; `ArtifactRecord` is a sealed interface with one variant per kind (`PRRecord`, `BranchRecord`, `CommitRecord`, `IssueRecord`, `PlanDocRecord`, `CILinkRecord`, `CommandRecord`), stored as tagged JSON and decoded at ONE parse function shared by v1 and v2 readers; v1 lists are kept and still written so an older binary can read the blob; scan-version bump resets positions through the paced backfill queue (`artifactBackfillDelay`) only when `artifact_scan_v2` is on (T8.1.6); the blob is capped at 128 KB (per-kind caps, oldest dropped, result text 512 B; AC above).
- Files: `session/artifacts/types.go`, `session/artifacts/records.go`, `session/artifacts/store.go`

- Use `tool_use_id` (present in `jsonl.go`, unused today); capture the line timestamp; authored vs mentioned.
- Files: `session/artifacts/jsonl.go`, `session/artifacts/extractor.go`, `session/artifacts/scan.go`

##### Task 8.1.6: `artifact_scan_v2` kill switch (S)

### Epic 9: Cross-session artifact search (A3)
**Goal**: Find sessions by PR, commit, branch, issue or free text.

#### Story 9.1: Index
**As an** operator, **I want** one index over artifacts, **so that** a query returns sessions.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Tokenizer.
  - *Given* the query `acme/app#42`, *When* tokenized for artifact documents, *Then* the PR document for `acme/app#42` is retrieved; likewise a 7-char SHA prefix `1a2b3c4` and branch `feat-b`.
- Exact PR lookup unions both stores.
  - *Given* a `SessionPR` row for session `u1` and an artifact `authored` record for `u2` on the same PR, *When* `ArtifactIndex.SessionsForPR(acme/app#12)` (T9.1.2) runs, *Then* both sessions return, `u1` with `source=session_pr` and `u2` with `source=artifact` (the `FindSessionsByPullRequest` RPC over it is tested in T9.2.1).
- Incremental.
  - *Given* a new scan result, *When* `OnScanComplete` fires, *Then* the index contains it without a restart.
- History search is untouched.
  - *Given* a fixed history corpus and a recorded `SearchClaudeHistory` result for a query, *When* 100 artifact documents containing the same terms are indexed, *Then* the history result (hits, order, scores) is identical, because the artifact index is a separate `Engine`/`DocumentStore` with its own corpus statistics.
**Files**: `session/search/tokenizer.go`, `session/search/engine.go`, `session/artifact_index.go`, `session/artifact_index_test.go`

##### Task 9.1.1: Artifact documents in a dedicated BM25 engine (M)
- Start with a failing tokenizer test for `owner/repo#42`, SHAs, branch names (the INFERRED row). Do NOT add artifact documents to the history-search `Document`/`DocumentStore` (shaped around `SessionID`/`MessageIndex`/`MessageRole`, shared `SessionIndex`; mixing would leak artifact hits into `SearchClaudeHistory` and skew avgdl/idf). Instead instantiate a second `search.Engine` + store for artifacts, reusing the tokenizer and BM25 code; build from stored blobs at start; update from `OnScanComplete`. Keep the index memory-only like the existing engine (ADR-004). Add the "history search unchanged" regression test. Build the index in the background after the listener is up and export `ssq_artifact_index_docs` (pre-mortem #14).
- Files: `session/search/tokenizer.go`, `session/search/engine.go`, `session/artifact_index.go`, `session/artifact_index_test.go`

##### Task 9.1.2: Exact lookup maps (M)
- `map[PRRef][]sessionUUID`, SHA-prefix map; unions `SessionPR` rows (repository query) and artifact PR records; never seeds `SessionPR` from artifacts.
- Files: `session/artifact_index.go`, `session/artifact_index_test.go`

#### Story 9.2: RPCs and omnibar
**As an** operator, **I want** search results to be sessions, **so that** I can jump to the right one.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Search RPC.
  - *Given* sessions `u1` (artifact commit `1a2b3c4`) and `u2` (none), *When* `SearchSessionArtifacts{query:"1a2b3c4"}` is called, *Then* the response lists `u1` with `matches[0].type=commit`, and respects the hidden/archived visibility semantics of `SearchClaudeHistoryRequest`.
- Omnibar.
  - *Given* the user pastes `https://github.com/acme/app/pull/12`, *When* the detector runs, *Then* an action "Find sessions for this PR" appears and emits the navigation target `/artifacts?pr=acme/app%2312` (asserted in `dispatch.test.ts`, T9.2.2); the page that serves it and its `pr` filter land in wave 4 (T10.1.1/T10.1.2), whose tests assert that the URL dispatches `FindSessionsByPullRequest`.
- Unscanned sessions are counted, not hidden.
  - *Given* 10 visible sessions of which 3 have `scan_state=not_linked` or `scanning`, *When* `SearchSessionArtifacts` or `FindSessionsByPullRequest` returns, *Then* the response carries `unscanned_session_count=3` so the UI can say "3 sessions not scanned yet; results may be incomplete".
- Orphans are filtered.
  - *Given* a link whose session was deleted, *When* `FindSessionsByPullRequest` runs, *Then* no stale `source=session_pr` hit is returned.
**Files**: `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `server/services/session_service_artifacts.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `server/services/terminal_write_rpcs_test.go`, `web-app/src/components/omnibar/detector.ts`, `web-app/src/components/omnibar/dispatch.ts`, `web-app/src/components/omnibar/types.ts`

##### Task 9.2.1: RPCs (M)
- `SearchSessionArtifacts`, `FindSessionsByPullRequest` on the existing service, handlers in the NEW file `session_service_artifacts.go` (delegates file routes only); both responses include `unscanned_session_count` and take the optional `pr` filter shared with T4.2.6; read RPCs go in `notTerminalWriteRPCs`; registry; metric `ssq_artifact_search_seconds` and the usage counter `ssq_artifact_search_queries_total{kind,result}` (kind from the query shape: PR ref, SHA prefix, branch, text; result hit or miss). Success-metric tests (requirements.md, one Go test per string kind): seeded sessions whose blobs hold a known PR URL, a branch name, a commit SHA prefix and a plan-doc path each return the owning session for that string, and a miss returns none and counts `result=miss`; a 2-character query is accepted and a 1-character query is rejected `InvalidArgument` (the UX rule in S8/S9). Ordering of `FindSessionsByPullRequest` results (Triad iteration 2, design/ux.md S9): `source=session_pr` (Tracked) rows first, then `source=artifact` (Seen in transcript), within each group by session `last_activity` descending with the session title ascending as the tie-break, one row per session (a session matched by both sources is returned once, as Tracked); test U-151. Canonical identity (moved from T1.3.6; `FindSessionsByPullRequest` first exists here): it given `Acme/App#12` or `acme/app#12` returns the same sessions.
- Files: `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `server/services/session_service_artifacts.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `server/services/terminal_write_rpcs_test.go`

## Phase 5: Rollout and docs (Wave 5)

### Epic 11: Rollout, docs, registry, final gates

#### Story 11.1: Ship gates and docs
**As an** owner, **I want** every CI gate green and a runbook for the staged flip, **so that** shipping is routine.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Gates.
  - *Given* the finished branch merged with current `main`, *When* `make ready` runs, *Then* it passes, including `proto-breaking`, `ready-complexity-gate` (dupl, gocognit, funlen, gocyclo on modified functions), `ready-duplication-gate-web` (jscpd under 0.14%), `TestConfigSurfaceProof`, the sinkguard `UIRpcSurface` test, `actor-field-guard`, `pr-field-guard` (T2.2.4), `pr-state-write-guard` (T2.3.4), the allow-list tests `legacy_pr_readers_test.go` and `legacy_item_pr_readers_test.go`, `lint-custom` (noarchivedrevival, norawghrequest, ent analyzers), `route-meta.test.ts` and the mobile-spec registration test (T6.3.3).
- Runbook.
  - *Given* the runbook, *When* followed, *Then* each stage lists the flag scope, the metric to watch (`ssq_pr_poll_total`, drift counter = 0) and the rollback.
**Files**: `docs/how-to/roll-out-session-multi-pr.md`, `docs/reference/session-pull-requests.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (reference index row), `docs/registry/features/`

##### Task 11.1.1: Docs (S)
- How-to with the staged flip (the runbook states the cold-poll drain time: `ceil(N / maxColdPollsPerTick)` ticks times the poller tick interval, e.g. 300 unknown states at 20 per tick drain in 15 ticks; that the state refresher runs from the first deploy with every scope off; and the supported rollback cycle), reference for the model (canonical lowercase identity, active PR rule incl. `unknown` ranking, done rule over the derived item PR set (current round), mirror contract, proto fields), index row in `CLAUDE.md`. Runbook notes (iteration 4): an abandoned open PR on a superseded round retains the shared reused worktree path indefinitely and is visible only through `ssq_cleanup_gate_blocked_items` and the stuck report; reopen path 5 keeps stale prior worktrees while any PR is open (PR-fix loops), terminal cleanup removes them later; `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows` and the skipped-backfill counts are read before each stage flip; the exit for a fork session or an `unknown` PR is the matching detach; the post-boot window in which unverified terminal rows are treated as non-terminal until re-verified. Runbook notes (iteration 5): (1) a PR that is closed and then REOPENED on GitHub is noticed only if the `pull_request.reopened` webhook (`docs/how-to/expose-github-webhook-endpoint.md`) is enabled; otherwise its row stays terminal and cleanup can run under the reopened PR (owner-accepted risk K1); closed-then-merged IS detected for pr_pending items; (2) every restart (each `make install-service`) re-zeroes verification counters, so with frequent restarts an unfetchable historical row may never reach 5 counted failures over 30 minutes and its worktree is retained (safe direction, not a gate bug; read `ssq_pr_unverified_terminal_rows`); (3) private-repo, SSO, expired-token and secondary-rate-limit failures are deliberately not counted toward exhaustion (`ssq_pr_poll_failures_total{reason}`), so such rows are held until detached; (4) skipped backfill rows (`skipped_invalid`, `skipped_item_bad_ref`) are logged each boot as a count plus 10 titles and keep their worktrees until a valid `pr_url` is set or the PR attached by URL; (5) the orphan GC only removes owner-less links older than 24 hours. Runbook notes (Phase 4): step 0 of the first deploy is the 10-minute `ssq_pr_poll_total` watch with every reader scope off and the `refresher_off` kill switch (Known Risk K10 budget: <= 1,500 PR requests per hour); the expected post-boot numbers from T1.5.4 (about 560 rows, about 28 cold-drain ticks, a blocked-items gauge near 300 labelled `reason="unverified_drain"` that falls); `ssq_pr_maintenance_failed_total` and the 5-minute retry; the live-work hold (`merged, session still active`, `liveWorkIdleWindow`); the issue-close prompt and its `reader:backlog` scope; a dry run of `artifact_scan_v2` on a database copy before flipping it (blob size p99).
- Runbook additions (Triad iteration 2): step -1 is the manual first-deploy rehearsal of Known Risk K13 (the T1.5.4 harness does not boot the service); enable the reader scopes in order (the order is applied at read time, so an out-of-order enable stores a value that resolves off); the kill switch is the scope `refresher_off`; after the max hold a live session keeps its pane and worktree (K12), so the operator ends it with **End session**; the adoption keep/cut thresholds (Owner confirmations pending #12) are read at each soak review against the baseline recorded in requirements.md.
- Files: `docs/how-to/roll-out-session-multi-pr.md`, `docs/reference/session-pull-requests.md`, `CLAUDE.md`

##### Task 11.1.2: Registry and feature markers (S)
- `make registry-generate`, hand-set `tested`/`testIds` for every new RPC and component in one pass; `// +feature:` markers on new components.
- Files: `docs/registry/features/**`

##### Task 11.1.3: Final gate sweep (S)
- `git fetch origin main`, merge main, rerun `make ready` and `cd web-app && bun run lint:eslint`; check PR state before pushing follow-ups; do not `git add -A` or force-add generated ent/proto output. CI budget note (Triad iteration 1, engineering 4; open BUG-067, `docs/bugs/open/BUG-067-server-services-race-suite-exceeds-150s-ci-timeout.md`: `mcp-integration.yml` runs `go test -tags=integration -timeout=150s -race ./server/mcp/... ./server/services/...` and the package is already borderline): this plan adds many `server/services` tests (T4.2.x, T5.4.x, T5.5.x, T9.2.1, T10.1.1). Rules: put pure logic tests in `session/` and keep `server/services` tests to handler-level cases; every new test is hermetic and `t.Parallel()`, with no tmux and no `time.Sleep`; this task measures `go test -tags=integration -race -count=1 ./server/services/...` wall time on the branch and on `origin/main` and records both; if the branch exceeds 120 s (80% of the 150 s ceiling) the BUG-067 fix (raising the timeout or splitting the package) lands first as its own PR, per `fix-flaky-tests-dont-defer`. No new workflow or job is added (`docs/reference/ci-job-budget.md`, "prefer a step over a job"); the plan-table check (T11.1.4) is a `make` target run inside an existing job's step at most.
- Files: none

##### Task 11.1.4: Dependency-table check and per-task briefs (XS, wave 0, first before any worker is spawned)
- Triad iteration 1, engineering 9: the table check and the brief generation are tasks, not prose. Add `scripts/plan-check/session_multi_pr_table.py` and a `make plan-check-session-multi-pr` target (a step in the existing `go-guards` job at most, no new job). The script parses the "Task dependency graph (Phase 4)" table and the `##### Task` headings of this plan and fails on: a task missing from the table or the table naming a task without a heading, an unknown prerequisite, a prerequisite in a later wave, a cycle, and a wave-0 task depending on a later wave; `--brief T4.2.3` prints the per-task brief (the task text, its prerequisites with their wave, the owning story's ACs and the files) that the orchestrator hands each worker (pre-mortem #11). AC: *Given* the committed plan, *When* the target runs, *Then* it exits 0 and prints `tasks N headings N violations 0`; *Given* a copy with a prerequisite pointed at a later wave, *Then* it exits non-zero naming the task. The orchestrator also runs it before each wave.
- Files: `scripts/plan-check/session_multi_pr_table.py`, `Makefile`

## Acceptance traceability (requirements success metrics)

| Metric | Covered by |
|---|---|
| N PRs visible with own state in detail view and list data | Story 4.1, Story 6.2 |
| Actions target an explicit PR; default is active, never stale; test A merged B open | Task 1.3.4, Story 3.2, Story 4.2 |
| Backlog item with 2+ PRs reaches done by the rule; not stuck on an old PR | Story 5.2, Story 5.3 |
| Single-PR sessions load unchanged | Story 1.5, Story 2.3, flag-off tests in Stories 1.1, 2.1, 6.3 |
| Artifacts: reliable, richer, searchable, browsable | Epics 7 to 10 |
| Re-importing a finished item offers a one-click GitHub issue close, never a silent close (owner request 2026-11-03) | Story 5.5 |
| Create a PR after an earlier one merged (requirements In Scope, Phase 4 C10) | Task 4.2.4, Story 4.2 |
| Mobile assertions run under real mobile projects, not a resized desktop window (owner request 2026-11-03) | Task 6.3.3 and the five `*-mobile.spec.ts` specs |

## Effort estimate

Basis: bands and weights INFERRED (ESTIMATION.md); recalibrate after the first implementer run. Raw tokens use XS=20k, S=55k, M=130k per worker run. Assumed mix for implementation runs: output 12% of tokens, and 70% of input read from cache, so 1 raw token is about 0.93 CU (0.88 x (0.7x0.1 + 0.3x1) + 0.12 x 5); I round to 0.9. Verification multiplier x1.75 per story (spec review, code quality review, `6-verify` layers, CI reruns).

Iteration 3 (simplification) deleted T5.1.3 (mirror links, M) and T5.1.4 (supersede-path enumeration, S), replaced them with one S round-membership test task, added T2.3.4 (single-writer guard, S), and re-sized T3.2.5 (M to S, no legacy-path writes), T1.5.2 (S to XS, projection only) and T1.3.6 (S to XS, no guard test): net 101 tasks, -0.35M CU. Only re-sized or deleted tasks changed; tasks that merely got simpler in text (T1.3.2, T5.4.1, T5.4.2) keep their size band until the first implementer run recalibrates. Size band: **Large (3-15M)**, about 0.7M below the upper edge (informational; full scope is planned, nothing is cut).

**Critical path (agent waves)**: W0 [E1 (S1.1 || S1.2 -> S1.3 -> S1.4 || T1.5.1) || E7] -> W1 [E2 -> T1.5.2 -> T1.5.3 -> T1.5.4] -> W2 [T1.3.4 first, then E3 (T3.4.1 first) || E4 (T4.1.1 first; T4.3.0 before T4.3.1), then E5 (S5.2 and S5.4 after T3.2.1 and T4.3.0, with T5.2.1 -> T5.2.4 and T5.4.2 -> T5.4.6 split in iteration 3; Story 5.5 last) || E8 (T8.1.6 before T8.1.1 ships)] -> W3 [T6.3.3 first; E6 || E9; then T8.2.2 (after T6.1.2) and T5.5.3 -> T5.5.4] -> W4 [E10] -> W5 [E11]. Six waves. CONCURRENCY IS CAPPED AT 2 WORKERS in every wave (Phase 4 pre-mortem #2; the earlier "widest fan-out is four concurrent epics in W2" is withdrawn): W2 runs as two steps of two slots, written out in the Worker schedule under the Dependency Visualization, together with the free-memory and load check before each spawn, the disk-scratch rule (never `/tmp`, a RAM tmpfs on this host) and the per-worker `MemoryMax`. Each parallel worker needs its own worktree created from the feature branch (memory `feedback_parallel_workers_one_worktree_sweep_each_other`), merged in the order proto, ent schema, session, server, web. Shared-file serialization points are listed in the dependency diagram. The memory cap of 2 concurrent backlog sessions applies if run as backlog items; otherwise use Agent worktree isolation, still at most 2.

**Overrun checkpoint**: if cumulative spend on completed stories exceeds the table value by more than 25% (about 18.7M CU for the whole project, 125% of the 14,926k table total, or 25% over the running sum of completed stories), pause and report the delta with its cause. Checked per epic as well as project-wide: an epic whose completed stories exceed that epic's table sum by 25% triggers the pause on its own (Epics 6 and 4 are the likeliest to run high: T6.2.x and T2.2.2 each span many files). Informational, not a cut line.

**Wall-clock blockers (owners)**:
- Soak windows between flag stages `reader:poller`, `reader:backlog`, `reader:actions`, `reader:ui`: Tyler.
- Owner confirmation of three repair-pass decisions (session archival ungated, PR attach to a done item allowed, `unknown` after 5 failures over 30 minutes) and of the iteration 3 behavior changes (a superseded round's open PR still holds its worktree; a session-link-only PR of a superseded round does not count toward the done verdict while item-direct PRs always do) and of the iteration 4 stance on force-reset / backward-to-ready (ungated with a warning): Tyler.
- Flipping `artifact_scan_v2` (paced rescan of every session's history): Tyler.
- Acknowledging Known Risks K1-K9 (no polling is added for K1): Tyler.
- Mobile app compatibility check of the additive `Session` fields: Tyler.
- Phase 4 confirmations: `liveWorkIdleWindow` (10 minutes) and holding the item in `pr_pending` while the agent works (T5.2.4); the `reader:backlog` scope for the issue-close prompt (T5.5.1); the production-database copy and the +5 ms `ListSessions` budget for the T1.5.4 rehearsal; the ADR-003 items marked proposed (cleanup-gate scope, ungated explicit removals): Tyler.
- Real-device mobile pass for the PR list, Artifacts tab and `/artifacts` page: Tyler.
- CI queue time and `origin/main` movement (re-merge main before the final push): CI.
- Review latency on the PR: Tyler.

## Review repair log

Sources: `architecture-review.md` (B1-B3 and concerns), `adversarial-review.md` (B1-B3, C1-C11, minors), `design/ux.md` gaps. Code claims were re-checked in this pass (see the "Repair pass" rows in the spot-verification table). Nothing was built or run beyond read-only greps.

### Blockers

| # | Item | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Arch B1 | `actor-field-guard` extension fails on unrelated `session_driver.go` lines | Rejected extending the file list. New field-scoped target `pr-field-guard` (receivers `inst\|instance\|liveInst`, five PR fields, all non-test Go outside `session/instance*.go`), which matches only `session_driver.go:961-962` today; AC proves red-then-green | T2.2.4, Story 2.2 AC, Tech Debt Disposition |
| Arch B2 + Adv B1 | Sweeper reads `InstanceData`, not snapshots; empty set read as all-terminal; full saves cannot clear terminal | Bulk query `ListSessionPRViewsByUUIDs` as its own task before the sweeper change; sweeper fails closed and is not flag-gated; "legacy number but no link" is never terminal (also in `PRsAllTerminal`, the gate and the fallback); (b) full-save path made clearable for linked sessions | T4.3.0, T4.3.1, T1.2.3, T2.3.3, Story 4.3 AC |
| Arch B3 + Adv B3 | One entity with `""` sentinel gives divergent copies; item set unions every session ever linked; backfilled item rows have no state and are never polled | Chose the split: `PullRequestState` (one row per `PRRef`, polled once, no fan-out) plus `SessionPR` link rows. Membership rule defined (item links UNION links of current non-superseded `ItemSession`s, deduped by state id, `abandoned` links excluded from "none open"; merged abandoned PRs still count). Backfill seeds state from stored data (items from status, else `unknown`), poller polls states so item-only rows are refreshed, cold polling bounded. ADR-001 rewritten | Glossary, Alternatives (A'), T1.3.1-T1.3.5, T1.5.1, T3.2.1, T5.1.2, T5.1.3, ADR-001 |
| Adv B2 | Cleanup gated at one of 7+ paths; no re-run; attach-to-done undefined | Path inventory table (8 paths with true definition sites); one shared `PRCleanupGate` called at `cleanupItemWorktreesExcept` and `CleanupTerminalItem`; safety-net sweep does not settle a blocked item; poller triggers cleanup on last-PR-terminal; attaching to a `done` item is allowed, does not reopen, holds cleanup. Session archival (no data loss) deliberately ungated, flagged for owner | Story 5.4, T5.4.1-T5.4.4, Unresolved Questions |

### Concerns folded in

| Concern | Decision (one-line rationale) |
|---|---|
| Legacy-mirror gating across four reader scopes; flag flip does not republish | Adopted: mirror is always the `ActivePR` projection once any link exists; the flag gates readers only; scopes must enable in order (enforced in flag service). Removes the republish problem and keeps `buildSnapshot` pure (T2.1.1, T1.4.1, Migration Plan) |
| New PR setters in a new file | Adopted: `session/instance_pr_set.go`; old file keeps thin wrappers (T2.1.2) |
| Row upsert + mirror in one transaction | Adopted: DB authoritative on crash; persist from snapshot at persist time (T2.3.1) |
| Poller keyed by Title; empty uuid on legacy rows | Adopted: poller state keyed by uuid, Title fallback; backfill assigns uuid or counts-and-logs, and the "legacy number, no link" rule keeps such sessions protected (T3.2.1, T1.5.1) |
| No artifact docs in history `Document`/`DocumentStore` | Adopted: second `Engine`/store, regression test that history search is unchanged (T9.1.1, ADR-004) |
| `ArtifactRecord` illegal combinations | Adopted: sealed-interface sum type, one parse point (T8.1.1) |
| New RPCs landing in `session_service_delegates.go` | Adopted: `session_service_pr_refs.go` and `session_service_artifacts.go`; delegates file routes only |
| T1.1.2 needs Story 1.2 types | Adopted: headline tests moved to T1.3.4; Story 1.1 is characterization only; ordering stated in Epic 1 and the diagram |
| MCP caller-identity check | Adopted: T5.3.0, test first |
| Blob v2 older-binary read + kill switch | Adopted: AC in Story 8.1; flag `artifact_scan_v2` (T8.1.6, registered in T1.4.1) |
| Row state maintained while flag off | Adopted: T3.2.5 (legacy poller also writes the state row), so the flip has no cold burst; residual cold polling capped by `maxColdPollsPerTick` |
| Backfill must not resurrect detached PRs or rerun destructively | Adopted: tombstone table, run-once marker, zero-links-only, at-most-one primary (Migration Plan, T1.5.1); the run-once marker was replaced by a per-boot zero-link top-up in iteration 2 |
| Terminal scan repo match | Adopted: host/owner/repo must equal the session's and push-output only (T2.2.1). The adversarial suggestion of an extra `unverified` tier for the done rule is REJECTED: the repo match plus push-line restriction removes the unrelated-PR case it targeted, and a second trust tier would complicate the verdict |
| Ratchet for backlog `PrNumber` readers | Adopted: T2.2.5 plus stuck report moved to the seam (T5.2.2) |
| Orphan-row GC | Adopted: T1.3.5 and search-side filtering |
| `unknown` state for persistent poll failure | Adopted: N=5 over >= 30 min, rate limits excluded, owner can detach or waive (T3.2.4, T4.2.3); N flagged for owner |
| Persist-after-actor and reconciler racing the poller | Adopted: reconciler fixes drift through the actor, persists from the snapshot, `-race` test (T1.5.2, T2.1.2) |
| Adv C2 reader scopes not independent | Adopted (ordering enforcement in the flag service) |
| Adv C10 per-epic overrun check | Adopted in the overrun checkpoint |
| Adv C11 artifacts outside the flag | Adopted via `artifact_scan_v2`; Epics 7, 9, 10 other than the rescan stay outside `session_multi_pr` (additive/bug fixes), now recorded in Risk Control |
| Adv minors: scope additions (Up Next chip, omnibar action, redaction, `terminalDiscoveryBackoff`) | Kept, marked additive/optional here: they are not in requirements.md and can be dropped without affecting the success metrics |
| Arch nitpicks | `PullRequestState` naming rule honored; T3.4.1 ordered first in E3; Story 7.1 marked as a soft dependency in the effort table. "Strategy-free refactor" label not changed in the Pattern Decisions table (cosmetic) |
